# scatrs Phase 1a (Homogeneous-Sphere Mie) Implementation Plan

> **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use usask-foundation:subagent-driven-development to implement this plan task-by-task. Steps use checkbox (`- [ ]`) syntax for tracking.

**Goal:** Lorenz–Mie scattering by homogeneous spheres in scatrs: coefficients, efficiencies, asymmetry parameter and amplitudes with closed-form derivatives, validated against certified Arb reference values, with a declared accuracy over a validated domain that CI enforces, and a vectorized Python API.

**Architecture:**
- **Rust kernel** (`scatrs::mie`), generic over `Real` (`f64`, `DoubleDouble`, `Dual`). Dₙ(mx) by a contracting downward ratio recurrence, ψₙ/ξₙ by a hybrid upward/ratio scheme, cancellation-free numerators, absorption from an exact per-term identity, derivatives in closed form.
- **Reference generator** (`reference/`, python-flint). Rigorous Mie values: the same recurrences seeded by a proven enclosure, disk arithmetic for complex recurrences, and a proven series-tail bound for the "certified" label.
- **Accuracy contract** (`scatrs::mie::ValidatedDomain`). Error ≤ C·(κ′ + cond)·2⁻⁵², enforced by a reference test against Arb and by a dense f64-against-DoubleDouble accuracy map, both run in CI on three operating systems.
- **Python** (`scatrs.mie`). Broadcast inputs passed zero-copy as strided views; GIL released; frozen result classes with `.d(param)`.

**Tech Stack:**
- Rust 1.85+ (edition 2024), num-complex 0.4.6, num-traits 0.2.19, thiserror 2; proptest and criterion 0.8 (dev).
- pyo3 0.29, numpy (crate) 0.29.
- Python ≥ 3.11, numpy ≥ 1.26, python-flint 0.9.0, pytest; matplotlib for the docs.

**Spec:** `docs/specs/2026-10-13-phase-1-mie-design.md`, including section 12 (decisions made while planning), and the main spec `docs/specs/2026-10-12-scatrs-design.md` (§5.1, §6, §8, §9.1, §13).

**How this plan was made.** Every task's code was written, then replayed in order in a clean worktree, with every check below green after each task. The numbers quoted in expected outputs (worst constants, timings, record counts) are measured. Code blocks are complete: copy them as they are. Patches apply with `git apply` from the repository root on top of the previous task.

**Deferred to phase 1b** (spec section 9): Bogaert O(n) Gauss–Legendre and the GL API rework; batched Wigner functions; phase matrix and Greek coefficients; rayon batches with a caller-supplied pool; the instruction-count CI gate and the cross-code comparison; cargo-fuzz; musllinux wheel tests; cross-platform golden digests.

## Global Constraints

These apply to every task.

**Rust**
- Edition 2024, `rust-version = "1.85"` (MSRV, checked for the libraries).
- The core crate depends only on `num-complex`, `num-traits` and `thiserror` (rayon arrives in phase 1b). Dev-dependencies are unrestricted.
- The core crate keeps `#![forbid(unsafe_code)]` and `#![warn(missing_docs)]`.
- No `ndarray` or `pyo3` types in the core crate's public API.
- No input to a public library function may cause a panic or a silent NaN: invalid inputs return `Error::InvalidInput`, inputs outside the validated domain return `Error::OutsideValidatedDomain` unless `allow_unvalidated` is set.
- Results are bitwise deterministic; `Dual<f64, N>` values equal the `f64` values bit for bit.
- `cargo fmt --all -- --check`, `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings` and `RUSTDOCFLAGS="-D warnings" cargo doc -p scatrs --no-deps --document-private-items` pass at every commit.

**Conventions** (spec §3): Bohren & Huffman (1983), time dependence e^(−iωt); m = n + ik with Im m ≥ 0 absorbing; negative Im m (and negative Re m) rejected with `InvalidInput`, never flipped; x = 2πr/λ; the coefficients and amplitudes are holomorphic in m, so ∂/∂(Re m) = d/dm and ∂/∂(Im m) = i·d/dm.

**Validated domain:** x ∈ [10⁻⁶, 2·10⁴], 0 < Re m ≤ 10, 0 ≤ Im m ≤ 10, with at least the default number of terms. Accepted inputs: x ∈ [10⁻³⁰, 10⁶], |m| ∈ [10⁻⁶, 10³], `n_max` ≤ `MAX_MIE_ORDER` = 2²⁰.

**Python**
- Python ≥ 3.11, abi3 wheels, numpy ≥ 1.26.
- Python converts types and shapes (`scatrs._args`); Rust validates values. Array parameters are `Bound<PyArray…>` borrowed with `try_readonly()`, never `PyReadonlyArray` parameters; alignment is checked before `as_array()`. The GIL is released for all computation. Fresh views, never the caller's array objects, are passed to Rust.

**Reference data**
- Format version 2 (`hi`/`lo`/`lo2`/`dec`/`err`); `label` is `certified` only when every error, series tails included, is rigorously bounded.
- Generators draw no input through the platform's maths library (`reference/tests/test_portable_inputs.py`); regeneration is byte-identical on every platform.
- Committed data ≤ 20 MB in total.

**Process**
- TDD: write the tests of each task first and see them fail.
- Commit at the end of each task, with the message given, ending with the trailer `Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5.5 (1M context) <noreply@anthropic.com>`.
- Never loosen a test tolerance without a written justification in the test.
- No personal data (names, emails) in any network request.

---
### Task 2: Rigorous Mie coefficients in Arb (disk arithmetic)

The reference generator's core: aₙ, bₙ and their closed-form derivatives in ball arithmetic, at the exact inputs, with every error rigorously bounded. Arb's Bessel functions are unusable at large orders (5 of 320 bits at order 2000), so only recurrences are used: the logarithmic-derivative ratios uₖ = ψₖ/ψₖ₋₁ seeded by the enclosure |u| ≤ 1 (theory chapter, Lemma 1) and recurred downward, ψₙ as a product of ratios, ξₙ through w = ξₙ/ξₙ₋₁. Arb's complex balls are rectangles, which complex recurrences rotate and Arb must re-enclose: they grew 1.5× per step and became useless after a few hundred orders; `disk.py` keeps a centre and a radius instead (Gargantini & Henrici 1972). The generator is not registered yet (Task 3 adds the series and the data).

**Files:**
- Create: `reference/scatrs_reference/disk.py`, `reference/scatrs_reference/generators/mie.py`
- Test: `reference/tests/test_mie.py`
- Modify: `pyproject.toml` (ruff: allow ρ, × and ′ in docstrings)

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: python-flint `acb`, `arb`, `ctx`.
- Produces:
  - `disk.Disk(centre: acb, radius: arb)`; `disk.disk(v: acb) -> Disk`, `disk.ball(d: Disk) -> acb`, `disk.sub_from(c: acb, d: Disk) -> Disk`, `disk.inv(d: Disk) -> Disk` (raises `ArithmeticError` if d may contain 0), `disk.mul(d: Disk, e: Disk) -> Disk`.
  - `mie.ratios_real(x: arb, n_top: int) -> list[arb]` and `mie.ratios_complex(z: acb, n_top: int) -> list[acb]`: uₖ at index k for k = 1..n_top + 1.
  - `mie.Solution` dataclass with `x: arb, m: acb, n_ref: int, a, b, da_dx, da_dm, db_dx, db_dm, d, delta_a, delta_b: list[acb]` (index n − 1) and `t_last: acb` (ψ_N/ξ_N), `r_last: acb` (ξ_{N−1}/ξ_N).
  - `mie.solve(x: float, m_re: float, m_im: float, n_ref: int) -> Solution` and `mie.solve_exact(xa: arb, m: acb, n_ref: int) -> Solution`, at the current working precision.

```toml
# Deliberate typography in docstrings and comments: minus sign and en dash (Gauss–Legendre).
allowed-confusables = ["−", "–"]
```

with

```toml
# Deliberate typography in docstrings and comments: minus sign and en dash (Gauss–Legendre), and

allowed-confusables = ["−", "–", "ρ", "×", "′"]
```

- [ ] **Step 2: Write the failing tests**

The derivatives are checked against central differences at 600 bits (step 2⁻¹²⁰: truncation about 2⁻²⁴⁰), the absorption identity (theory chapter, Proposition 1) against the coefficients themselves, and the disk operations against exact results at points of the disk.

Create `reference/tests/test_mie.py`:

```python
"""Checks of the Mie generator against independent computations in Arb."""

import pytest
from flint import acb, arb, ctx

from scatrs_reference import disk
from scatrs_reference.generators import mie


@pytest.mark.parametrize(
    ("x", "m_re", "m_im"), [(0.7, 1.5, 0.01), (5.0, 2.5, 1.2), (4.0, 1.1, 0.3)]
)
def test_closed_form_derivatives_match_central_differences(x, m_re, m_im):
    # Central differences with step 2^-120 at 600 bits: truncation ~2^-240, rounding ~2^-480.
    with ctx.workprec(600):
        n = 12
        s = mie.solve(x, m_re, m_im, n)
        h = arb(2) ** -120

        def at(dx, dre):
            p = mie.solve_exact(arb(x) + dx, acb(arb(m_re) + dre, arb(m_im)), n)
            return p.a, p.b

        (ax_p, bx_p), (ax_n, bx_n) = at(h, 0), at(-h, 0)
        (am_p, bm_p), (am_n, bm_n) = at(0, h), at(0, -h)
        for k in range(n):
            pairs = [
                (s.da_dx[k], (ax_p[k] - ax_n[k]) / (2 * h)),
                (s.db_dx[k], (bx_p[k] - bx_n[k]) / (2 * h)),
                (s.da_dm[k], (am_p[k] - am_n[k]) / (2 * h)),
                (s.db_dm[k], (bm_p[k] - bm_n[k]) / (2 * h)),
            ]
            for got, fd in pairs:
                assert abs(got - fd) <= arb(2) ** -200 * abs(fd) + arb(2) ** -400


@pytest.mark.parametrize(
    ("x", "m_re", "m_im"), [(0.7, 1.5, 0.01), (30.0, 1.33, 1e-8), (8.0, 0.2, 3.0)]
)
def test_absorption_identity(x, m_re, m_im):
    # Re a_n − |a_n|² = −Im(D_n/m)/|Δ_a|², and likewise for b_n with m D_n.
    with ctx.workprec(400):
        s = mie.solve(x, m_re, m_im, 40)
        for k in range(40):
            a, b, d = s.a[k], s.b[k], s.d[k]
            lhs_a = a.real - abs(a) ** 2
            rhs_a = -(d / s.m).imag / abs(s.delta_a[k]) ** 2
            lhs_b = b.real - abs(b) ** 2
            rhs_b = -(s.m * d).imag / abs(s.delta_b[k]) ** 2
            assert abs(lhs_a - rhs_a) <= arb(2) ** -300 * (abs(a) + arb(2) ** -1000)
            assert abs(lhs_b - rhs_b) <= arb(2) ** -300 * (abs(b) + arb(2) ** -1000)


def test_disk_arithmetic_encloses_the_exact_results():
    with ctx.workprec(200):
        c, w = acb("0.3", "-1.7"), acb("2.5", "0.25")
        d = disk.Disk(c, arb("1e-20"))
        e = disk.Disk(w, arb("1e-25"))
        for corner in (acb("1e-20"), acb(0, "-1e-20"), acb("-7e-21", "7e-21")):
            p = c + corner
            assert disk.ball(disk.inv(d)).contains(1 / p)
            assert disk.ball(disk.mul(d, e)).contains(p * w)
            assert disk.ball(disk.sub_from(w, d)).contains(w - p)
        with pytest.raises(ArithmeticError):
            disk.inv(disk.Disk(acb(0), arb(1)))
```

- [ ] **Step 4: Write the disk arithmetic**

Create `reference/scatrs_reference/disk.py`:

```python
"""Complex disk arithmetic on top of Arb, for long complex recurrences.

Arb's complex balls (``acb``) are rectangles: the real and imaginary parts each carry a radius.
A recurrence that multiplies by complex numbers of modulus near 1 rotates the rectangle, and
Arb must enclose each rotated rectangle in a larger axis-parallel one, up to √2 times larger
per step, although the set of possible values stays a disk of about the same size. In the Mie
recurrences the radius grew by about 1.5× per step, and the balls became useless after a few
hundred orders. A :class:`Disk` keeps an exact centre and a radius instead (circular complex
interval arithmetic, Gargantini & Henrici 1972): each operation computes the centre with Arb,
recentres on the midpoint of Arb's result, and adds a rigorous bound on the propagated radius.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass

from flint import acb, arb


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True)
class Disk:
    """The closed disk {w : |w − centre| ≤ radius}.

    ``centre`` is an exact ``acb`` (zero radius) and ``radius`` an exact ``arb`` ≥ 0.
    """

    centre: acb
    radius: arb


def _recentre(v: acb, extra: arb | None = None) -> Disk:
    """The disk centred on the midpoint of ``v`` that contains ``v`` grown by ``extra``.

    The rectangle of ``v`` lies within ``re.rad() + im.rad()`` of its midpoint (its
    half-diagonal is no longer than that).
    """
    r = v.real.rad() + v.imag.rad()
    if extra is not None:
        r = r + extra
    return Disk(v.mid(), r.abs_upper())


def disk(v: acb) -> Disk:
    """The smallest disk this module builds around the ball ``v``."""
    return _recentre(v)


def ball(d: Disk) -> acb:
    """An Arb ball (a square) containing the disk ``d``."""
    return d.centre + acb(arb(0, d.radius), arb(0, d.radius))


def sub_from(c: acb, d: Disk) -> Disk:
    """A disk containing ``c − w`` for every point ``c`` of the ball and ``w`` of ``d``."""
    return _recentre(c - d.centre, d.radius)


def inv(d: Disk) -> Disk:
    """A disk containing ``1/w`` for every ``w`` in ``d``.

    For ``|w − c| ≤ r < |c|``: ``|1/w − 1/c| = |c − w| / (|w| |c|) ≤ r / ((|c| − r) |c|)``.

    Raises
    ------
    ArithmeticError
        If ``d`` may contain zero.
    """
    m = abs(d.centre)
    lower = m - d.radius
    if not lower > 0:
        raise ArithmeticError("the disk may contain zero")
    return _recentre(1 / d.centre, d.radius / (m * lower))


def mul(d: Disk, e: Disk) -> Disk:
    """A disk containing ``v w`` for every ``v`` in ``d`` and ``w`` in ``e``.

    ``|v w − c₁ c₂| ≤ |c₁| r₂ + |c₂| r₁ + r₁ r₂``.
    """
    return _recentre(
        d.centre * e.centre,
        abs(d.centre) * e.radius + abs(e.centre) * d.radius + d.radius * e.radius,
    )
```

- [ ] **Step 5: Write the coefficient generator**

Create `reference/scatrs_reference/generators/mie.py`:

```python
"""Arb reference values for Lorenz–Mie scattering by homogeneous spheres: the coefficients.

Conventions are those of Bohren & Huffman (1983), with time dependence e^(−iωt): the
refractive index m = n + ik has k ≥ 0 for absorbing spheres, and

    a_n = [(D_n(mx)/m + n/x) ψ_n(x) − ψ_{n−1}(x)] / [(D_n(mx)/m + n/x) ξ_n(x) − ξ_{n−1}(x)],

b_n likewise with m·D_n(mx) in place of D_n(mx)/m. Here ψ_n(ρ) = ρ j_n(ρ), ξ_n = ψ_n − iχ_n with
χ_n(ρ) = −ρ y_n(ρ), and D_n = ψ_n′/ψ_n. Each value is computed at the exact inputs.

Method. Every step is rigorous; nothing calls Arb's Bessel functions, which lose most of their
accuracy at large orders (5 of 320 bits at order 2000):

* u_k(ρ) = ψ_k(ρ)/ψ_{k−1}(ρ) for k ≤ n + 1, by the downward recurrence
  u_k = 1/((2k + 1)/ρ − u_{k+1}), started at k₀ ≥ 2|ρ| from the enclosure |u_{k₀+1}| ≤ 1. The
  enclosure holds because u ↦ 1/(c − u) maps the unit disk into itself when |c| ≥ 2, and the
  continued fraction converges to u_k (Pincherle's theorem: ψ is the minimal solution of the
  recurrence). Each step from k ≥ 2|ρ| shrinks the uncertainty at least ninefold. Then
  D_n(ρ) = (n + 1)/ρ − u_{n+1}(ρ). Complex ρ uses disk arithmetic (`scatrs_reference.disk`).
* ψ_n(x) = ψ_{n−1}(x)·u_n(x) from ψ₀ = sin x; ξ_n = ξ_{n−1}·w_n, with
  w_n = ξ_n/ξ_{n−1} = (2n − 1)/x − 1/w_{n−1} from w₀ = ξ₀/ξ₋₁ = −i, in disk arithmetic.
* a_n and b_n from the formulas above, and their derivatives from the closed forms
  ∂a_n/∂m = i(D − zD′)/(m²Δ_a²), ∂b_n/∂m = −i(D + zD′)/Δ_b²,
  ∂a_n/∂x = i(m² − 1)(n(n + 1)/x² + D²)/(m²Δ_a²) and ∂b_n/∂x = i(m² − 1)/Δ_b², where z = mx,
  D = D_n(z), D′ = n(n + 1)/z² − 1 − D² and Δ_a, Δ_b are the denominators of a_n and b_n.
  ``tests/test_mie.py`` checks them against Arb central differences.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass

from flint import acb, arb, ctx

from scatrs_reference import disk


def _ceil_abs(v: arb | acb) -> int:
    return int(abs(v).abs_upper().ceil().unique_fmpz())


def ratios_real(x: arb, n_top: int) -> list[arb]:
    """u_k(x) = ψ_k(x)/ψ_{k−1}(x) for k = 1, …, n_top + 1, at index k (index 0 is unused)."""
    start = max(n_top + 1, 2 * _ceil_abs(x)) + ctx.prec // 2
    out: list[arb] = [arb(0)] * (n_top + 2)
    inv_x = 1 / x
    u = arb(0, 1)  # encloses u_{start+1}
    for k in range(start, 0, -1):
        u = 1 / ((2 * k + 1) * inv_x - u)
        if k <= n_top + 1:
            out[k] = u
    return out


def ratios_complex(z: acb, n_top: int) -> list[acb]:
    """u_k(z) = ψ_k(z)/ψ_{k−1}(z) for k = 1, …, n_top + 1, at index k, in disk arithmetic."""
    start = max(n_top + 1, 2 * _ceil_abs(z)) + ctx.prec // 2
    out: list[acb] = [acb(0)] * (n_top + 2)
    inv_z = 1 / z
    u = disk.Disk(acb(0), arb(1))  # encloses u_{start+1}
    for k in range(start, 0, -1):
        u = disk.inv(disk.sub_from((2 * k + 1) * inv_z, u))
        if k <= n_top + 1:
            out[k] = disk.ball(u)
    return out


@dataclass
class Solution:
    """Mie coefficients of one sphere for n = 1..n_ref (index n − 1), at the working precision."""

    x: arb
    m: acb
    n_ref: int
    a: list[acb]
    b: list[acb]
    da_dx: list[acb]
    da_dm: list[acb]
    db_dx: list[acb]
    db_dm: list[acb]
    #: D_n(mx) and the denominators Δ_a, Δ_b of a_n and b_n.
    d: list[acb]
    delta_a: list[acb]
    delta_b: list[acb]
    #: T = ψ_N/ξ_N and R = ξ_{N−1}/ξ_N at N = n_ref, for the tail bound.
    t_last: acb
    r_last: acb


def solve(x: float, m_re: float, m_im: float, n_ref: int) -> Solution:
    """a_n, b_n and their derivatives for n = 1..n_ref at the current working precision."""
    return solve_exact(arb(x), acb(m_re, m_im), n_ref)


def solve_exact(xa: arb, m: acb, n_ref: int) -> Solution:
    """`solve` at the exact (ball) inputs ``xa`` and ``m``; real m (an exactly zero imaginary
    part) takes the real recurrence for D_n(mx)."""
    z = m * xa
    ux = ratios_real(xa, n_ref)
    if m.imag == 0:
        uz = [acb(u) for u in ratios_real(m.real * xa, n_ref)]
    else:
        uz = ratios_complex(z, n_ref)
    sin_x, cos_x = xa.sin(), xa.cos()
    psi_prev, psi = cos_x, sin_x  # ψ₋₁, ψ₀
    xi = disk.disk(acb(sin_x, -cos_x))  # ξ₀
    w = disk.Disk(acb(0, -1), arb(0))  # w₀ = ξ₀/ξ₋₁
    i = acb(0, 1)
    m2 = m * m
    s = Solution(xa, m, n_ref, [], [], [], [], [], [], [], [], [], acb(0), acb(0))
    xi_prev = disk.ball(xi)
    xin = xi_prev
    for n in range(1, n_ref + 1):
        psi_prev, psi = psi, psi * ux[n]
        w = disk.sub_from(acb((2 * n - 1) / xa), disk.inv(w))
        xi_prev = disk.ball(xi)
        xi = disk.mul(xi, w)
        xin = disk.ball(xi)
        d = (n + 1) / z - uz[n + 1]
        big_a = d / m + n / xa
        big_b = m * d + n / xa
        delta_a = big_a * xin - xi_prev
        delta_b = big_b * xin - xi_prev
        s.a.append((big_a * psi - psi_prev) / delta_a)
        s.b.append((big_b * psi - psi_prev) / delta_b)
        dp = n * (n + 1) / (z * z) - 1 - d * d
        s.da_dm.append(i * (d - z * dp) / (m2 * delta_a * delta_a))
        s.db_dm.append(-i * (d + z * dp) / (delta_b * delta_b))
        s.da_dx.append(i * (m2 - 1) * (n * (n + 1) / (xa * xa) + d * d) / (m2 * delta_a * delta_a))
        s.db_dx.append(i * (m2 - 1) / (delta_b * delta_b))
        s.d.append(d)
        s.delta_a.append(delta_a)
        s.delta_b.append(delta_b)
    s.t_last = psi / xin
    s.r_last = xi_prev / xin
    return s
```

### Task 3: Mie series, the tail bound, and the reference data

The series (Q_ext, Q_sca, Q_abs = Q_ext − Q_sca, Q_back, g, S₁, S₂, and the derivatives of each) and the proven tail bound (theory chapter, Proposition 3): for n_ref ≥ |m|x and n_ref ≥ 1.5x, |aₙ| + |bₙ| ≤ |T_N|(F_a + F_b)qⁿ⁻ᴺ beyond N = n_ref, so each series tail is bounded by a geometric sum, which is added to the value's error. π_n and τ_n come from Arb's Legendre polynomials (accurate at any order, 0.3 s for 30 000 orders). The precision starts at 320 bits and doubles until every stored value's radius is below 2⁻²⁰⁰ of its scale. Cases run in parallel processes (`SCATRS_REFERENCE_WORKERS`); the records do not depend on the number.

Two files, two records per sphere (`…,coefficients` and `…,series`): `mie.jsonl` (306 spheres: 19 x × 14 m and 40 random; values) and `mie_derivatives.jsonl` (80 spheres; derivatives). Coefficient records are certified; series records of values are certified where n_ref ≤ 60 000 (297 of 306), else high-precision; series of derivatives are high-precision (no tail bound yet).

**Files:**
- Modify: `reference/scatrs_reference/generators/mie.py` (replace), `reference/scatrs_reference/generators/__init__.py`, `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, `docs/validation/reference-suite.md`
- Test: `reference/tests/test_mie.py` (replace)
- Create (generated): `reference/data/mie.jsonl`, `reference/data/mie_derivatives.jsonl`; modified: `reference/data/manifest.json`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: Task 2's `solve`, `solve_exact`, `Solution`, `ratios_*`, `disk`.
- Produces (used by the Rust tests of Tasks 5):
  - Record inputs: `x`, `m_re`, `m_im` (exact doubles); `orders` (1-based, coefficient records); `mu` (cosines, exactly 0 at 90°) and `n_ref` (series records).
  - Outputs of `mie.jsonl`: `a_re`, `a_im`, `b_re`, `b_im` at `orders`; `q_ext`, `q_sca`, `q_abs`, `q_back`, `g` (one value each); `s1_re`, `s1_im`, `s2_re`, `s2_im` at `mu`.
  - Outputs of `mie_derivatives.jsonl`: `da_dx_*`, `da_dm_*`, `db_dx_*`, `db_dm_*` (`*` = `re`, `im`) at `orders`; `{q_ext,q_sca,q_abs,q_back,g}_{dx,dm_re,dm_im}`; `s1_dx_*`, `s1_dm_*`, `s2_dx_*`, `s2_dm_*` at `mu` (dm is the complex derivative).
  - Python: `mie.n_ref(x, m_re, m_im) -> tuple[int, bool]`, `mie.sum_series(s, mus) -> Sums`, `mie.tail_bounds(s) -> Tail | None`, `mie.series_outputs(s, sums, tail) -> dict | None`, `mie.series_derivatives(s, sums) -> dict`, `mie.generate()`, `mie.generate_derivatives()`.

```python
"""Checks of the Mie generator against independent computations in Arb."""

import json
from pathlib import Path

import pytest
from flint import acb, arb, ctx

from scatrs_reference import disk
from scatrs_reference.generators import mie

DATA = Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent / "data"


@pytest.mark.parametrize(
    ("x", "m_re", "m_im"), [(0.7, 1.5, 0.01), (5.0, 2.5, 1.2), (4.0, 1.1, 0.3)]
)
def test_closed_form_derivatives_match_central_differences(x, m_re, m_im):
    # Central differences with step 2^-120 at 600 bits: truncation ~2^-240, rounding ~2^-480.
    with ctx.workprec(600):
        n = 12
        s = mie.solve(x, m_re, m_im, n)
        h = arb(2) ** -120

        def at(dx, dre):
            p = mie.solve_exact(arb(x) + dx, acb(arb(m_re) + dre, arb(m_im)), n)
            return p.a, p.b

        (ax_p, bx_p), (ax_n, bx_n) = at(h, 0), at(-h, 0)
        (am_p, bm_p), (am_n, bm_n) = at(0, h), at(0, -h)
        for k in range(n):
            pairs = [
                (s.da_dx[k], (ax_p[k] - ax_n[k]) / (2 * h)),
                (s.db_dx[k], (bx_p[k] - bx_n[k]) / (2 * h)),
                (s.da_dm[k], (am_p[k] - am_n[k]) / (2 * h)),
                (s.db_dm[k], (bm_p[k] - bm_n[k]) / (2 * h)),
            ]
            for got, fd in pairs:
                assert abs(got - fd) <= arb(2) ** -200 * abs(fd) + arb(2) ** -400


@pytest.mark.parametrize(
    ("x", "m_re", "m_im"), [(0.7, 1.5, 0.01), (30.0, 1.33, 1e-8), (8.0, 0.2, 3.0)]
)
def test_absorption_identity(x, m_re, m_im):
    # Re a_n − |a_n|² = −Im(D_n/m)/|Δ_a|², and likewise for b_n with m D_n.
    with ctx.workprec(400):
        s = mie.solve(x, m_re, m_im, 40)
        for k in range(40):
            a, b, d = s.a[k], s.b[k], s.d[k]
            lhs_a = a.real - abs(a) ** 2
            rhs_a = -(d / s.m).imag / abs(s.delta_a[k]) ** 2
            lhs_b = b.real - abs(b) ** 2
            rhs_b = -(s.m * d).imag / abs(s.delta_b[k]) ** 2
            assert abs(lhs_a - rhs_a) <= arb(2) ** -300 * (abs(a) + arb(2) ** -1000)
            assert abs(lhs_b - rhs_b) <= arb(2) ** -300 * (abs(b) + arb(2) ** -1000)


def test_wiscombe_published_values():
    # Wiscombe (1979): x = 1000, m = 0.75, Q_sca = 1.997908, S(0) = 4.994770e5 + 1.336502e4 i
    # (conjugated: MIEV0 uses e^(+iwt)).
    with ctx.workprec(320):
        s = mie.solve(1000.0, 0.75, 0.0, 1100)
        sums = mie.sum_series(s, [1.0])
        assert abs(sums.q_sca - arb("1.997908")) < arb("1e-6")
        assert abs(sums.s1[0] - acb("4.994770e5", "1.336502e4")) < arb("0.1")


def test_tail_bound_dominates_the_next_terms():
    for x, m_re, m_im in [(1.0, 1.5, 0.01), (50.0, 1.33, 0.0), (20.0, 4.0, 0.01), (5.0, 0.2, 3.0)]:
        terms, certify = mie.n_ref(x, m_re, m_im)
        assert certify
        with ctx.workprec(320):
            s = mie.solve(x, m_re, m_im, terms)
            tail = mie.tail_bounds(s)
            assert tail is not None and tail.ratio < 1
            longer = mie.solve(x, m_re, m_im, terms + 30)
            for n in range(terms + 1, terms + 31):
                size = abs(longer.a[n - 1]) + abs(longer.b[n - 1])
                assert size <= tail.amplitude * tail.ratio ** (n - terms)


def test_certified_series_contain_a_longer_sum():
    x, m_re, m_im = 10.0, 1.5, 0.1
    terms, _ = mie.n_ref(x, m_re, m_im)
    with ctx.workprec(320):
        s = mie.solve(x, m_re, m_im, terms)
        out = mie.series_outputs(s, mie.sum_series(s, [1.0, 0.0]), mie.tail_bounds(s))
        longer = mie.solve(x, m_re, m_im, terms + 50)
        ref = mie.series_outputs(longer, mie.sum_series(longer, [1.0, 0.0]), None)
        for name in out:
            for v, w in zip(out[name], ref[name], strict=True):
                assert v.overlaps(w), name


def test_optical_theorem():
    with ctx.workprec(320):
        for x, m_re, m_im in [(0.1, 1.33, 0.0), (7.0, 1.5, 0.1), (300.0, 0.2, 3.0)]:
            s = mie.solve(x, m_re, m_im, mie.n_ref(x, m_re, m_im)[0])
            sums = mie.sum_series(s, [1.0])
            q = 4 * sums.s1[0].real / (s.x * s.x)
            assert abs(q - sums.q_ext) <= arb(2) ** -250 * sums.q_ext


def test_disk_arithmetic_encloses_the_exact_results():
    with ctx.workprec(200):
        c, w = acb("0.3", "-1.7"), acb("2.5", "0.25")
        d = disk.Disk(c, arb("1e-20"))
        e = disk.Disk(w, arb("1e-25"))
        for corner in (acb("1e-20"), acb(0, "-1e-20"), acb("-7e-21", "7e-21")):
            p = c + corner
            assert disk.ball(disk.inv(d)).contains(1 / p)
            assert disk.ball(disk.mul(d, e)).contains(p * w)
            assert disk.ball(disk.sub_from(w, d)).contains(w - p)
        with pytest.raises(ArithmeticError):
            disk.inv(disk.Disk(acb(0), arb(1)))


def test_records_follow_the_label_rules():
    for line in (DATA / "mie.jsonl").read_text(encoding="utf-8").splitlines():
        rec = json.loads(line)
        if rec["case"].endswith(",coefficients"):
            assert rec["label"] == "certified"
        else:
            x, m_re, m_im = (rec["inputs"][k] for k in ("x", "m_re", "m_im"))
            terms, certify = mie.n_ref(x, m_re, m_im)
            assert rec["inputs"]["n_ref"] == terms
            if rec["label"] == "certified":
                assert certify
    for line in (DATA / "mie_derivatives.jsonl").read_text(encoding="utf-8").splitlines():
        rec = json.loads(line)
        expected = "certified" if rec["case"].endswith(",coefficients") else "high-precision"
        assert rec["label"] == expected
```

```python
"""Arb reference values for Lorenz–Mie scattering by homogeneous spheres.

Conventions are those of Bohren & Huffman (1983), with time dependence e^(−iωt): the
refractive index m = n + ik has k ≥ 0 for absorbing spheres, and

    a_n = [(D_n(mx)/m + n/x) ψ_n(x) − ψ_{n−1}(x)] / [(D_n(mx)/m + n/x) ξ_n(x) − ξ_{n−1}(x)],

b_n likewise with m·D_n(mx) in place of D_n(mx)/m. Here ψ_n(ρ) = ρ j_n(ρ), ξ_n = ψ_n − iχ_n with
χ_n(ρ) = −ρ y_n(ρ), and D_n = ψ_n′/ψ_n. Each value is computed at the exact double inputs.

Method. Every step is rigorous; nothing calls Arb's Bessel functions, which lose most of their
accuracy at large orders (5 of 320 bits at order 2000):

* u_k(ρ) = ψ_k(ρ)/ψ_{k−1}(ρ) for k ≤ n + 1, by the downward recurrence
  u_k = 1/((2k + 1)/ρ − u_{k+1}), started at k₀ ≥ 2|ρ| from the enclosure |u_{k₀+1}| ≤ 1. The
  enclosure holds because u ↦ 1/(c − u) maps the unit disk into itself when |c| ≥ 2, and the
  continued fraction converges to u_k (Pincherle's theorem: ψ is the minimal solution of the
  recurrence). Each step from k ≥ 2|ρ| shrinks the uncertainty at least ninefold. Then
  D_n(ρ) = (n + 1)/ρ − u_{n+1}(ρ). Complex ρ uses disk arithmetic (`scatrs_reference.disk`).
* ψ_n(x) = ψ_{n−1}(x)·u_n(x) from ψ₀ = sin x; ξ_n = ξ_{n−1}·w_n, with
  w_n = ξ_n/ξ_{n−1} = (2n − 1)/x − 1/w_{n−1} from w₀ = ξ₀/ξ₋₁ = −i, in disk arithmetic.
* a_n and b_n from the formulas above, and their derivatives from the closed forms
  ∂a_n/∂m = i(D − zD′)/(m²Δ_a²), ∂b_n/∂m = −i(D + zD′)/Δ_b²,
  ∂a_n/∂x = i(m² − 1)(n(n + 1)/x² + D²)/(m²Δ_a²) and ∂b_n/∂x = i(m² − 1)/Δ_b², where z = mx,
  D = D_n(z), D′ = n(n + 1)/z² − 1 − D² and Δ_a, Δ_b are the denominators of a_n and b_n.
  ``tests/test_mie.py`` checks them against Arb central differences.
* The series Q_ext, Q_sca, Q_abs = Q_ext − Q_sca (exactly 0 for real m), Q_back, g, S₁ and S₂
  are summed to n_ref terms. π_n and τ_n come from Arb's Legendre polynomials:
  π_n = n(μP_n − P_{n−1})/(μ² − 1) and τ_n = n(n + 1)P_n − μπ_n, with the closed forms
  π_n(±1) = (±1)^(n+1) n(n + 1)/2 at μ = ±1.

Each case is computed at ``PRECISION_BITS``, and the precision is doubled until every stored
value has a radius of at most 2^-``REL_BITS`` of its scale (the largest value of its array; for
Q_abs, Q_ext; for Q_back, Q_sca; for S₁ and S₂, the RMS amplitude x√Q_sca/2).

Records. For each case ``x=…,m=(…, …)`` the file holds a record ``…,coefficients`` with a_n and
b_n at the orders ``orders`` (``mie.jsonl``; with the four derivatives in
``mie_derivatives.jsonl``), and a record ``…,series`` with the series at the cosines ``mu``
(and, in ``mie_derivatives.jsonl``, their derivatives with respect to x, Re m and Im m; for S₁
and S₂, with respect to x and m).

Labels. Coefficient records are finite computations, so certified. A series record of values
is certified when n_ref is large enough for the tail bound of `tail_bounds` (proved in the
theory chapter, "Series tail bound"): n_ref ≥ |m|x and n_ref ≥ 1.5x. The bound is then added
to each value's error. Otherwise, when that would take more than ``CERTIFY_MAX_ORDER`` terms,
the series is summed to ⌈x + 18x^(1/3) + 32⌉ terms and labelled high-precision. The series of
derivatives have no tail bound yet: they are high-precision.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import os
import random
from collections.abc import Callable, Sequence
from concurrent.futures import ProcessPoolExecutor
from dataclasses import dataclass

from flint import acb, arb, ctx

from scatrs_reference import disk
from scatrs_reference.records import PORTABLE_BITS, nearest_double, nearest_pow10, record

PRECISION_BITS = 320
REL_BITS = 200
MAX_PRECISION_BITS = 2560
#: Series needing more terms than this to certify are summed to the high-precision length.
CERTIFY_MAX_ORDER = 60_000

XS = (
    1e-6, 1e-4, 1e-2, 0.1, 0.5, 1.0, 2.0, 5.0, 10.0, 20.0, 50.0, 100.0, 200.0, 500.0, 1000.0,
    2000.0, 5000.0, 10000.0, 20000.0,
)  # fmt: skip
#: (Re m, Im m): near-unity, water and aerosol, high index, strong absorption and metal-like.
MS = (
    (1.0001, 0.0), (1.01, 0.0), (1.33, 0.0), (1.33, 1e-8), (1.5, 1e-3), (1.5, 0.1),
    (1.75, 0.44), (2.5, 1.0), (4.0, 0.01), (10.0, 0.0), (10.0, 10.0), (1.5, 5.0), (0.2, 3.0),
    (0.05, 4.0),
)  # fmt: skip
RANDOM_CASES = 40
SEED = 20261013
#: Scattering angles of the amplitude records, in degrees.
ANGLES_DEG = (0.0, 0.5, 5.0, 30.0, 60.0, 90.0, 135.0, 170.0, 180.0)
DERIVATIVE_XS = (1e-6, 1e-2, 0.5, 2.0, 10.0, 50.0, 200.0, 1000.0, 5000.0, 20000.0)
DERIVATIVE_MS = (
    (1.0001, 0.0), (1.33, 0.0), (1.33, 1e-8), (1.5, 0.1), (2.5, 1.0), (10.0, 10.0), (0.2, 3.0),
    (1.5, 5.0),
)  # fmt: skip

Case = tuple[float, float, float]


def size_terms(x: float, c: int, d: int) -> int:
    """⌈x + c·x^(1/3) + d⌉, computed exactly (scatrs keeps (c, d) = (6, 3) terms in f64)."""
    with ctx.workprec(PORTABLE_BITS):
        v = arb(x) + c * arb(x).root(3) + d
        return int(v.ceil().unique_fmpz())


def orders(n: int) -> list[int]:
    """The orders whose coefficients a record stores: all of 1..n for n ≤ 12, else 11 of them."""
    if n <= 12:
        return list(range(1, n + 1))
    return sorted({1, 2, 3, n // 64, n // 16, n // 4, n // 2, 3 * n // 4, n - 2, n - 1, n} - {0})


def cosines() -> list[float]:
    """cos θ for θ in ``ANGLES_DEG``, rounded to the nearest double (exactly 0 at 90°)."""
    with ctx.workprec(PORTABLE_BITS):
        return [nearest_double((arb(a) / 180).cos_pi()) for a in ANGLES_DEG]


def _ceil_abs(v: arb | acb) -> int:
    return int(abs(v).abs_upper().ceil().unique_fmpz())


def ratios_real(x: arb, n_top: int) -> list[arb]:
    """u_k(x) = ψ_k(x)/ψ_{k−1}(x) for k = 1, …, n_top + 1, at index k (index 0 is unused)."""
    start = max(n_top + 1, 2 * _ceil_abs(x)) + ctx.prec // 2
    out: list[arb] = [arb(0)] * (n_top + 2)
    inv_x = 1 / x
    u = arb(0, 1)  # encloses u_{start+1}
    for k in range(start, 0, -1):
        u = 1 / ((2 * k + 1) * inv_x - u)
        if k <= n_top + 1:
            out[k] = u
    return out


def ratios_complex(z: acb, n_top: int) -> list[acb]:
    """u_k(z) = ψ_k(z)/ψ_{k−1}(z) for k = 1, …, n_top + 1, at index k, in disk arithmetic."""
    start = max(n_top + 1, 2 * _ceil_abs(z)) + ctx.prec // 2
    out: list[acb] = [acb(0)] * (n_top + 2)
    inv_z = 1 / z
    u = disk.Disk(acb(0), arb(1))  # encloses u_{start+1}
    for k in range(start, 0, -1):
        u = disk.inv(disk.sub_from((2 * k + 1) * inv_z, u))
        if k <= n_top + 1:
            out[k] = disk.ball(u)
    return out


@dataclass
class Solution:
    """Mie coefficients of one sphere for n = 1..n_ref (index n − 1), at the working precision."""

    x: arb
    m: acb
    n_ref: int
    a: list[acb]
    b: list[acb]
    da_dx: list[acb]
    da_dm: list[acb]
    db_dx: list[acb]
    db_dm: list[acb]
    #: D_n(mx) and the denominators Δ_a, Δ_b of a_n and b_n.
    d: list[acb]
    delta_a: list[acb]
    delta_b: list[acb]
    #: T = ψ_N/ξ_N and R = ξ_{N−1}/ξ_N at N = n_ref, for the tail bound.
    t_last: acb
    r_last: acb


def solve(x: float, m_re: float, m_im: float, n_ref: int) -> Solution:
    """a_n, b_n and their derivatives for n = 1..n_ref at the current working precision."""
    return solve_exact(arb(x), acb(m_re, m_im), n_ref)


def solve_exact(xa: arb, m: acb, n_ref: int) -> Solution:
    """`solve` at the exact (ball) inputs ``xa`` and ``m``; real m (an exactly zero imaginary
    part) takes the real recurrence for D_n(mx)."""
    z = m * xa
    ux = ratios_real(xa, n_ref)
    if m.imag == 0:
        uz = [acb(u) for u in ratios_real(m.real * xa, n_ref)]
    else:
        uz = ratios_complex(z, n_ref)
    sin_x, cos_x = xa.sin(), xa.cos()
    psi_prev, psi = cos_x, sin_x  # ψ₋₁, ψ₀
    xi = disk.disk(acb(sin_x, -cos_x))  # ξ₀
    w = disk.Disk(acb(0, -1), arb(0))  # w₀ = ξ₀/ξ₋₁
    i = acb(0, 1)
    m2 = m * m
    s = Solution(xa, m, n_ref, [], [], [], [], [], [], [], [], [], acb(0), acb(0))
    xi_prev = disk.ball(xi)
    xin = xi_prev
    for n in range(1, n_ref + 1):
        psi_prev, psi = psi, psi * ux[n]
        w = disk.sub_from(acb((2 * n - 1) / xa), disk.inv(w))
        xi_prev = disk.ball(xi)
        xi = disk.mul(xi, w)
        xin = disk.ball(xi)
        d = (n + 1) / z - uz[n + 1]
        big_a = d / m + n / xa
        big_b = m * d + n / xa
        delta_a = big_a * xin - xi_prev
        delta_b = big_b * xin - xi_prev
        s.a.append((big_a * psi - psi_prev) / delta_a)
        s.b.append((big_b * psi - psi_prev) / delta_b)
        dp = n * (n + 1) / (z * z) - 1 - d * d
        s.da_dm.append(i * (d - z * dp) / (m2 * delta_a * delta_a))
        s.db_dm.append(-i * (d + z * dp) / (delta_b * delta_b))
        s.da_dx.append(i * (m2 - 1) * (n * (n + 1) / (xa * xa) + d * d) / (m2 * delta_a * delta_a))
        s.db_dx.append(i * (m2 - 1) / (delta_b * delta_b))
        s.d.append(d)
        s.delta_a.append(delta_a)
        s.delta_b.append(delta_b)
    s.t_last = psi / xin
    s.r_last = xi_prev / xin
    return s


def _pi_tau(mu: float, n_ref: int) -> tuple[list[arb], list[arb]]:
    """π_n(μ) and τ_n(μ) for n = 1..n_ref (index n − 1)."""
    mu_a = arb(mu)
    pis, taus = [], []
    if mu in (1.0, -1.0):
        for n in range(1, n_ref + 1):
            sign = 1 if mu == 1.0 or n % 2 == 1 else -1
            pi_n = arb(sign * n * (n + 1)) / 2
            pis.append(pi_n)
            taus.append(n * (n + 1) * mu_a.legendre_p(n) - mu_a * pi_n)
        return pis, taus
    p_prev = arb(1)
    for n in range(1, n_ref + 1):
        p = mu_a.legendre_p(n)
        pi_n = n * (mu_a * p - p_prev) / (mu_a * mu_a - 1)
        pis.append(pi_n)
        taus.append(n * (n + 1) * p - mu_a * pi_n)
        p_prev = p
    return pis, taus


@dataclass
class Sums:
    """Partial sums of the series up to n_ref."""

    q_ext: arb
    q_sca: arb
    s_back: acb  # Σ (2n + 1)(−1)^n (a_n − b_n)
    g_sum: arb  # G, with g = 4G/(x² Q_sca)
    s1: list[acb]
    s2: list[acb]
    pi_tau: list[tuple[list[arb], list[arb]]]


def sum_series(s: Solution, mus: Sequence[float]) -> Sums:
    """The partial sums of every series of `series_outputs`, to n_ref terms."""
    two_x2 = 2 / (s.x * s.x)
    q_ext, q_sca, s_back, g_sum = arb(0), arb(0), acb(0), arb(0)
    n_ref = s.n_ref
    for n in range(1, n_ref + 1):
        a, b = s.a[n - 1], s.b[n - 1]
        q_ext += (2 * n + 1) * (a + b).real
        q_sca += (2 * n + 1) * (abs(a) ** 2 + abs(b) ** 2)
        sign = 1 if n % 2 == 0 else -1
        s_back += (2 * n + 1) * sign * (a - b)
        if n < n_ref:
            a1, b1 = s.a[n], s.b[n]
            g_sum += (
                arb(n * (n + 2)) / (n + 1) * ((a * a1.conjugate()).real + (b * b1.conjugate()).real)
            )
        g_sum += arb(2 * n + 1) / (n * (n + 1)) * (a * b.conjugate()).real
    s1, s2, pts = [], [], []
    for mu in mus:
        pis, taus = _pi_tau(mu, n_ref)
        t1, t2 = acb(0), acb(0)
        for n in range(1, n_ref + 1):
            c = arb(2 * n + 1) / (n * (n + 1))
            a, b = s.a[n - 1], s.b[n - 1]
            t1 += c * (a * pis[n - 1] + b * taus[n - 1])
            t2 += c * (a * taus[n - 1] + b * pis[n - 1])
        s1.append(t1)
        s2.append(t2)
        pts.append((pis, taus))
    return Sums(two_x2 * q_ext, two_x2 * q_sca, s_back, g_sum, s1, s2, pts)


@dataclass
class Tail:
    """|a_n| + |b_n| ≤ amplitude·ratio^(n − N) for every n > N = n_ref."""

    amplitude: arb
    ratio: arb


def tail_bounds(s: Solution) -> Tail | None:
    """The geometric bound on the coefficients beyond n_ref, or None where it does not apply.

    See the theory chapter, "Series tail bound". With N = n_ref, U_z bounds |u_k(mx)|, U_x
    bounds u_k(x) and R bounds |ξ_{k−1}/ξ_k| for all k > N; then |T_{n+1}| ≤ U_x·R·|T_n| for
    T_n = ψ_n/ξ_n, and the factors that turn T_n into a_n and b_n are bounded by F_a and F_b.
    """
    x, m, big_n = s.x, s.m, s.n_ref
    z_abs = abs(m * x)
    m_abs = abs(m)
    if not (2 * big_n + 3 >= 2 * z_abs and (2 * big_n + 1) / x >= 3 and abs(s.r_last) <= 1):
        return None
    u_z = 1 / ((2 * big_n + 3) / z_abs - 1)
    u_x = 1 / ((2 * big_n + 3) / x - 1)
    r_b = 1 / ((2 * big_n + 1) / x - 1)
    inv_m2 = 1 / (m * m)
    c1 = abs(inv_m2 - 1) / x
    e1 = u_z / m_abs + u_x
    c2 = abs(1 + inv_m2) / x
    e2 = 1 / (m_abs * m_abs * x) + u_z / m_abs + r_b
    den_a = (big_n + 1) * c2 - e2
    d_b = (2 * big_n + 3) / x - m_abs * u_z - r_b
    if not (den_a > 0 and d_b > 0):
        return None
    # f(n) = ((n + 1)c1 + e1)/(n c2 − e2) is monotone for n ≥ N + 1, so its supremum is at most
    # the sum of its value at N + 1 and its limit c1/c2.
    f_a = ((big_n + 2) * c1 + e1) / den_a + c1 / c2
    f_b = (u_x + m_abs * u_z) / d_b
    amplitude = abs(s.t_last) * (f_a + f_b)
    if not amplitude.is_finite():
        return None
    return Tail(amplitude, u_x * r_b)


def geometric_tail(weight: Callable[[int], arb], n0: int, amplitude: arb, ratio: arb) -> arb | None:
    """A bound on Σ_{n ≥ n0} weight(n)·amplitude·ratio^(n − n0 + 1).

    Valid for positive weights whose ratio weight(n + 1)/weight(n) does not increase with n
    (products of factors a·n + b with a, b ≥ 0). None if the terms do not decrease geometrically.
    """
    w0 = weight(n0)
    r = ratio * (weight(n0 + 1) / w0)
    if not r < 1:
        return None
    return w0 * amplitude * ratio / (1 - r)


def _grow(v: arb, t: arb) -> arb:
    return v + arb(0, t)


def _grow_c(v: acb, t: arb) -> acb:
    return v + acb(arb(0, t), arb(0, t))


def series_outputs(s: Solution, sums: Sums, tail: Tail | None) -> dict[str, list] | None:
    """Q_ext, Q_sca, Q_abs, Q_back, g and S₁, S₂, each grown by its tail bound if ``tail``.

    None if a tail sum does not converge geometrically.
    """
    x2 = s.x * s.x
    q_ext, q_sca, s_back, g_sum = sums.q_ext, sums.q_sca, sums.s_back, sums.g_sum
    s1, s2 = list(sums.s1), list(sums.s2)
    if tail is not None:
        n0 = s.n_ref + 1
        c, q = tail.amplitude, tail.ratio
        t_lin = geometric_tail(lambda n: arb(2 * n + 1), n0, c, q)
        t_sq = geometric_tail(lambda n: arb(2 * n + 1), n0, c * c, q * q)
        t_g1 = geometric_tail(lambda n: arb(n + 1), n0, c * c, q * q)
        t_g2 = geometric_tail(lambda n: arb(1), n0, c * c, q * q)
        t_s = geometric_tail(lambda n: arb((2 * n + 1) * n * (n + 1)) / 4, n0, c, q)
        if None in (t_lin, t_sq, t_g1, t_g2, t_s):
            return None
        last = abs(s.a[-1]) + abs(s.b[-1])
        q_ext = _grow(q_ext, 2 * t_lin / x2)
        q_sca = _grow(q_sca, 2 * t_sq / x2)
        s_back = _grow_c(s_back, t_lin)
        g_sum = _grow(g_sum, (s.n_ref + 1) * last * c * q + q * t_g1 + t_g2)
        s1 = [_grow_c(v, t_s) for v in s1]
        s2 = [_grow_c(v, t_s) for v in s2]
    q_abs = arb(0) if s.m.imag == 0 else q_ext - q_sca
    return {
        "q_ext": [q_ext],
        "q_sca": [q_sca],
        "q_abs": [q_abs],
        "q_back": [abs(s_back) ** 2 / x2],
        "g": [4 * g_sum / (x2 * q_sca)],
        "s1_re": [v.real for v in s1],
        "s1_im": [v.imag for v in s1],
        "s2_re": [v.real for v in s2],
        "s2_im": [v.imag for v in s2],
    }


def series_derivatives(s: Solution, sums: Sums) -> dict[str, list]:
    """∂/∂x, ∂/∂Re m and ∂/∂Im m of the five efficiencies, and ∂/∂x and d/dm of S₁ and S₂.

    Partial sums to n_ref terms, at the cosines of ``sums``.
    """
    x = s.x
    x2 = x * x
    i = acb(0, 1)
    q_sca = sums.q_sca
    q_back = abs(sums.s_back) ** 2 / x2
    g = 4 * sums.g_sum / (x2 * q_sca)
    q_ext = sums.q_ext
    out: dict[str, list] = {}
    # ∂a/∂p for p = x, Re m, Im m.
    da = {"dx": s.da_dx, "dm_re": s.da_dm, "dm_im": [i * v for v in s.da_dm]}
    db = {"dx": s.db_dx, "dm_re": s.db_dm, "dm_im": [i * v for v in s.db_dm]}
    for p in ("dx", "dm_re", "dm_im"):
        d_ext, d_sca, d_back, d_g = arb(0), arb(0), acb(0), arb(0)
        n_ref = s.n_ref
        for n in range(1, n_ref + 1):
            a, b, pa, pb = s.a[n - 1], s.b[n - 1], da[p][n - 1], db[p][n - 1]
            d_ext += (2 * n + 1) * (pa + pb).real
            d_sca += (2 * n + 1) * 2 * ((a.conjugate() * pa).real + (b.conjugate() * pb).real)
            sign = 1 if n % 2 == 0 else -1
            d_back += (2 * n + 1) * sign * (pa - pb)
            if n < n_ref:
                a1, b1, pa1, pb1 = s.a[n], s.b[n], da[p][n], db[p][n]
                d_g += (
                    arb(n * (n + 2))
                    / (n + 1)
                    * (
                        (pa * a1.conjugate() + a * pa1.conjugate()).real
                        + (pb * b1.conjugate() + b * pb1.conjugate()).real
                    )
                )
            d_g += arb(2 * n + 1) / (n * (n + 1)) * (pa * b.conjugate() + a * pb.conjugate()).real
        d_ext *= 2 / x2
        d_sca *= 2 / x2
        d_back_q = 2 * (sums.s_back.conjugate() * d_back).real / x2
        if p == "dx":
            d_ext -= 2 * q_ext / x
            d_sca -= 2 * q_sca / x
            d_back_q -= 2 * q_back / x
        d_g_out = 4 * d_g / (x2 * q_sca) - g * d_sca / q_sca
        if p == "dx":
            d_g_out -= 2 * g / x
        out[f"q_ext_{p}"] = [d_ext]
        out[f"q_sca_{p}"] = [d_sca]
        # Q_abs is identically 0 for real m, so its derivatives along x and Re m are too.
        real_m = s.m.imag == 0 and p != "dm_im"
        out[f"q_abs_{p}"] = [arb(0) if real_m else d_ext - d_sca]
        out[f"q_back_{p}"] = [d_back_q]
        out[f"g_{p}"] = [d_g_out]
    for key, coeff_a, coeff_b in (("dx", s.da_dx, s.db_dx), ("dm", s.da_dm, s.db_dm)):
        d1, d2 = [], []
        for pis, taus in sums.pi_tau:
            t1, t2 = acb(0), acb(0)
            for n in range(1, s.n_ref + 1):
                c = arb(2 * n + 1) / (n * (n + 1))
                pa, pb = coeff_a[n - 1], coeff_b[n - 1]
                t1 += c * (pa * pis[n - 1] + pb * taus[n - 1])
                t2 += c * (pa * taus[n - 1] + pb * pis[n - 1])
            d1.append(t1)
            d2.append(t2)
        out[f"s1_{key}_re"] = [v.real for v in d1]
        out[f"s1_{key}_im"] = [v.imag for v in d1]
        out[f"s2_{key}_re"] = [v.real for v in d2]
        out[f"s2_{key}_im"] = [v.imag for v in d2]
    return out


def _tight(outputs: dict[str, list], scales: dict[str, arb]) -> bool:
    """Whether every value's radius is at most 2^-REL_BITS of its scale (default: its array's
    largest midpoint)."""
    eps = arb(2) ** -REL_BITS
    for name, vs in outputs.items():
        scale = scales.get(name)
        if scale is None:
            scale = max((abs(v.mid()) for v in vs), default=arb(0))
        for v in vs:
            if not (v.is_finite() and v.rad() <= eps * max(abs(v.mid()), scale)):
                return False
    return True


def _coefficient_outputs(s: Solution, ns: Sequence[int], derivatives: bool) -> dict[str, list]:
    if derivatives:
        arrays = {"da_dx": s.da_dx, "da_dm": s.da_dm, "db_dx": s.db_dx, "db_dm": s.db_dm}
    else:
        arrays = {"a": s.a, "b": s.b}
    out: dict[str, list] = {}
    for name, vs in arrays.items():
        out[f"{name}_re"] = [vs[n - 1].real for n in ns]
        out[f"{name}_im"] = [vs[n - 1].imag for n in ns]
    # Real and imaginary parts share one scale: the largest modulus in the array.
    return out


def _array_scales(out: dict[str, list]) -> dict[str, arb]:
    scales: dict[str, arb] = {}
    for name in out:
        if name.endswith("_re"):
            stem = name[:-3]
            re, im = out[f"{stem}_re"], out[f"{stem}_im"]
            scale = max((abs(r.mid()) + abs(i.mid()) for r, i in zip(re, im, strict=True)))
            scales[f"{stem}_re"] = scales[f"{stem}_im"] = scale
    return scales


def n_ref(x: float, m_re: float, m_im: float) -> tuple[int, bool]:
    """(terms to sum, whether the tail bound can certify the series at that length)."""
    n_hp = size_terms(x, 18, 32)
    with ctx.workprec(PORTABLE_BITS):
        n_z = _ceil_abs(acb(m_re, m_im) * arb(x)) + 1
        n_x = int((3 * arb(x) / 2).ceil().unique_fmpz()) + 1
    n_cert = max(n_hp, n_z, n_x)
    return (n_cert, True) if n_cert <= CERTIFY_MAX_ORDER else (n_hp, False)


def case_name(x: float, m_re: float, m_im: float) -> str:
    return f"x={x!r},m=({m_re!r},{m_im!r})"


def _case_records(case: Case, derivatives: bool) -> list[dict]:
    x, m_re, m_im = case
    generator = "mie_derivatives" if derivatives else "mie"
    name = case_name(x, m_re, m_im)
    ns = orders(size_terms(x, 6, 3))
    mus = cosines()
    terms, certify = n_ref(x, m_re, m_im)
    prec = PRECISION_BITS
    while prec <= MAX_PRECISION_BITS:
        with ctx.workprec(prec):
            s = solve(x, m_re, m_im, terms)
            coefficients = _coefficient_outputs(s, ns, derivatives)
            sums = sum_series(s, mus)
            if derivatives:
                series, label = series_derivatives(s, sums), "high-precision"
                # A derivative of Q_abs or Q_back may be tiny or zero; measure it against the
                # derivatives of Q_ext and Q_sca.
                series_scales: dict[str, arb] = {}
                for p in ("dx", "dm_re", "dm_im"):
                    ext, sca = series[f"q_ext_{p}"][0].mid(), series[f"q_sca_{p}"][0].mid()
                    scale = max(abs(ext), abs(sca))
                    series_scales[f"q_abs_{p}"] = series_scales[f"q_back_{p}"] = scale
            else:
                tail = tail_bounds(s) if certify else None
                series = series_outputs(s, sums, tail)
                if series is None:
                    series = series_outputs(s, sums, None)
                    tail = None
                assert series is not None
                label = "certified" if tail is not None else "high-precision"
                q_ext, q_sca = series["q_ext"][0], series["q_sca"][0]
                s_rms = s.x * q_sca.sqrt() / 2
                series_scales = {"q_abs": abs(q_ext.mid()), "q_back": abs(q_sca.mid())}
                series_scales |= {k: abs(s_rms.mid()) for k in series if k.startswith("s")}
            if _tight(coefficients, _array_scales(coefficients)) and _tight(series, series_scales):
                inputs = {"x": x, "m_re": m_re, "m_im": m_im}
                return [
                    record(
                        generator, f"{name},coefficients", inputs | {"orders": ns}, coefficients
                    ),
                    record(
                        generator,
                        f"{name},series",
                        inputs | {"mu": mus, "n_ref": terms},
                        series,
                        label,
                    ),
                ]
        prec *= 2
    raise ArithmeticError(f"{name} needs more than {MAX_PRECISION_BITS} bits")


def value_records(case: Case) -> list[dict]:
    return _case_records(case, derivatives=False)


def derivative_records(case: Case) -> list[dict]:
    return _case_records(case, derivatives=True)


def cases() -> list[Case]:
    """The grid ``XS`` × ``MS`` and ``RANDOM_CASES`` random cases inside the target domain."""
    out = [(x, m_re, m_im) for x in XS for (m_re, m_im) in MS]
    rng = random.Random(SEED)
    for _ in range(RANDOM_CASES):
        x = nearest_pow10(rng.uniform(-6.0, 4.3))
        if rng.random() < 0.15:
            m_re, m_im = rng.uniform(0.05, 1.0), rng.uniform(1.0, 8.0)
        else:
            m_re = rng.uniform(1.0, 3.0)
            m_im = 0.0 if rng.random() < 0.2 else nearest_pow10(rng.uniform(-8.0, 0.5))
        out.append((x, m_re, m_im))
    return out


def derivative_cases() -> list[Case]:
    return [(x, m_re, m_im) for x in DERIVATIVE_XS for (m_re, m_im) in DERIVATIVE_MS]


def _map(fn: Callable[[Case], list[dict]], items: list[Case]) -> list[dict]:
    """``fn`` over ``items`` in worker processes, results in order.

    ``SCATRS_REFERENCE_WORKERS`` sets the number of processes (default: one per CPU; 1 runs
    in this process). The records do not depend on it.
    """
    workers = int(os.environ.get("SCATRS_REFERENCE_WORKERS", "0")) or os.cpu_count() or 1
    if workers == 1:
        results = [fn(c) for c in items]
    else:
        with ProcessPoolExecutor(max_workers=workers) as pool:
            results = list(pool.map(fn, items, chunksize=1))
    return [r for rs in results for r in rs]


def generate() -> tuple[int, list[dict]]:
    return PRECISION_BITS, _map(value_records, cases())


def generate_derivatives() -> tuple[int, list[dict]]:
    return PRECISION_BITS, _map(derivative_records, derivative_cases())
```

### Task 4: The Rust Mie solver

`scatrs::mie`: the workspace `Mie<T>`, options and input checks, the recurrences (theory chapter), the coefficients and their closed-form derivatives, the efficiencies, g and their Jacobian, and the amplitudes S₁, S₂ and their derivatives; a crate-private Smith reciprocal (`complex::inv`) validated against Arb. Correctness here is established without the tolerance model, which Task 5 adds: by Wiscombe's published tables, the Rayleigh limit, exact symmetries, the optical theorem, physical bounds, and the `Dual` oracle (the closed forms against derivatives propagated through the same recurrences, whose values are bitwise those of `f64`).

Key algorithm choices (measured while planning; see spec section 12):
- Truncation N = ⌈x + 6x^(1/3) + 3⌉ for `f64`, ⌈x + 12x^(1/3) + 6⌉ for `DoubleDouble` (Wiscombe's 4.05/2 left 10⁻⁶ errors in Q_back).
- Dₙ(mx) by the downward recurrence of uₖ from uₖ₊₁ = 0 at max(N + 1, ⌈1.5|mx|⌉) plus a margin: no continued fraction, no convergence test.
- ψₙ, χₙ upward for n ≤ ⌊x⌋; above, the ratios Tₙ = ψₙ/ξₙ, 1/ξₙ, Rₙ = ξₙ₋₁/ξₙ, with numerators free of the cancelling n/x terms.
- Q_abs from Re aₙ − |aₙ|² = −Im(Dₙ/m)/|Δₐ|²; Q_ext = Q_sca + Q_abs.
- D + zD′ = (2n + 1)uₙ₊₁ − z(1 + uₙ₊₁²) at small z.

**Files:**
- Create: `scatrs/src/complex.rs`, `scatrs/src/mie/{mod,kernel,efficiencies,amplitudes,domain}.rs`
- Modify: `scatrs/src/lib.rs`, `scatrs/tests/common/mod.rs`
- Test: `scatrs/tests/common/mie.rs`, `scatrs/tests/mie.rs`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `scatrs::Real`, `scatrs::scalar::{DoubleDouble, Dual}`, `crate::test_data::reference_file` (unit tests).
- Produces (public, `scatrs::mie`):
  - `const MAX_MIE_ORDER: usize = 1 << 20`, `MIN_SIZE_PARAMETER: f64 = 1e-30`, `MAX_SIZE_PARAMETER: f64 = 1e6`, `MIN_REFRACTIVE_INDEX: f64 = 1e-6`, `MAX_REFRACTIVE_INDEX: f64 = 1e3`; `fn default_n_max(x: f64) -> usize`.
  - `struct MieOptions { pub n_max: Option<usize>, pub allow_unvalidated: bool }` (`#[non_exhaustive]`, `Default`), `MieOptions::new()`, `.with_n_max(usize)`, `.with_allow_unvalidated(bool)`.
  - `struct Mie<T: Real>`: `new()`, `compute(&mut self, x: T, m: Complex<T>, &MieOptions) -> Result<MieSolution<'_, T>>`, `compute_with_derivatives(…)`.
  - `struct MieSolution<'a, T>`: `x()`, `m()`, `n_terms()`, `a() -> &'a [Complex<T>]`, `b()`, `derivatives() -> Option<CoefficientDerivatives<'a, T>>` (fields `da_dx`, `da_dm`, `db_dx`, `db_dm`), `efficiencies() -> Efficiencies<T>` (fields `q_ext`, `q_sca`, `q_abs`, `q_back`, `g`), `efficiencies_jacobian() -> Option<EfficiencyJacobian<T>>` (fields `x`, `m_re`, `m_im`, each an `Efficiencies<T>`), `amplitudes(&self, mu: &[T], AmplitudeBuffers<'_, T>) -> Result<()>`, `amplitudes_with_derivatives(&self, mu, values, d_dx, d_dm: AmplitudeBuffers) -> Result<()>`.
  - `struct AmplitudeBuffers<'b, T> { pub s1: &'b mut [Complex<T>], pub s2: &'b mut [Complex<T>] }`.
  - `struct ValidatedDomain { pub x_min, x_max, m_re_max, m_im_max: f64 }` (`#[non_exhaustive]`), `const VALIDATED_DOMAIN`, `ValidatedDomain::contains(&self, x, m: Complex<f64>) -> bool`, `ValidatedDomain::condition_factor(x, m) -> f64` (κ = (1 + |m|x)·max(1, |m|/|m − 1|)), `Display`.
  - Test helpers (`scatrs/tests/common/mie.rs`): `Outputs<T>` (fields `a`, `b`, `d: [Vec; 4]` = ∂a/∂x, da/dm, ∂b/∂x, db/dm, `q`, `jac: [Efficiencies; 3]`, `s: [Vec; 6]` = S₁, S₂, ∂S₁/∂x, ∂S₂/∂x, dS₁/dm, dS₂/dm), `solve<T: Real>(x, m, mu: &[T], n_max: Option<usize>) -> Outputs<T>` (always `allow_unvalidated`), `q_of<T: Copy>(&Efficiencies<T>, k) -> T`, `EFFICIENCY_NAMES`.

Create `scatrs/tests/common/mie.rs`:

```rust
//! Shared by the Mie tests: solving one sphere in any scalar type.

use num_complex::Complex;
use scatrs::Real;
use scatrs::mie::{AmplitudeBuffers, Efficiencies, Mie, MieOptions};

/// Everything the Mie tests compare, from one solution.
#[derive(Clone, Debug)]
pub struct Outputs<T> {
    pub a: Vec<Complex<T>>,
    pub b: Vec<Complex<T>>,
    /// ∂a/∂x, da/dm, ∂b/∂x, db/dm.
    pub d: [Vec<Complex<T>>; 4],
    pub q: Efficiencies<T>,
    /// ∂/∂x, ∂/∂Re m, ∂/∂Im m.
    pub jac: [Efficiencies<T>; 3],
    /// S₁, S₂, ∂S₁/∂x, ∂S₂/∂x, dS₁/dm, dS₂/dm at each cosine.
    pub s: [Vec<Complex<T>>; 6],
}

/// Solves (x, m) with derivatives and amplitudes at `mu`, inside or outside the domain.
pub fn solve<T: Real>(x: T, m: Complex<T>, mu: &[T], n_max: Option<usize>) -> Outputs<T> {
    let mut opts = MieOptions::new().with_allow_unvalidated(true);
    if let Some(n) = n_max {
        opts = opts.with_n_max(n);
    }
    let mut mie = Mie::<T>::new();
    let sol = mie
        .compute_with_derivatives(x, m, &opts)
        .expect("valid inputs");
    let d = sol.derivatives().expect("computed");
    let jac = sol.efficiencies_jacobian().expect("computed");
    let zero = Complex::new(T::zero(), T::zero());
    let mut s: [Vec<Complex<T>>; 6] = std::array::from_fn(|_| vec![zero; mu.len()]);
    {
        let [s1, s2, dx1, dx2, dm1, dm2] = &mut s;
        sol.amplitudes_with_derivatives(
            mu,
            AmplitudeBuffers { s1, s2 },
            AmplitudeBuffers { s1: dx1, s2: dx2 },
            AmplitudeBuffers { s1: dm1, s2: dm2 },
        )
        .expect("valid cosines");
    }
    Outputs {
        a: sol.a().to_vec(),
        b: sol.b().to_vec(),
        d: [
            d.da_dx.to_vec(),
            d.da_dm.to_vec(),
            d.db_dx.to_vec(),
            d.db_dm.to_vec(),
        ],
        q: sol.efficiencies(),
        jac: [jac.x, jac.m_re, jac.m_im],
        s,
    }
}

/// Efficiency k of `e`: Q_ext, Q_sca, Q_abs, Q_back, g.
pub fn q_of<T: Copy>(e: &Efficiencies<T>, k: usize) -> T {
    [e.q_ext, e.q_sca, e.q_abs, e.q_back, e.g][k]
}

pub const EFFICIENCY_NAMES: [&str; 5] = ["q_ext", "q_sca", "q_abs", "q_back", "g"];
```

Create `scatrs/tests/mie.rs`:

```rust
//! Properties of the Mie solutions that need no reference data: input checks, published
//! tables, invariants, the `Dual` derivative oracle, determinism and robustness.
#![allow(clippy::needless_range_loop)] // parallel arrays are indexed together

mod common;

use common::mie::{q_of, solve};
use num_complex::Complex;
use proptest::prelude::*;
use scatrs::Error;
use scatrs::mie::{
    AmplitudeBuffers, MAX_MIE_ORDER, Mie, MieOptions, VALIDATED_DOMAIN, ValidatedDomain,
    default_n_max,
};
use scatrs::scalar::Dual;

const EPS: f64 = f64::EPSILON;

fn c(re: f64, im: f64) -> Complex<f64> {
    Complex::new(re, im)
}

#[test]
fn default_truncation() {
    assert_eq!(default_n_max(1e-6), 4); // 3.06
    assert_eq!(default_n_max(0.1), 6); // 5.785
    assert_eq!(default_n_max(1.0), 10); // exactly 10
    assert_eq!(default_n_max(1000.0), 1063); // exactly 1063
    assert_eq!(default_n_max(2e4), 20166); // 20000 + 162.86 + 3
}

#[test]
fn invalid_inputs_are_rejected() {
    let mut mie = Mie::<f64>::new();
    let opts = MieOptions::new().with_allow_unvalidated(true);
    let invalid = |r: Result<_, Error>| matches!(r, Err(Error::InvalidInput(_)));
    for x in [0.0, -1.0, f64::NAN, f64::INFINITY, 1e-31, 1.1e6] {
        assert!(
            invalid(mie.compute(x, c(1.5, 0.0), &opts).map(|_| ())),
            "x = {x}"
        );
    }
    for m in [
        c(f64::NAN, 0.0),
        c(1.5, f64::INFINITY),
        c(-1.5, 0.1),
        c(1.5, -1e-9),
        c(0.0, 0.0),
        c(1e-7, 0.0),
        c(2e3, 0.0),
    ] {
        assert!(invalid(mie.compute(1.0, m, &opts).map(|_| ())), "m = {m}");
    }
    for n in [0, MAX_MIE_ORDER + 1] {
        let opts = opts.with_n_max(n);
        assert!(
            invalid(mie.compute(1.0, c(1.5, 0.0), &opts).map(|_| ())),
            "n_max = {n}"
        );
    }
}

#[test]
fn the_validated_domain_is_enforced_unless_allowed() {
    let mut mie = Mie::<f64>::new();
    let outside = |r: Result<_, Error>| matches!(r, Err(Error::OutsideValidatedDomain(_)));
    let strict = MieOptions::new();
    let lenient = MieOptions::new().with_allow_unvalidated(true);
    for (x, m) in [
        (2.0001e4, c(1.5, 0.0)),
        (9e-7, c(1.5, 0.0)),
        (1.0, c(10.5, 0.0)),
        (1.0, c(1.5, 10.5)),
        (1.0, c(0.0, 2.0)),
    ] {
        assert!(!VALIDATED_DOMAIN.contains(x, m));
        assert!(
            outside(mie.compute(x, m, &strict).map(|_| ())),
            "x = {x}, m = {m}"
        );
        assert!(mie.compute(x, m, &lenient).is_ok(), "x = {x}, m = {m}");
    }
    // Fewer terms than the default.
    let few = MieOptions::new().with_n_max(default_n_max(10.0) - 1);
    assert!(outside(mie.compute(10.0, c(1.5, 0.0), &few).map(|_| ())));
    assert!(
        mie.compute(10.0, c(1.5, 0.0), &few.with_allow_unvalidated(true))
            .is_ok()
    );
    // The corners are inside.
    for (x, m) in [
        (1e-6, c(10.0, 10.0)),
        (2e4, c(1e-3, 0.0)),
        (1.0, c(1.0, 0.0)),
    ] {
        assert!(mie.compute(x, m, &strict).is_ok(), "x = {x}, m = {m}");
    }
}

/// Wiscombe (1979), *Mie scattering calculations: advances in technique and fast,
/// vector-speed computer codes*, NCAR/TN-140+STR, test case of x = 1000, m = 0.75: Q_sca and
/// S₁, S₂ at Θ = 0°, 30°, …, 180°, to the 7 digits printed. MIEV0 uses e^(+iωt), so its S are
/// the complex conjugates of ours.
#[test]
fn wiscombe_published_values() {
    let mut mie = Mie::<f64>::new();
    let s = mie
        .compute(1000.0, c(0.75, 0.0), &MieOptions::new())
        .unwrap();
    assert!((s.efficiencies().q_sca - 1.997908).abs() <= 1e-6);
    let table = [
        (c(4.994770e5, -1.336502e4), c(4.994770e5, -1.336502e4)),
        (c(-3.999296e2, -3.316361e2), c(-3.946018e2, -1.147791e2)),
        (c(-5.209852e2, -5.776614e2), c(-1.970767e2, -6.937470e2)),
        (c(-1.600887e2, 1.348013e2), c(-4.152365e1, 1.143000e2)),
        (c(8.431720e1, -1.209493e2), c(-4.261732e1, 5.535055e1)),
        (c(-7.556092e1, -8.134810e1), c(4.218303e1, 9.100831e1)),
        (c(1.705778e1, 4.842510e2), c(-1.705778e1, -4.842510e2)),
    ];
    let mu: Vec<f64> = (0..7)
        .map(|k| (f64::from(k) * std::f64::consts::PI / 6.0).cos())
        .collect();
    let mut s1 = vec![c(0.0, 0.0); 7];
    let mut s2 = s1.clone();
    s.amplitudes(
        &mu,
        AmplitudeBuffers {
            s1: &mut s1,
            s2: &mut s2,
        },
    )
    .unwrap();
    for (k, (t1, t2)) in table.into_iter().enumerate() {
        for (got, want) in [(s1[k], t1.conj()), (s2[k], t2.conj())] {
            assert!(
                (got - want).norm() <= 1e-6 * want.norm(),
                "angle {}: {got} vs {want}",
                30 * k
            );
        }
    }
}

#[test]
fn rayleigh_limit() {
    // Q_sca = (8/3) x⁴ |(m² − 1)/(m² + 2)|² (1 + O(x²)).
    let mut mie = Mie::<f64>::new();
    for m in [c(1.33, 0.0), c(1.5, 0.1), c(10.0, 10.0)] {
        let x = 1e-4;
        let q = mie
            .compute(x, m, &MieOptions::new())
            .unwrap()
            .efficiencies();
        let k = (m * m - 1.0) / (m * m + 2.0);
        let rayleigh = 8.0 / 3.0 * x.powi(4) * k.norm_sqr();
        assert!((q.q_sca / rayleigh - 1.0).abs() <= 1e-6, "m = {m}");
    }
}

#[test]
fn derivatives_need_compute_with_derivatives() {
    let mut mie = Mie::<f64>::new();
    let s = mie.compute(5.0, c(1.5, 0.1), &MieOptions::new()).unwrap();
    assert!(s.derivatives().is_none());
    assert!(s.efficiencies_jacobian().is_none());
    let mut v = [c(0.0, 0.0); 6];
    let [a, b, d, e, f, g] = &mut v[..] else {
        unreachable!()
    };
    let r = s.amplitudes_with_derivatives(
        &[0.5],
        AmplitudeBuffers {
            s1: std::slice::from_mut(a),
            s2: std::slice::from_mut(b),
        },
        AmplitudeBuffers {
            s1: std::slice::from_mut(d),
            s2: std::slice::from_mut(e),
        },
        AmplitudeBuffers {
            s1: std::slice::from_mut(f),
            s2: std::slice::from_mut(g),
        },
    );
    assert!(matches!(r, Err(Error::InvalidInput(_))));
}

#[test]
fn bad_cosines_and_buffers_are_rejected_without_writing() {
    let mut mie = Mie::<f64>::new();
    let s = mie.compute(5.0, c(1.5, 0.1), &MieOptions::new()).unwrap();
    let marker = c(7.0, 7.0);
    for mu in [vec![0.5, 1.5], vec![f64::NAN], vec![-1.0 - 1e-15]] {
        let (mut s1, mut s2) = (vec![marker; mu.len()], vec![marker; mu.len()]);
        let r = s.amplitudes(
            &mu,
            AmplitudeBuffers {
                s1: &mut s1,
                s2: &mut s2,
            },
        );
        assert!(matches!(r, Err(Error::InvalidInput(_))), "{mu:?}");
        assert!(s1.iter().chain(&s2).all(|&v| v == marker));
    }
    let (mut s1, mut s2) = (vec![marker; 2], vec![marker; 3]);
    let r = s.amplitudes(
        &[0.1, 0.2],
        AmplitudeBuffers {
            s1: &mut s1,
            s2: &mut s2,
        },
    );
    assert!(matches!(r, Err(Error::InvalidInput(_))));
}

/// Forward and backward symmetries hold exactly: πₙ(±1) = ±τₙ(±1) in exact integers.
#[test]
fn forward_and_backward_amplitudes() {
    let mut mie = Mie::<f64>::new();
    for (x, m) in [
        (0.3, c(1.33, 0.0)),
        (25.0, c(1.5, 0.1)),
        (700.0, c(0.2, 3.0)),
    ] {
        let s = mie.compute(x, m, &MieOptions::new()).unwrap();
        let (mut s1, mut s2) = (vec![c(0.0, 0.0); 2], vec![c(0.0, 0.0); 2]);
        s.amplitudes(
            &[1.0, -1.0],
            AmplitudeBuffers {
                s1: &mut s1,
                s2: &mut s2,
            },
        )
        .unwrap();
        assert_eq!(s1[0], s2[0]);
        assert_eq!(s1[1], -s2[1]);
        // Optical theorem: Q_ext = (4/x²) Re S(0).
        let q = s.efficiencies();
        let kappa = ValidatedDomain::condition_factor(x, m);
        assert!((4.0 / (x * x) * s1[0].re / q.q_ext - 1.0).abs() <= 64.0 * kappa * EPS);
        // Q_back = 4|S₁(π)|²/x².
        assert!((4.0 * s1[1].norm_sqr() / (x * x) / q.q_back - 1.0).abs() <= 64.0 * kappa * EPS);
    }
}

#[test]
fn workspace_reuse_is_bitwise_deterministic() {
    let mut mie = Mie::<f64>::new();
    let first: Vec<_> = mie
        .compute_with_derivatives(300.0, c(1.5, 0.01), &MieOptions::new())
        .unwrap()
        .a()
        .to_vec();
    mie.compute(2.0, c(4.0, 0.1), &MieOptions::new()).unwrap();
    mie.compute(5000.0, c(1.33, 0.0), &MieOptions::new())
        .unwrap();
    let again = mie
        .compute_with_derivatives(300.0, c(1.5, 0.01), &MieOptions::new())
        .unwrap()
        .a()
        .to_vec();
    assert_eq!(first, again);
    let fresh = Mie::<f64>::new()
        .compute(300.0, c(1.5, 0.01), &MieOptions::new())
        .unwrap()
        .a()
        .to_vec();
    assert_eq!(first, fresh);
}

type D3 = Dual<f64, 3>;

/// The closed-form derivatives against `Dual<f64, 3>` (seeds x, Re m, Im m) propagated
/// through the same recurrences: the values agree bitwise, and the derivatives within
/// 64·κ′·2⁻⁵² of their array's (or the efficiency's natural) scale, with κ′ the a-priori
/// factor of the derivative tolerances (`ValidatedDomain`): both carry rounding errors of
/// that size, from different formulas.
#[test]
fn closed_form_derivatives_agree_with_dual() {
    let mu = [1.0, 0.6, 0.0, -0.3, -1.0];
    for (x, m) in [
        (1e-6, c(1.33, 1e-8)),
        (0.5, c(1.5, 0.01)),
        (3.0, c(1.0001, 0.0)),
        (10.0, c(2.5, 1.0)),
        (40.0, c(1.75, 0.44)),
        (150.0, c(1.33, 0.0)),
        (25.0, c(0.2, 3.0)),
        (300.0, c(4.0, 0.01)),
        (1000.0, c(1.5, 5.0)),
        (3000.0, c(10.0, 10.0)),
    ] {
        let f = solve(x, m, &mu, None);
        let near_one = (m.norm() / (m - 1.0).norm()).max(1.0);
        let bound = 64.0 * ValidatedDomain::condition_factor(x, m) * near_one * EPS;
        let xd = D3::new(x, [1.0, 0.0, 0.0]);
        let md = Complex::new(
            D3::new(m.re, [0.0, 1.0, 0.0]),
            D3::new(m.im, [0.0, 0.0, 1.0]),
        );
        let mu_d: Vec<D3> = mu.iter().map(|&v| D3::constant(v)).collect();
        let d = solve(xd, md, &mu_d, None);
        let value = |z: Complex<D3>| c(z.re.re(), z.im.re());
        let deriv = |z: Complex<D3>, k: usize| c(z.re.eps()[k], z.im.eps()[k]);
        let i1 = c(0.0, 1.0);
        // Coefficients: ∂/∂x, ∂/∂Re m = d/dm, ∂/∂Im m = i d/dm.
        for (vals, dx, dm) in [(&d.a, &f.d[0], &f.d[1]), (&d.b, &f.d[2], &f.d[3])] {
            for (k, analytic) in [
                (0, dx.clone()),
                (1, dm.clone()),
                (2, dm.iter().map(|&v| i1 * v).collect()),
            ] {
                let scale = analytic
                    .iter()
                    .map(|v: &Complex<f64>| v.norm())
                    .fold(0.0, f64::max);
                for (n, z) in vals.iter().enumerate() {
                    let err = (deriv(*z, k) - analytic[n]).norm();
                    assert!(
                        err <= bound * scale,
                        "x = {x}, m = {m}, n = {}, seed {k}: {err:e}",
                        n + 1
                    );
                }
            }
        }
        for (n, z) in d.a.iter().enumerate() {
            assert_eq!(
                value(*z),
                f.a[n],
                "values differ: x = {x}, m = {m}, n = {}",
                n + 1
            );
        }
        // Efficiencies: the Jacobian against the derivative parts.
        let sizes = [x, m.norm(), m.norm()];
        for k in 0..5 {
            let q = q_of(&d.q, k);
            assert_eq!(q.re(), q_of(&f.q, k));
            for p in 0..3 {
                let analytic = q_of(&f.jac[p], k);
                let natural =
                    q_of(&f.q, 0).abs().max(q.re().abs()) * (1.0 + m.norm() * x) / sizes[p];
                let err = (q.eps()[p] - analytic).abs();
                assert!(
                    err <= bound * analytic.abs().max(natural),
                    "x = {x}, m = {m}, Q{k} p{p}: {err:e}"
                );
            }
        }
        // Amplitudes: ∂S/∂x and dS/dm.
        for j in 0..mu.len() {
            for i in 0..2 {
                let z = d.s[i][j];
                for (k, analytic) in [(0, f.s[2 + i][j]), (1, f.s[4 + i][j])] {
                    let scale = analytic.norm().max(f.s[i][j].norm());
                    let err = (deriv(z, k) - analytic).norm();
                    assert!(
                        err <= bound * scale,
                        "x = {x}, m = {m}, S{} mu {}: {err:e}",
                        i + 1,
                        mu[j]
                    );
                }
            }
        }
    }
}

proptest! {
    #![proptest_config(ProptestConfig::with_cases(256))]

    /// Inside the domain: Q_abs ≥ 0 (exactly 0 for real m), Q_sca ≤ Q_ext, |g| ≤ 1, and
    /// |aₙ|² ≤ Re aₙ up to rounding (passive media).
    #[test]
    fn physical_bounds(log_x in -6.0f64..4.3, re in 0.05f64..10.0, log_im in -10.0f64..1.0, real in proptest::bool::ANY) {
        let x = 10f64.powf(log_x).min(2e4);
        let m = c(re, if real { 0.0 } else { 10f64.powf(log_im) });
        let mut mie = Mie::<f64>::new();
        let s = mie.compute(x, m, &MieOptions::new()).unwrap();
        let q = s.efficiencies();
        if real {
            prop_assert_eq!(q.q_abs, 0.0);
        } else {
            prop_assert!(q.q_abs >= -1e-14 * q.q_ext);
        }
        prop_assert!(q.q_sca <= q.q_ext * (1.0 + 1e-14) && q.q_sca > 0.0);
        prop_assert!(q.g.abs() <= 1.0 + 1e-12);
        let scale = s.a().iter().map(|a| a.norm()).fold(0.0, f64::max);
        let kappa = ValidatedDomain::condition_factor(x, m);
        for a in s.a().iter().chain(s.b()) {
            prop_assert!(a.norm_sqr() <= a.re + 64.0 * kappa * EPS * scale);
        }
    }

    /// Over the whole accepted input range, outside the domain too: every output finite.
    #[test]
    fn no_nan_or_infinity_over_the_supported_inputs(
        log_x in -30.0f64..5.0,
        log_abs_m in -6.0f64..3.0,
        angle in 0.0f64..std::f64::consts::FRAC_PI_2,
    ) {
        let x = 10f64.powf(log_x);
        let r = 10f64.powf(log_abs_m);
        let m = c(r * angle.cos(), r * angle.sin());
        prop_assume!(m.norm() * x <= 1e6); // keep the cost of Dₙ(mx) bounded
        let opts = MieOptions::new().with_allow_unvalidated(true);
        let mut mie = Mie::<f64>::new();
        let s = mie.compute_with_derivatives(x, m, &opts).unwrap();
        let q = s.efficiencies();
        let j = s.efficiencies_jacobian().unwrap();
        for e in [q, j.x, j.m_re, j.m_im] {
            for k in 0..5 {
                prop_assert!(q_of(&e, k).is_finite(), "x = {x:e}, m = {m}: Q{k} = {}", q_of(&e, k));
            }
        }
        let mu = [1.0, 0.3, -1.0];
        let zero = c(0.0, 0.0);
        let mut v: [Vec<Complex<f64>>; 6] = std::array::from_fn(|_| vec![zero; 3]);
        let [s1, s2, dx1, dx2, dm1, dm2] = &mut v;
        s.amplitudes_with_derivatives(
            &mu,
            AmplitudeBuffers { s1, s2 },
            AmplitudeBuffers { s1: dx1, s2: dx2 },
            AmplitudeBuffers { s1: dm1, s2: dm2 },
        ).unwrap();
        for z in v.iter().flatten() {
            prop_assert!(z.re.is_finite() && z.im.is_finite(), "x = {x:e}, m = {m}");
        }
    }
}
```
