- [ ] **Step 4: Write the complex reciprocal** (with its unit tests against Arb's quotients in `dd_complex.jsonl`)

Create `scatrs/src/complex.rs`:

```rust
//! Complex reciprocal for the scattering kernels.
//!
//! num-complex computes 1/(c + id) as (c − id)/(c² + d²). That overflows once |c| or |d|
//! exceeds about 10¹⁵⁴ (in `f64` and `DoubleDouble` alike) and underflows below about
//! 10⁻¹⁵⁴. Smith's algorithm (Smith 1962) divides by the larger of c and d first, so the
//! intermediate values stay near the size of the result.

use num_complex::Complex;

use crate::scalar::Real;

/// 1/z by Smith's algorithm: correct to a few ulps of |1/z| wherever 1/z is a normal number.
///
/// Infinite or NaN for z = 0, like `1.0 / 0.0`.
#[inline]
pub(crate) fn inv<T: Real>(z: Complex<T>) -> Complex<T> {
    let (c, d) = (z.re, z.im);
    if d.abs() <= c.abs() {
        let r = d / c;
        let t = (c + d * r).recip();
        Complex::new(t, -(r * t))
    } else {
        let r = c / d;
        let t = (c * r + d).recip();
        Complex::new(r * t, -t)
    }
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;
    use crate::scalar::DoubleDouble as Dd;

    fn c(re: f64, im: f64) -> Complex<f64> {
        Complex::new(re, im)
    }

    /// |got − want| / |want|.
    fn rel(got: Complex<f64>, want: Complex<f64>) -> f64 {
        (got - want).norm() / want.norm()
    }

    #[test]
    fn agrees_with_num_complex_on_ordinary_values() {
        for z in [
            c(3.0, -4.0),
            c(0.125, 8.0),
            c(0.0, -2.0),
            c(1.0, 1.0),
            c(-7.5, 0.25),
        ] {
            assert!(rel(inv(z), z.inv()) <= 2.0 * f64::EPSILON, "{z}");
        }
    }

    #[test]
    fn exact_cases() {
        assert_eq!(inv(c(2.0, 0.0)), c(0.5, -0.0));
        assert_eq!(inv(c(0.0, 4.0)), c(0.0, -0.25));
        assert_eq!(inv(c(-4.0, 0.0)), c(-0.25, 0.0));
    }

    #[test]
    fn no_overflow_or_underflow_where_num_complex_fails() {
        // c² + d² is 25·10⁶⁰⁰ or 25·10⁻⁶⁰⁰, outside the f64 range.
        for scale in [1e300, 1e-300] {
            let z = c(3.0 * scale, 4.0 * scale);
            let want = c(3.0 / 25.0 / scale, -4.0 / 25.0 / scale);
            assert!(rel(inv(z), want) <= 4.0 * f64::EPSILON, "scale {scale:e}");
            assert!(!rel(z.inv(), want).is_finite() || rel(z.inv(), want) > 0.5);
        }
    }

    #[test]
    fn zero_gives_infinity_or_nan_not_a_panic() {
        let w = inv(c(0.0, 0.0));
        assert!(!w.re.is_finite() || !w.im.is_finite());
    }

    /// `n · inv(d)` for `Complex<DoubleDouble>` against the Arb quotients n/d in
    /// `dd_complex.jsonl` (200 random pairs with moduli between 10⁻³⁰ and 10³⁰): within 8u²
    /// of |n/d|, including the rounding of the product.
    #[test]
    fn double_double_reciprocal_matches_arb() {
        let Some(text) = crate::test_data::reference_file("dd_complex.jsonl") else {
            return;
        };
        let rec: serde_json::Value = text
            .lines()
            .map(|l| serde_json::from_str::<serde_json::Value>(l).expect("valid record"))
            .find(|r| r["case"] == "div")
            .expect("a div record");
        let get = |v: &serde_json::Value| -> Vec<f64> {
            v.as_array()
                .expect("array")
                .iter()
                .map(|x| x.as_f64().expect("number"))
                .collect()
        };
        let input = |key: &str| get(&rec["inputs"][key]);
        let output = |key: &str, part: &str| get(&rec["outputs"][key][part]);
        let dd = |hi: &[f64], lo: &[f64], i: usize| Dd::new(hi[i], lo[i]);
        let (re_hi, re_lo, im_hi, im_lo) = (
            input("re_hi"),
            input("re_lo"),
            input("im_hi"),
            input("im_lo"),
        );
        let (y_re_hi, y_re_lo, y_im_hi, y_im_lo) = (
            input("y_re_hi"),
            input("y_re_lo"),
            input("y_im_hi"),
            input("y_im_lo"),
        );
        let (q_re_hi, q_re_lo) = (output("re", "hi"), output("re", "lo"));
        let (q_im_hi, q_im_lo) = (output("im", "hi"), output("im", "lo"));
        let u2 = 2f64.powi(-104);
        let mut worst = 0.0f64;
        for i in 0..re_hi.len() {
            let n = Complex::new(dd(&re_hi, &re_lo, i), dd(&im_hi, &im_lo, i));
            let d = Complex::new(dd(&y_re_hi, &y_re_lo, i), dd(&y_im_hi, &y_im_lo, i));
            let want = Complex::new(dd(&q_re_hi, &q_re_lo, i), dd(&q_im_hi, &q_im_lo, i));
            let err = ((n * inv(d) - want).norm() / want.norm()).hi();
            worst = worst.max(err / u2);
            assert!(err <= 8.0 * u2, "case {i}: error {:.2} u²", err / u2);
        }
        eprintln!("Complex<DoubleDouble> n·inv(d): worst error {worst:.2} u² of |n/d|");
    }
}
```

- [ ] **Step 5: Write the public API**

Create `scatrs/src/mie/mod.rs`:

````rust
//! Lorenz–Mie scattering by homogeneous spheres.
//!
//! [`Mie`] is a reusable workspace: [`Mie::compute`] (or [`Mie::compute_with_derivatives`])
//! solves one sphere and returns a [`MieSolution`], from which the coefficients aₙ, bₙ, the
//! [`Efficiencies`], their [`EfficiencyJacobian`] and the amplitudes S₁, S₂ are read.
//!
//! ```
//! use num_complex::Complex;
//! use scatrs::mie::{Mie, MieOptions};
//!
//! let mut mie = Mie::<f64>::new();
//! let sphere = mie.compute(10.0, Complex::new(1.33, 1e-8), &MieOptions::new())?;
//! let q = sphere.efficiencies();
//! assert!((q.q_ext - (q.q_sca + q.q_abs)).abs() <= 1e-15 * q.q_ext);
//! # Ok::<(), scatrs::Error>(())
//! ```
//!
//! # Conventions
//!
//! Bohren & Huffman (1983), with time dependence e^(−iωt): the refractive index m = n + ik has
//! k ≥ 0 for absorbing spheres, x = 2πr/λ is the size parameter, and
//!
//! aₙ = [(Dₙ(mx)/m + n/x) ψₙ(x) − ψₙ₋₁(x)] / [(Dₙ(mx)/m + n/x) ξₙ(x) − ξₙ₋₁(x)],
//! bₙ = [(m Dₙ(mx) + n/x) ψₙ(x) − ψₙ₋₁(x)] / [(m Dₙ(mx) + n/x) ξₙ(x) − ξₙ₋₁(x)],
//!
//! with ψₙ(ρ) = ρ jₙ(ρ), ξₙ = ψₙ − iχₙ, χₙ(ρ) = −ρ yₙ(ρ) and Dₙ = ψₙ′/ψₙ. Codes using
//! e^(+iωt), such as Wiscombe's MIEV0, report the complex conjugates of aₙ, bₙ, S₁ and S₂.
//!
//! # Method
//!
//! See the theory chapter of the documentation, "Homogeneous spheres", for the derivations.
//!
//! * **Truncation.** N = ⌈x + 6x^(1/3) + 3⌉ terms in `f64` ([`default_n_max`]), and
//!   ⌈x + 12x^(1/3) + 6⌉ in `DoubleDouble`, beyond Wiscombe's ⌈x + 4.05x^(1/3) + 2⌉, which
//!   leaves truncation errors up to 10⁻⁶ in backscattering.
//! * **Dₙ(mx)** by the downward recurrence of uₖ = ψₖ/ψₖ₋₁ = 1/((2k + 1)/(mx) − uₖ₊₁), from
//!   uₖ₊₁ = 0 at k = max(N + 1, ⌈1.5|mx|⌉) plus a margin of about half the bits of precision.
//!   Above 1.5|mx| every step shrinks an error at least sixfold, so the start value is
//!   forgotten to rounding level. Then Dₙ = (n + 1)/(mx) − uₙ₊₁. No continued fraction is
//!   evaluated, and nothing can fail to converge.
//! * **ψₙ and ξₙ.** Up to n = ⌊x⌋ (for x ≥ 1) by upward recurrence; above, through the ratios
//!   Tₙ = ψₙ/ξₙ and 1/ξₙ, built from uₙ(x) (downward, as for Dₙ) and Rₙ = ξₙ₋₁/ξₙ (upward).
//!   The upward ψ recurrence loses about log₁₀(1/x²) digits at small x (ψ₁ = sin x/x − cos x
//!   cancels), and ψₙ underflows while ξₙ overflows as n grows beyond x; the ratios do
//!   neither, so no small-particle expansion is needed and any `n_max` is safe.
//! * **Numerators without cancellation.** In the ratio region, Dₙ(mx)/m − Dₙ(x) =
//!   (n + 1)(1/m² − 1)/x + uₙ₊₁(x) − uₙ₊₁(mx)/m and m·Dₙ(mx) − Dₙ(x) = uₙ₊₁(x) − m·uₙ₊₁(mx):
//!   the terms of size n/x that cancel are removed exactly.
//! * **Absorption.** Re aₙ − |aₙ|² = −Im(Dₙ(mx)/m)/|Δₐ|² and Re bₙ − |bₙ|² =
//!   −Im(m Dₙ(mx))/|Δ_b|², where Δ are the denominators above; Q_abs is summed from these
//!   terms, without the cancellation of Q_ext − Q_sca, and Q_ext = Q_sca + Q_abs.
//! * **Derivatives** in closed form (each a few operations per order):
//!   ∂aₙ/∂m = i(D − zD′)/(m²Δₐ²), ∂bₙ/∂m = −i(D + zD′)/Δ_b²,
//!   ∂aₙ/∂x = i(m² − 1)(n(n + 1)/x² + D²)/(m²Δₐ²), ∂bₙ/∂x = i(m² − 1)/Δ_b²,
//!   with z = mx, D = Dₙ(z) and D′ = n(n + 1)/z² − 1 − D². aₙ and bₙ are holomorphic in m,
//!   so ∂/∂(Re m) = d/dm and ∂/∂(Im m) = i·d/dm.
//!
//! # Accuracy
//!
//! [`VALIDATED_DOMAIN`] is the domain where the accuracy is validated.
//!
//! # Generic scalars
//!
//! Every method is generic over [`Real`]: `f64`, `DoubleDouble` and `Dual<T, N>`. With
//! `Dual`, the derivatives are those of the computed values with respect to whatever the
//! caller seeds in x and m, and the values are bitwise those of the underlying type: the
//! derivative oracle for the closed forms.

mod amplitudes;
mod domain;
mod efficiencies;
mod kernel;

use num_complex::Complex;

use crate::scalar::Real;
use crate::{Error, Result};

pub use amplitudes::AmplitudeBuffers;
pub use domain::{VALIDATED_DOMAIN, ValidatedDomain};
pub use efficiencies::{Efficiencies, EfficiencyJacobian};

/// The largest number of terms [`Mie::compute`] accepts, 2²⁰.
///
/// It bounds the workspace to about 200 MB with derivatives. Larger `n_max`, or a size
/// parameter whose default truncation exceeds it, raise [`Error::InvalidInput`].
pub const MAX_MIE_ORDER: usize = 1 << 20;
/// The smallest size parameter accepted. Below it the coefficients (∝ x³) and efficiencies
/// (∝ x⁴) leave the normal `f64` range.
pub const MIN_SIZE_PARAMETER: f64 = 1e-30;
/// The largest size parameter accepted (its default truncation is below [`MAX_MIE_ORDER`]).
pub const MAX_SIZE_PARAMETER: f64 = 1e6;
/// The smallest |m| accepted.
pub const MIN_REFRACTIVE_INDEX: f64 = 1e-6;
/// The largest |m| accepted. The cost of Dₙ(mx) grows like |m|x.
pub const MAX_REFRACTIVE_INDEX: f64 = 1e3;

/// The default number of terms for `f64`: N = ⌈x + 6x^(1/3) + 3⌉.
///
/// Wiscombe's ⌈x + 4.05x^(1/3) + 2⌉ (MIEV0) leaves truncation errors up to about 10⁻¹⁰ in
/// Q_ext and 10⁻⁶ in Q_back (measured against Arb). This rule was chosen from the same
/// measurements so that truncation errors are below rounding errors in `f64`.
pub fn default_n_max(x: f64) -> usize {
    terms(x, 6.0, 3.0)
}

/// ⌈x + c·x^(1/3) + d⌉ (`usize::MAX` if not finite, which every limit rejects).
fn terms(x: f64, c: f64, d: f64) -> usize {
    let n = (x + c * x.cbrt() + d).ceil();
    if n.is_finite() && n < 2f64.powi(53) {
        n as usize
    } else {
        usize::MAX
    }
}

/// The default truncation for scalar type `T`: [`default_n_max`] for `f64` (and `Dual<f64>`),
/// ⌈x + 12x^(1/3) + 6⌉ for `DoubleDouble`.
fn default_terms<T: Real>(x: f64) -> usize {
    if T::epsilon().approx_f64() < 2f64.powi(-60) {
        terms(x, 12.0, 6.0)
    } else {
        default_n_max(x)
    }
}

/// Options for [`Mie::compute`].
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, Default, PartialEq)]
#[non_exhaustive]
pub struct MieOptions {
    /// The number of terms; `None` for the default (see [`default_n_max`]). Fewer terms than
    /// the default are outside the validated domain.
    pub n_max: Option<usize>,
    /// Compute even outside [`VALIDATED_DOMAIN`] instead of returning
    /// [`Error::OutsideValidatedDomain`].
    pub allow_unvalidated: bool,
}

impl MieOptions {
    /// The defaults: the default truncation, and only inside the validated domain.
    pub fn new() -> Self {
        Self::default()
    }

    /// With `n_max` terms.
    #[must_use]
    pub fn with_n_max(mut self, n_max: usize) -> Self {
        self.n_max = Some(n_max);
        self
    }

    /// Allowing (or not) inputs outside the validated domain.
    #[must_use]
    pub fn with_allow_unvalidated(mut self, allow: bool) -> Self {
        self.allow_unvalidated = allow;
        self
    }
}

/// A reusable workspace for Mie calculations, one per thread.
///
/// Its buffers grow to the largest number of terms used and are then reused, so repeated
/// calls do not allocate.
#[derive(Clone, Debug)]
pub struct Mie<T: Real> {
    state: kernel::State<T>,
}

impl<T: Real> Default for Mie<T> {
    fn default() -> Self {
        Self::new()
    }
}

impl<T: Real> Mie<T> {
    /// An empty workspace.
    pub fn new() -> Self {
        Self {
            state: kernel::State::new(),
        }
    }

    /// Solves the sphere of size parameter `x` and refractive index `m`: the coefficients and
    /// everything derived from them, without derivatives.
    ///
    /// # Errors
    ///
    /// * [`Error::InvalidInput`] if x is not in [[`MIN_SIZE_PARAMETER`],
    ///   [`MAX_SIZE_PARAMETER`]], if m is not finite, if Re m < 0 or Im m < 0 (gain media), if
    ///   |m| is not in [[`MIN_REFRACTIVE_INDEX`], [`MAX_REFRACTIVE_INDEX`]], or if the number of
    ///   terms is 0 or above [`MAX_MIE_ORDER`].
    /// * [`Error::OutsideValidatedDomain`] if (x, m) is outside [`VALIDATED_DOMAIN`] or `n_max`
    ///   is below the default, unless `allow_unvalidated` is set.
    ///
    /// For `Dual` inputs the checks apply to the values.
    pub fn compute(
        &mut self,
        x: T,
        m: Complex<T>,
        opts: &MieOptions,
    ) -> Result<MieSolution<'_, T>> {
        self.solve(x, m, opts, false)
    }

    /// As [`compute`](Self::compute), also computing the derivatives of the coefficients
    /// with respect to x and m, which [`MieSolution::derivatives`],
    /// [`MieSolution::efficiencies_jacobian`] and [`MieSolution::amplitudes_with_derivatives`]
    /// need.
    ///
    /// # Errors
    ///
    /// As [`compute`](Self::compute).
    pub fn compute_with_derivatives(
        &mut self,
        x: T,
        m: Complex<T>,
        opts: &MieOptions,
    ) -> Result<MieSolution<'_, T>> {
        self.solve(x, m, opts, true)
    }

    fn solve(
        &mut self,
        x: T,
        m: Complex<T>,
        opts: &MieOptions,
        derivatives: bool,
    ) -> Result<MieSolution<'_, T>> {
        let n = check_inputs::<T>(x.approx_f64(), m.re.approx_f64(), m.im.approx_f64(), opts)?;
        self.state.solve(x, m, n, derivatives);
        Ok(MieSolution { state: &self.state })
    }
}

/// Validates the inputs and returns the number of terms.
fn check_inputs<T: Real>(x: f64, m_re: f64, m_im: f64, opts: &MieOptions) -> Result<usize> {
    if !(MIN_SIZE_PARAMETER..=MAX_SIZE_PARAMETER).contains(&x) {
        return Err(Error::InvalidInput(format!(
            "the size parameter x must be in [{MIN_SIZE_PARAMETER:e}, {MAX_SIZE_PARAMETER:e}], \
             got {x:e}"
        )));
    }
    if !(m_re.is_finite() && m_im.is_finite()) {
        return Err(Error::InvalidInput(format!(
            "the refractive index must be finite, got {m_re:e} + {m_im:e}i"
        )));
    }
    if m_re < 0.0 || m_im < 0.0 {
        return Err(Error::InvalidInput(format!(
            "the refractive index must have Re m >= 0 and Im m >= 0 (Im m < 0 is a gain medium), \
             got {m_re:e} + {m_im:e}i"
        )));
    }
    let abs_m = m_re.hypot(m_im);
    if !(MIN_REFRACTIVE_INDEX..=MAX_REFRACTIVE_INDEX).contains(&abs_m) {
        return Err(Error::InvalidInput(format!(
            "|m| must be in [{MIN_REFRACTIVE_INDEX:e}, {MAX_REFRACTIVE_INDEX:e}], got {abs_m:e}"
        )));
    }
    let default = default_terms::<T>(x);
    let n = opts.n_max.unwrap_or(default);
    if n == 0 || n > MAX_MIE_ORDER {
        return Err(Error::InvalidInput(format!(
            "the number of terms must be in [1, {MAX_MIE_ORDER}], got {n}"
        )));
    }
    if !opts.allow_unvalidated {
        let m = Complex::new(m_re, m_im);
        if !VALIDATED_DOMAIN.contains(x, m) {
            return Err(Error::OutsideValidatedDomain(format!(
                "x = {x:e}, m = {m_re:e} + {m_im:e}i is outside the validated domain {}",
                VALIDATED_DOMAIN
            )));
        }
        if n < default {
            return Err(Error::OutsideValidatedDomain(format!(
                "n_max = {n} is below the default truncation {default} for x = {x:e}"
            )));
        }
    }
    Ok(n)
}

/// The solution for one sphere: a view of the workspace that computed it.
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug)]
pub struct MieSolution<'a, T: Real> {
    state: &'a kernel::State<T>,
}

/// The derivatives of the coefficients, indexed like [`MieSolution::a`].
///
/// d/dm is the complex derivative: ∂/∂(Re m) = d/dm and ∂/∂(Im m) = i·d/dm.
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug)]
#[non_exhaustive]
pub struct CoefficientDerivatives<'a, T> {
    /// ∂aₙ/∂x.
    pub da_dx: &'a [Complex<T>],
    /// daₙ/dm.
    pub da_dm: &'a [Complex<T>],
    /// ∂bₙ/∂x.
    pub db_dx: &'a [Complex<T>],
    /// dbₙ/dm.
    pub db_dm: &'a [Complex<T>],
}

impl<'a, T: Real> MieSolution<'a, T> {
    /// The size parameter.
    pub fn x(&self) -> T {
        self.state.x
    }

    /// The refractive index.
    pub fn m(&self) -> Complex<T> {
        self.state.m
    }

    /// The number of terms N.
    pub fn n_terms(&self) -> usize {
        self.state.a.len()
    }

    /// aₙ for n = 1, …, N (index n − 1).
    pub fn a(&self) -> &'a [Complex<T>] {
        &self.state.a
    }

    /// bₙ for n = 1, …, N (index n − 1).
    pub fn b(&self) -> &'a [Complex<T>] {
        &self.state.b
    }

    /// The derivatives of the coefficients, if computed by
    /// [`Mie::compute_with_derivatives`].
    pub fn derivatives(&self) -> Option<CoefficientDerivatives<'a, T>> {
        self.state.derivatives.then(|| CoefficientDerivatives {
            da_dx: &self.state.da_dx,
            da_dm: &self.state.da_dm,
            db_dx: &self.state.db_dx,
            db_dm: &self.state.db_dm,
        })
    }
}
````

Create `scatrs/src/mie/domain.rs`:

```rust
//! The validated domain of the Mie outputs.

use core::fmt;

use num_complex::Complex;

/// The region of inputs whose accuracy has been validated.
///
/// [`Mie::compute`](super::Mie::compute) returns
/// [`Error::OutsideValidatedDomain`](crate::Error::OutsideValidatedDomain) outside it unless
/// the caller sets `allow_unvalidated`.
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq)]
#[non_exhaustive]
pub struct ValidatedDomain {
    /// The smallest validated size parameter.
    pub x_min: f64,
    /// The largest validated size parameter.
    pub x_max: f64,
    /// The largest validated Re m (the smallest is any value above 0).
    pub m_re_max: f64,
    /// The largest validated Im m (the smallest is 0).
    pub m_im_max: f64,
}

/// The validated domain: x ∈ [10⁻⁶, 2·10⁴], 0 < Re m ≤ 10, 0 ≤ Im m ≤ 10.
pub const VALIDATED_DOMAIN: ValidatedDomain = ValidatedDomain {
    x_min: 1e-6,
    x_max: 2e4,
    m_re_max: 10.0,
    m_im_max: 10.0,
};

impl ValidatedDomain {
    /// Whether (x, m) is inside the domain.
    pub fn contains(&self, x: f64, m: Complex<f64>) -> bool {
        (self.x_min..=self.x_max).contains(&x)
            && m.re > 0.0
            && m.re <= self.m_re_max
            && (0.0..=self.m_im_max).contains(&m.im)
    }

    /// κ(x, m) = (1 + |m|x)·max(1, |m|/|m − 1|): infinite for m = 1, where the coefficients
    /// vanish.
    ///
    /// The outputs depend on the phase mx, so rounding x or m changes them by about |m|x times
    /// the rounding; and aₙ, bₙ ∝ m − 1 as m → 1, so a relative change in m changes them by
    /// |m|/|m − 1| times as much.
    pub fn condition_factor(x: f64, m: Complex<f64>) -> f64 {
        (1.0 + m.norm() * x) * near_one(m)
    }
}

/// max(1, |m|/|m − 1|).
fn near_one(m: Complex<f64>) -> f64 {
    (m.norm() / (m - Complex::new(1.0, 0.0)).norm()).max(1.0)
}

impl fmt::Display for ValidatedDomain {
    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> fmt::Result {
        write!(
            f,
            "x in [{:e}, {:e}], 0 < Re m <= {}, 0 <= Im m <= {}",
            self.x_min, self.x_max, self.m_re_max, self.m_im_max
        )
    }
}

Create `scatrs/src/mie/kernel.rs`:

```rust
//! The Mie recurrences: coefficients, their derivatives and the absorption sums.
//!
//! See the module documentation of [`crate::mie`] for the method. Notation: u_k(ρ) =
//! ψ_k(ρ)/ψ_{k−1}(ρ), D_n(ρ) = (n + 1)/ρ − u_{n+1}(ρ), R_n = ξ_{n−1}/ξ_n, T_n = ψ_n/ξ_n, and
//! Δ_a = A ξ_n − ξ_{n−1}, Δ_b = B ξ_n − ξ_{n−1} with A = D_n(mx)/m + n/x and
//! B = m D_n(mx) + n/x, so that a_n = (A ψ_n − ψ_{n−1})/Δ_a and b_n = (B ψ_n − ψ_{n−1})/Δ_b.

use num_complex::Complex;

use crate::complex::inv;
use crate::scalar::Real;

#[derive(Clone, Debug)]
pub(super) struct State<T: Real> {
    pub(super) x: T,
    pub(super) m: Complex<T>,
    pub(super) a: Vec<Complex<T>>,
    pub(super) b: Vec<Complex<T>>,
    pub(super) derivatives: bool,
    pub(super) da_dx: Vec<Complex<T>>,
    pub(super) da_dm: Vec<Complex<T>>,
    pub(super) db_dx: Vec<Complex<T>>,
    pub(super) db_dm: Vec<Complex<T>>,
    /// (x²/2) Q_abs, and its derivatives with respect to x, Re m and Im m (of the sum alone,
    /// without the factor 2/x²).
    pub(super) abs_sum: T,
    pub(super) abs_sum_jacobian: [T; 3],
    /// u_k(mx) for k = 1, …, N + 1 (index k).
    u_z: Vec<Complex<T>>,
    /// u_k(x) for k = n_s + 1, …, N + 1 (index k).
    u_x: Vec<T>,
    /// u_k(mx) for real m, before conversion into `u_z`.
    u_real: Vec<T>,
}

fn real<T: Real>(v: T) -> Complex<T> {
    Complex::new(v, T::zero())
}

fn int<T: Real>(k: usize) -> T {
    // Exact: the orders are below 2^53.
    T::from_f64(k as f64)
}

/// Steps from the start of a downward ratio recurrence to the first stored value: enough for
/// the start value to be forgotten to rounding level (each step shrinks it at least sixfold).
fn margin<T: Real>() -> usize {
    let bits = -T::epsilon().approx_f64().log2();
    (bits / 2.0).ceil() as usize + 4
}

/// The first index of a downward ratio recurrence that must reach `n_top`: above both `n_top`
/// and 1.5|ρ| by the margin.
fn start_index<T: Real>(n_top: usize, rho_abs: f64) -> usize {
    let above = (1.5 * rho_abs).ceil();
    let above = if above < n_top as f64 {
        n_top
    } else {
        above as usize
    };
    above + margin::<T>()
}

impl<T: Real> State<T> {
    pub(super) fn new() -> Self {
        Self {
            x: T::zero(),
            m: real(T::zero()),
            a: Vec::new(),
            b: Vec::new(),
            derivatives: false,
            da_dx: Vec::new(),
            da_dm: Vec::new(),
            db_dx: Vec::new(),
            db_dm: Vec::new(),
            abs_sum: T::zero(),
            abs_sum_jacobian: [T::zero(); 3],
            u_z: Vec::new(),
            u_x: Vec::new(),
            u_real: Vec::new(),
        }
    }

    /// u_k(ρ) for k = 1, …, n_top + 1 into `self.u_z` (complex ρ = z).
    fn ratios_complex(&mut self, z: Complex<T>, n_top: usize) {
        let inv_z = inv(z);
        let start = start_index::<T>(n_top + 1, z.norm().approx_f64());
        self.u_z.clear();
        self.u_z.resize(n_top + 2, real(T::zero()));
        let mut u = real(T::zero());
        for k in (1..=start).rev() {
            let c = inv_z.scale(int(2 * k + 1));
            let mut den = c - u;
            if den.re == T::zero() && den.im == T::zero() {
                // Only at an exact zero of ψ_{k−1}: nudge by a rounding error, as Lentz's
                // method does, so that the pole of D_{k−1} stays finite.
                den = real(T::epsilon() * (c.norm() + u.norm()));
            }
            u = inv(den);
            if k <= n_top + 1 {
                self.u_z[k] = u;
            }
        }
    }

    /// u_k(ρ) for k = from, …, n_top + 1 (real ρ), returned at index k of a vector.
    fn ratios_real(out: &mut Vec<T>, rho: T, from: usize, n_top: usize) {
        let inv_rho = rho.recip();
        let start = start_index::<T>(n_top + 1, rho.approx_f64().abs());
        out.clear();
        out.resize(n_top + 2, T::zero());
        let mut u = T::zero();
        for k in (from.max(1)..=start).rev() {
            let c = inv_rho * int::<T>(2 * k + 1);
            let mut den = c - u;
            if den == T::zero() {
                den = T::epsilon() * (c.abs() + u.abs());
            }
            u = den.recip();
            if k <= n_top + 1 {
                out[k] = u;
            }
        }
    }

    /// Computes a_n, b_n for n = 1, …, `n` (and their derivatives if asked), and the
    /// absorption sum.
    pub(super) fn solve(&mut self, x: T, m: Complex<T>, n: usize, derivatives: bool) {
        let one = T::one();
        let two = T::from_f64(2.0);
        let i1 = Complex::new(T::zero(), one);
        self.x = x;
        self.m = m;
        self.derivatives = derivatives;
        let z = m.scale(x);
        // D_n(mx). For real m (with no derivative seeded in Im m) the real recurrence is used:
        // twice as fast, and for m = 1 exactly it reproduces u_k(x) bit for bit.
        if m.im.is_zero() {
            Self::ratios_real(&mut self.u_real, z.re, 1, n);
            self.u_z.clear();
            self.u_z.extend(self.u_real.iter().map(|&u| real(u)));
        } else {
            self.ratios_complex(z, n);
        }
        let xf = x.approx_f64();
        // Orders up to n_s use the upward ψ and χ recurrences; above, the ratios.
        let n_s = if xf >= 1.0 {
            (xf.floor() as usize).min(n)
        } else {
            0
        };
        if n_s < n {
            Self::ratios_real(&mut self.u_x, x, n_s + 1, n);
        }
        for v in [
            &mut self.a,
            &mut self.b,
            &mut self.da_dx,
            &mut self.da_dm,
            &mut self.db_dx,
            &mut self.db_dm,
        ] {
            v.clear();
        }
        let inv_x = x.recip();
        let inv_z = inv(z);
        let inv_m = inv(m);
        let m2 = m * m;
        let inv_m2 = inv_m * inv_m;
        // (m − 1)(m + 1): m − 1 is exact for m near 1, unlike m² − 1.
        let m2_minus_1 = (m - real(one)) * (m + real(one));
        let c_a = -(m2_minus_1 * inv_m2); // 1/m² − 1
        let (sin_x, cos_x) = x.sin_cos();
        let (mut psi_prev, mut psi) = (cos_x, sin_x); // ψ₋₁, ψ₀
        let (mut chi_prev, mut chi) = (-sin_x, cos_x); // χ₋₁, χ₀
        // Ratio-region state: T_n, 1/ξ_n and R_n, starting at n = 0 when n_s = 0.
        let mut t = real(T::zero());
        let mut inv_xi = real(T::zero());
        let mut r = real(T::zero());
        if n_s == 0 {
            let xi0 = Complex::new(sin_x, -cos_x);
            t = real(sin_x) * inv(xi0);
            inv_xi = Complex::new(sin_x, cos_x); // 1/ξ₀ = i e^(−ix)
            r = i1; // ξ₋₁/ξ₀
        }
        let mut abs_sum = T::zero();
        let mut jac = [T::zero(); 3];
        for k in 1..=n {
            let kf: T = int(k);
            let k_x = kf * inv_x;
            let d = inv_z.scale(int(k + 1)) - self.u_z[k + 1];
            let d_m = d * inv_m;
            let md = d * m;
            let big_a = d_m + real(k_x);
            let big_b = md + real(k_x);
            // a_n, b_n, 1/Δ_a, 1/Δ_b, ξ_n/Δ and ξ_{n−1}/Δ.
            let (an, bn, inv_da, inv_db, g_a, g_b, h_a, h_b);
            if k <= n_s {
                let c: T = int::<T>(2 * k - 1) * inv_x;
                let psi_next = c * psi - psi_prev;
                psi_prev = psi;
                psi = psi_next;
                let chi_next = c * chi - chi_prev;
                chi_prev = chi;
                chi = chi_next;
                let xi = Complex::new(psi, -chi);
                let xi_prev = Complex::new(psi_prev, -chi_prev);
                inv_da = inv(big_a * xi - xi_prev);
                inv_db = inv(big_b * xi - xi_prev);
                an = (big_a.scale(psi) - real(psi_prev)) * inv_da;
                bn = (big_b.scale(psi) - real(psi_prev)) * inv_db;
                g_a = xi * inv_da;
                g_b = xi * inv_db;
                h_a = xi_prev * inv_da;
                h_b = xi_prev * inv_db;
                if k == n_s {
                    inv_xi = inv(xi);
                    t = real(psi) * inv_xi;
                    r = xi_prev * inv_xi;
                }
            } else {
                r = inv(real(int::<T>(2 * k - 1) * inv_x) - r);
                t = (t * r).scale(self.u_x[k]);
                inv_xi = inv_xi * r;
                let u_z1 = self.u_z[k + 1];
                let u_x1 = real(self.u_x[k + 1]);
                // D/m − D_n(x) and m D − D_n(x), without the cancelling (n + 1)/x terms.
                let num_a = c_a.scale(int::<T>(k + 1) * inv_x) + u_x1 - u_z1 * inv_m;
                let num_b = u_x1 - m * u_z1;
                let ia = inv(big_a - r);
                let ib = inv(big_b - r);
                an = t * num_a * ia;
                bn = t * num_b * ib;
                inv_da = inv_xi * ia;
                inv_db = inv_xi * ib;
                g_a = ia;
                g_b = ib;
                h_a = r * ia;
                h_b = r * ib;
            }
            self.a.push(an);
            self.b.push(bn);
            let na = inv_da.norm_sqr();
            let nb = inv_db.norm_sqr();
            let weight: T = int(2 * k + 1);
            // Re a_n − |a_n|² and Re b_n − |b_n|².
            abs_sum += weight * (-(d_m.im * na) - md.im * nb);
            if derivatives {
                // D′ = n(n + 1)/z² − 1 − D², D + zD′ and D − zD′ in terms of u = u_{n+1}(z),
                // with D = (n + 1)/z − u: the terms in 1/z² and 1/z that cancel at small z
                // (D + zD′ = O(z)) are removed exactly.
                let u = self.u_z[k + 1];
                let one_u2 = real(one) + u * u;
                let dp = (u.scale(two) * inv_z - inv_z * inv_z).scale(int(k + 1)) - one_u2;
                let d_plus = u.scale(weight) - z * one_u2; // D + zD′
                let d_minus = d.scale(two) - d_plus; // D − zD′
                let kk1: T = int(k * (k + 1));
                let ida2 = inv_da * inv_da;
                let idb2 = inv_db * inv_db;
                self.da_dm.push(i1 * d_minus * inv_m2 * ida2);
                self.db_dm.push(-(i1 * d_plus * idb2));
                self.da_dx
                    .push(i1 * m2_minus_1 * (d * d + real(kk1 * inv_x * inv_x)) * inv_m2 * ida2);
                self.db_dx.push(i1 * m2_minus_1 * idb2);
                // Derivatives of −Im(f)/|Δ|² with f = D/m or m D: for a parameter p,
                // −[Im(∂f/∂p) − 2 Im(f) Re(∂Δ/∂p / Δ)]/|Δ|².
                let fa_m = -(d_minus * inv_m2); // d(D/m)/dm = dA/dm
                let fb_m = d_plus; // d(mD)/dm = dB/dm
                let k_x2 = k_x * inv_x;
                let la_x = g_a * (dp - real(k_x2) - big_a.scale(k_x) + real(one)) + h_a * d_m;
                let lb_x = g_b * (m2 * dp - real(k_x2) - big_b.scale(k_x) + real(one)) + h_b * md;
                let la_m = fa_m * g_a;
                let lb_m = fb_m * g_b;
                let term = |f: Complex<T>, df: Complex<T>, l: Complex<T>, n2: T| {
                    -((df.im - two * f.im * l.re) * n2)
                };
                jac[0] += weight * (term(d_m, dp, la_x, na) + term(md, m2 * dp, lb_x, nb));
                jac[1] += weight * (term(d_m, fa_m, la_m, na) + term(md, fb_m, lb_m, nb));
                jac[2] += weight
                    * (term(d_m, i1 * fa_m, i1 * la_m, na) + term(md, i1 * fb_m, i1 * lb_m, nb));
            }
        }
        self.abs_sum = abs_sum;
        self.abs_sum_jacobian = jac;
    }
}

```rust
//! Efficiencies, the asymmetry parameter and their Jacobian.

use num_complex::Complex;

use super::MieSolution;
use crate::scalar::Real;

/// Efficiencies (cross sections divided by the geometric cross section πr²) and the
/// asymmetry parameter.
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq)]
#[non_exhaustive]
pub struct Efficiencies<T> {
    /// Extinction, Q_sca + Q_abs. It equals (2/x²) Σ (2n + 1) Re(aₙ + bₙ) (the optical
    /// theorem), which is not used to compute it: for small non-absorbing spheres
    /// Re aₙ ≈ |aₙ|² ≪ |aₙ| would leave few correct digits.
    pub q_ext: T,
    /// Scattering, (2/x²) Σ (2n + 1)(|aₙ|² + |bₙ|²).
    pub q_sca: T,
    /// Absorption, (2/x²) Σ (2n + 1)(Re aₙ − |aₙ|² + Re bₙ − |bₙ|²), each term from the
    /// identity Re aₙ − |aₙ|² = −Im(Dₙ(mx)/m)/|Δₐ|² (and likewise for bₙ): accurate relative
    /// to itself even when Q_abs ≪ Q_ext, and exactly 0 for real m.
    pub q_abs: T,
    /// Backscattering, |Σ (2n + 1)(−1)ⁿ(aₙ − bₙ)|²/x² = 4|S₁(π)|²/x².
    pub q_back: T,
    /// Asymmetry parameter g = ⟨cos Θ⟩, (4/(x² Q_sca)) Σ [n(n + 2)/(n + 1)
    /// Re(aₙ aₙ₊₁* + bₙ bₙ₊₁*) + (2n + 1)/(n(n + 1)) Re(aₙ bₙ*)]; 0 when Q_sca = 0 (m = 1).
    pub g: T,
}

/// The derivatives of each efficiency with respect to x, Re m and Im m.
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq)]
#[non_exhaustive]
pub struct EfficiencyJacobian<T> {
    /// ∂/∂x.
    pub x: Efficiencies<T>,
    /// ∂/∂(Re m).
    pub m_re: Efficiencies<T>,
    /// ∂/∂(Im m).
    pub m_im: Efficiencies<T>,
}

/// Re(u v*).
fn re_mul_conj<T: Real>(u: Complex<T>, v: Complex<T>) -> T {
    u.re * v.re + u.im * v.im
}

fn int<T: Real>(k: usize) -> T {
    T::from_f64(k as f64)
}

/// The sums behind Q_sca (`sca`, with Q_sca = 2·sca/x²), Q_back (`back`, with
/// Q_back = |back|²/x²) and g (`g`, with g = 2·g/sca), or their derivatives.
struct Sums<T> {
    sca: T,
    back: Complex<T>,
    g: T,
}

fn value_sums<T: Real>(a: &[Complex<T>], b: &[Complex<T>]) -> Sums<T> {
    let n = a.len();
    let mut sca = T::zero();
    let mut back = Complex::new(T::zero(), T::zero());
    let mut g = T::zero();
    for k in 1..=n {
        let (ak, bk) = (a[k - 1], b[k - 1]);
        let w: T = int(2 * k + 1);
        sca += w * (ak.norm_sqr() + bk.norm_sqr());
        let t = (ak - bk).scale(w);
        back = if k % 2 == 0 { back + t } else { back - t };
        let kf: T = int(k);
        if k < n {
            let (a1, b1) = (a[k], b[k]);
            g += kf * (kf + T::from_f64(2.0)) / (kf + T::one())
                * (re_mul_conj(ak, a1) + re_mul_conj(bk, b1));
        }
        g += w / (kf * (kf + T::one())) * re_mul_conj(ak, bk);
    }
    Sums { sca, back, g }
}

/// The derivatives of the sums of [`value_sums`], given the coefficients' derivatives.
fn derivative_sums<T: Real>(
    a: &[Complex<T>],
    b: &[Complex<T>],
    da: impl Fn(usize) -> Complex<T>,
    db: impl Fn(usize) -> Complex<T>,
) -> Sums<T> {
    let n = a.len();
    let two = T::from_f64(2.0);
    let mut sca = T::zero();
    let mut back = Complex::new(T::zero(), T::zero());
    let mut g = T::zero();
    for k in 1..=n {
        let (ak, bk, dak, dbk) = (a[k - 1], b[k - 1], da(k - 1), db(k - 1));
        let w: T = int(2 * k + 1);
        sca += w * two * (re_mul_conj(dak, ak) + re_mul_conj(dbk, bk));
        let t = (dak - dbk).scale(w);
        back = if k % 2 == 0 { back + t } else { back - t };
        let kf: T = int(k);
        if k < n {
            let (a1, b1, da1, db1) = (a[k], b[k], da(k), db(k));
            g += kf * (kf + two) / (kf + T::one())
                * (re_mul_conj(dak, a1)
                    + re_mul_conj(ak, da1)
                    + re_mul_conj(dbk, b1)
                    + re_mul_conj(bk, db1));
        }
        g += w / (kf * (kf + T::one())) * (re_mul_conj(dak, bk) + re_mul_conj(ak, dbk));
    }
    Sums { sca, back, g }
}

impl<T: Real> MieSolution<'_, T> {
    /// The efficiencies and the asymmetry parameter.
    pub fn efficiencies(&self) -> Efficiencies<T> {
        let s = self.state;
        let inv_x = s.x.recip();
        let inv_x2 = inv_x * inv_x;
        let two_x2 = T::from_f64(2.0) * inv_x2;
        let sums = value_sums(&s.a, &s.b);
        let q_sca = two_x2 * sums.sca;
        let q_abs = two_x2 * s.abs_sum;
        let g = if sums.sca == T::zero() {
            T::zero()
        } else {
            T::from_f64(2.0) * sums.g / sums.sca
        };
        Efficiencies {
            q_ext: q_sca + q_abs,
            q_sca,
            q_abs,
            q_back: sums.back.norm_sqr() * inv_x2,
            g,
        }
    }

    /// The derivatives of the efficiencies with respect to x, Re m and Im m, if the solution
    /// was computed by [`Mie::compute_with_derivatives`](super::Mie::compute_with_derivatives).
    pub fn efficiencies_jacobian(&self) -> Option<EfficiencyJacobian<T>> {
        let s = self.state;
        if !s.derivatives {
            return None;
        }
        let i1 = Complex::new(T::zero(), T::one());
        let two = T::from_f64(2.0);
        let inv_x = s.x.recip();
        let inv_x2 = inv_x * inv_x;
        let two_x2 = two * inv_x2;
        let values = value_sums(&s.a, &s.b);
        let q = self.efficiencies();
        let mut out = [q; 3];
        for (p, o) in out.iter_mut().enumerate() {
            let sums = match p {
                0 => derivative_sums(&s.a, &s.b, |k| s.da_dx[k], |k| s.db_dx[k]),
                1 => derivative_sums(&s.a, &s.b, |k| s.da_dm[k], |k| s.db_dm[k]),
                _ => derivative_sums(&s.a, &s.b, |k| i1 * s.da_dm[k], |k| i1 * s.db_dm[k]),
            };
            // Q = (2/x²)·sum: ∂Q/∂x = (2/x²)·∂sum/∂x − (2/x)·Q.
            let x_term = |v: T| if p == 0 { two * inv_x * v } else { T::zero() };
            let q_sca = two_x2 * sums.sca - x_term(q.q_sca);
            let q_abs = two_x2 * s.abs_sum_jacobian[p] - x_term(q.q_abs);
            let q_back = two * re_mul_conj(sums.back, values.back) * inv_x2 - x_term(q.q_back);
            // g = 2G/S with S the scattering sum: ∂g = (2∂G − g ∂S)/S (x cancels).
            let g = if values.sca == T::zero() {
                T::zero()
            } else {
                (two * sums.g - q.g * sums.sca) / values.sca
            };
            *o = Efficiencies {
                q_ext: q_sca + q_abs,
                q_sca,
                q_abs,
                q_back,
                g,
            };
        }
        let [x, m_re, m_im] = out;
        Some(EfficiencyJacobian { x, m_re, m_im })
    }
}
```

Create `scatrs/src/mie/amplitudes.rs`:

```rust
//! The amplitude scattering functions S₁ and S₂.

use num_complex::Complex;

use super::MieSolution;
use crate::scalar::Real;
use crate::{Error, Result};

/// Output buffers for S₁ and S₂, or for their derivatives, one value per cosine.
#[derive(Debug)]
pub struct AmplitudeBuffers<'b, T> {
    /// S₁ (perpendicular polarization).
    pub s1: &'b mut [Complex<T>],
    /// S₂ (parallel polarization).
    pub s2: &'b mut [Complex<T>],
}

impl<T> AmplitudeBuffers<'_, T> {
    fn check(&self, len: usize, what: &str) -> Result<()> {
        if self.s1.len() != len || self.s2.len() != len {
            return Err(Error::InvalidInput(format!(
                "the {what} buffers must have one value per cosine ({len}), got {} and {}",
                self.s1.len(),
                self.s2.len()
            )));
        }
        Ok(())
    }
}

fn check_cosines<T: Real>(mu: &[T]) -> Result<()> {
    match mu
        .iter()
        .map(|v| v.approx_f64())
        .find(|v| !(-1.0..=1.0).contains(v))
    {
        Some(v) => Err(Error::InvalidInput(format!(
            "a cosine of the scattering angle must be in [-1, 1], got {v}"
        ))),
        None => Ok(()),
    }
}

/// Calls `f(n, wπₙ, wτₙ)` for n = 1..=n_max, with w = (2n + 1)/(n(n + 1)).
///
/// πₙ and τₙ by the upward recurrences (Bohren & Huffman 1983, Eq. 4.47):
/// πₙ = ((2n − 1)/(n − 1)) μ πₙ₋₁ − (n/(n − 1)) πₙ₋₂ from π₀ = 0, π₁ = 1, and
/// τₙ = n μ πₙ − (n + 1) πₙ₋₁.
fn for_each_order<T: Real>(mu: T, n_max: usize, mut f: impl FnMut(usize, T, T)) {
    let mut pi_prev = T::zero();
    let mut pi = T::one();
    for n in 1..=n_max {
        let nf = T::from_f64(n as f64);
        let n1 = nf + T::one();
        let tau = nf * mu * pi - n1 * pi_prev;
        let w = T::from_f64((2 * n + 1) as f64) / (nf * n1);
        f(n, w * pi, w * tau);
        let pi_next = (T::from_f64((2 * n + 1) as f64) * mu * pi - n1 * pi_prev) / nf;
        pi_prev = pi;
        pi = pi_next;
    }
}

impl<T: Real> MieSolution<'_, T> {
    /// S₁(μ) and S₂(μ) at the cosines `mu` of the scattering angle (Bohren & Huffman 1983,
    /// Eq. 4.74): S₁ = Σ (2n + 1)/(n(n + 1)) (aₙπₙ + bₙτₙ) and
    /// S₂ = Σ (2n + 1)/(n(n + 1)) (aₙτₙ + bₙπₙ).
    ///
    /// # Errors
    ///
    /// [`Error::InvalidInput`] if a cosine is NaN or outside [−1, 1] (for `Dual`, its value),
    /// or a buffer's length differs from `mu.len()`. Nothing is written then.
    pub fn amplitudes(&self, mu: &[T], out: AmplitudeBuffers<'_, T>) -> Result<()> {
        out.check(mu.len(), "amplitude")?;
        check_cosines(mu)?;
        let (a, b) = (&self.state.a, &self.state.b);
        for (j, &muj) in mu.iter().enumerate() {
            let zero = Complex::new(T::zero(), T::zero());
            let (mut s1, mut s2) = (zero, zero);
            for_each_order(muj, a.len(), |n, pi, tau| {
                let (an, bn) = (a[n - 1], b[n - 1]);
                s1 = s1 + an.scale(pi) + bn.scale(tau);
                s2 = s2 + an.scale(tau) + bn.scale(pi);
            });
            out.s1[j] = s1;
            out.s2[j] = s2;
        }
        Ok(())
    }

    /// S₁, S₂ and their derivatives: `d_dx` receives ∂S/∂x and `d_dm` the complex
    /// derivative dS/dm (∂/∂(Re m) = d/dm, ∂/∂(Im m) = i·d/dm).
    ///
    /// # Errors
    ///
    /// As [`amplitudes`](Self::amplitudes), and [`Error::InvalidInput`] if the solution was
    /// computed without derivatives.
    pub fn amplitudes_with_derivatives(
        &self,
        mu: &[T],
        values: AmplitudeBuffers<'_, T>,
        d_dx: AmplitudeBuffers<'_, T>,
        d_dm: AmplitudeBuffers<'_, T>,
    ) -> Result<()> {
        let Some(d) = self.derivatives() else {
            return Err(Error::InvalidInput(
                "amplitude derivatives need a solution from Mie::compute_with_derivatives".into(),
            ));
        };
        values.check(mu.len(), "amplitude")?;
        d_dx.check(mu.len(), "derivative")?;
        d_dm.check(mu.len(), "derivative")?;
        check_cosines(mu)?;
        let (a, b) = (&self.state.a, &self.state.b);
        for (j, &muj) in mu.iter().enumerate() {
            let zero = Complex::new(T::zero(), T::zero());
            let mut s = [zero; 6]; // S₁, S₂, ∂S₁/∂x, ∂S₂/∂x, dS₁/dm, dS₂/dm
            for_each_order(muj, a.len(), |n, pi, tau| {
                let k = n - 1;
                let pairs = [
                    (a[k], b[k]),
                    (d.da_dx[k], d.db_dx[k]),
                    (d.da_dm[k], d.db_dm[k]),
                ];
                for (i, (u, v)) in pairs.into_iter().enumerate() {
                    s[2 * i] = s[2 * i] + u.scale(pi) + v.scale(tau);
                    s[2 * i + 1] = s[2 * i + 1] + u.scale(tau) + v.scale(pi);
                }
            });
            (values.s1[j], values.s2[j]) = (s[0], s[1]);
            (d_dx.s1[j], d_dx.s2[j]) = (s[2], s[3]);
            (d_dm.s1[j], d_dm.s2[j]) = (s[4], s[5]);
        }
        Ok(())
    }
}
```

### Task 5: The validated domain, its tolerances, and the accuracy map

The exit criterion of phase 1a: a declared accuracy, enforced in CI. Inside the domain every `f64` output is within C·(κ′ + cond)·2⁻⁵² of the exact value for the exact inputs, in its error measure (`MieOutput`). cond is the output's relative condition number with respect to x and m (from double-double derivatives, and for the derivative outputs from central differences of them); κ′ is κ for values and κ·max(1, |m|/|m − 1|) for derivatives. Neither term alone suffices (spec section 12): κ alone fails at the sharp resonances of weakly absorbing spheres (constants up to 7600, growing with grid density), cond alone fails for metallic backscattering (6600). Declared constants, at least 4× the worst measured: 16 (coefficients, efficiencies, g), 64 (S₁, S₂), 32 (their derivatives), 128 (efficiency Jacobian), 256 (coefficient derivatives).

Two tests enforce it on every OS: `mie_reference.rs` against every Arb value (4.9 s), and `mie_accuracy_map.rs`, f64 against `DoubleDouble` on 104 size parameters × 24 refractive indices × 41 cosines (about 15–20 s with all cores), which also writes the JSON the documentation plots.

- Modify: `scatrs/src/mie/domain.rs` (replace), `scatrs/src/mie/mod.rs`, `scatrs/tests/common/mie.rs` (replace), `docs/index.md`, `pyproject.toml`, `uv.lock` (by `uv lock`), `tests/test_docs_consistency.py`
- Test: `scatrs/tests/mie_reference.rs`, `scatrs/tests/mie_accuracy_map.rs`
- Create: `docs/theory/mie.md`, `docs/validation/accuracy.md`, `docs/validation/data/mie-accuracy-map.json` (generated)

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: Task 3's records; Task 4's API and `common::mie::{Outputs, solve, q_of, EFFICIENCY_NAMES}`.
- Produces:
  - `enum MieOutput { Coefficients, CoefficientDerivatives, Extinction, Scattering, Absorption, Backscattering, Asymmetry, Amplitudes, EfficiencyJacobian, AmplitudeDerivatives }` (`#[non_exhaustive]`), re-exported from `scatrs::mie`.
  - `ValidatedDomain::tolerance(&self, output: MieOutput, x: f64, m: Complex<f64>, condition: f64) -> f64` and `ValidatedDomain::constant(output: MieOutput) -> f64`.
  - Test helpers: `Measure { scale, condition }`, `reference(x, m, mu) -> Outputs<Dd>`, `value_measures(x, m, &Outputs<Dd>, orders) -> ValueMeasures` (fields `coefficients: [Measure; 2]`, `efficiencies: [Measure; 5]`, `amplitudes: Vec<[Measure; 2]>`), `derivative_measures(x, m, mu, &Outputs<Dd>, orders) -> DerivativeMeasures` (fields `coefficients: [Measure; 4]`, `jacobian: [[Measure; 5]; 3]`, `amplitudes: [[Measure; 2]; 2]`), `g_floor(x)`.
  - `docs/validation/data/mie-accuracy-map.json`: `{"x": [...], "m": [[re, im], ...], "declared": {name: C}, "c": {name: [[C per m] per x]}}` with names `coefficients`, `q_ext`, `q_sca`, `q_abs`, `q_back`, `g`, `amplitudes`, `coefficient_derivatives`, `efficiency_jacobian`, `amplitude_derivatives`.

```rust
//! Shared by the Mie tests: solving one sphere in any scalar type, and the scale (the
//! denominator) and condition number of each error measure of `scatrs::mie::MieOutput`, from
//! double-double solutions (see `scatrs::mie::ValidatedDomain`).

use num_complex::Complex;
use scatrs::Real;
use scatrs::mie::{AmplitudeBuffers, Efficiencies, Mie, MieOptions};
use scatrs::scalar::DoubleDouble as Dd;

/// Everything the Mie tests compare, from one solution.
#[derive(Clone, Debug)]
pub struct Outputs<T> {
    pub a: Vec<Complex<T>>,
    pub b: Vec<Complex<T>>,
    /// ∂a/∂x, da/dm, ∂b/∂x, db/dm.
    pub d: [Vec<Complex<T>>; 4],
    pub q: Efficiencies<T>,
    /// ∂/∂x, ∂/∂Re m, ∂/∂Im m.
    pub jac: [Efficiencies<T>; 3],
    /// S₁, S₂, ∂S₁/∂x, ∂S₂/∂x, dS₁/dm, dS₂/dm at each cosine.
    pub s: [Vec<Complex<T>>; 6],
}

/// Solves (x, m) with derivatives and amplitudes at `mu`, inside or outside the domain.
pub fn solve<T: Real>(x: T, m: Complex<T>, mu: &[T], n_max: Option<usize>) -> Outputs<T> {
    let mut opts = MieOptions::new().with_allow_unvalidated(true);
    if let Some(n) = n_max {
        opts = opts.with_n_max(n);
    }
    let mut mie = Mie::<T>::new();
    let sol = mie
        .compute_with_derivatives(x, m, &opts)
        .expect("valid inputs");
    let d = sol.derivatives().expect("computed");
    let jac = sol.efficiencies_jacobian().expect("computed");
    let zero = Complex::new(T::zero(), T::zero());
    let mut s: [Vec<Complex<T>>; 6] = std::array::from_fn(|_| vec![zero; mu.len()]);
    {
        let [s1, s2, dx1, dx2, dm1, dm2] = &mut s;
        sol.amplitudes_with_derivatives(
            mu,
            AmplitudeBuffers { s1, s2 },
            AmplitudeBuffers { s1: dx1, s2: dx2 },
            AmplitudeBuffers { s1: dm1, s2: dm2 },
        )
        .expect("valid cosines");
    }
    Outputs {
        a: sol.a().to_vec(),
        b: sol.b().to_vec(),
        d: [
            d.da_dx.to_vec(),
            d.da_dm.to_vec(),
            d.db_dx.to_vec(),
            d.db_dm.to_vec(),
        ],
        q: sol.efficiencies(),
        jac: [jac.x, jac.m_re, jac.m_im],
        s,
    }
}

/// Efficiency k of `e`: Q_ext, Q_sca, Q_abs, Q_back, g.
pub fn q_of<T: Copy>(e: &Efficiencies<T>, k: usize) -> T {
    [e.q_ext, e.q_sca, e.q_abs, e.q_back, e.g][k]
}

pub const EFFICIENCY_NAMES: [&str; 5] = ["q_ext", "q_sca", "q_abs", "q_back", "g"];

/// The denominator and the condition number of one error measure.
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug)]
pub struct Measure {
    pub scale: f64,
    pub condition: f64,
}

/// The measures of the values at (x, m).
pub struct ValueMeasures {
    /// aₙ, bₙ, over the orders asked for.
    pub coefficients: [Measure; 2],
    pub efficiencies: [Measure; 5],
    /// S₁, S₂ at each cosine.
    pub amplitudes: Vec<[Measure; 2]>,
}

/// The measures of the derivatives at (x, m).
pub struct DerivativeMeasures {
    /// ∂a/∂x, da/dm, ∂b/∂x, db/dm, over the orders asked for.
    pub coefficients: [Measure; 4],
    /// [∂/∂x, ∂/∂Re m, ∂/∂Im m][Q_ext, Q_sca, Q_abs, Q_back, g].
    pub jacobian: [[Measure; 5]; 3],
    /// [∂/∂x, d/dm][S₁, S₂], over all cosines.
    pub amplitudes: [[Measure; 2]; 2],
}

fn cnorm(z: Complex<Dd>) -> f64 {
    z.re.hi().hypot(z.im.hi())
}

/// The floor of the asymmetry measure, 10⁻³ min(1, x²).
pub fn g_floor(x: f64) -> f64 {
    1e-3 * x.powi(2).min(1.0)
}

/// The double-double solution at (x, m) and cosines `mu`, with the default truncation.
pub fn reference(x: f64, m: Complex<f64>, mu: &[f64]) -> Outputs<Dd> {
    let mu_dd: Vec<Dd> = mu.iter().map(|&v| Dd::from_f64(v)).collect();
    solve(
        Dd::from_f64(x),
        Complex::new(Dd::from_f64(m.re), Dd::from_f64(m.im)),
        &mu_dd,
        None,
    )
}

/// max over `orders` (1-based) of |values| and of x·|d_dx| + |m|·|d_dm|, as a measure.
fn array_measure(
    orders: &[usize],
    values: &[Complex<Dd>],
    x: f64,
    abs_m: f64,
    d_dx: &dyn Fn(usize) -> f64,
    d_dm: &dyn Fn(usize) -> f64,
) -> Measure {
    let scale = orders
        .iter()
        .map(|&n| cnorm(values[n - 1]))
        .fold(0.0, f64::max);
    let num = orders
        .iter()
        .map(|&n| x * d_dx(n - 1) + abs_m * d_dm(n - 1))
        .fold(0.0, f64::max);
    Measure {
        scale,
        condition: num / scale,
    }
}

/// The sizes of Q_ext, Q_sca, Q_abs (Q_ext when Q_abs = 0), Q_back and g in their measures.
fn efficiency_sizes(d: &Outputs<Dd>, x: f64) -> [f64; 5] {
    let q = |k: usize| q_of(&d.q, k).hi();
    let q_abs = if q(2) > 0.0 { q(2) } else { q(0) };
    [
        q(0),
        q(1),
        q_abs,
        q(3).max(q(1)),
        q(4).abs().max(g_floor(x)),
    ]
}

/// The measures of the values at (x, m), from the double-double solution `d` there (whose
/// cosines are those of the amplitudes), over the coefficient orders `orders`.
pub fn value_measures(x: f64, m: Complex<f64>, d: &Outputs<Dd>, orders: &[usize]) -> ValueMeasures {
    let abs_m = m.norm();
    let cond = |dx: f64, dm: f64, scale: f64| (x * dx.abs() + abs_m * dm) / scale;
    let coefficients = [
        array_measure(orders, &d.a, x, abs_m, &|k| cnorm(d.d[0][k]), &|k| {
            cnorm(d.d[1][k])
        }),
        array_measure(orders, &d.b, x, abs_m, &|k| cnorm(d.d[2][k]), &|k| {
            cnorm(d.d[3][k])
        }),
    ];
    let sizes = efficiency_sizes(d, x);
    let efficiencies = std::array::from_fn(|k| {
        let (jx, jr, ji) = (
            q_of(&d.jac[0], k).hi(),
            q_of(&d.jac[1], k).hi(),
            q_of(&d.jac[2], k).hi(),
        );
        Measure {
            scale: sizes[k],
            condition: cond(jx, jr.hypot(ji), sizes[k]),
        }
    });
    let s_rms = x * q_of(&d.q, 1).hi().sqrt() / 2.0;
    let amplitudes = (0..d.s[0].len())
        .map(|j| {
            std::array::from_fn(|i| {
                let scale = cnorm(d.s[i][j]).max(s_rms);
                Measure {
                    scale,
                    condition: cond(cnorm(d.s[2 + i][j]), cnorm(d.s[4 + i][j]), scale),
                }
            })
        })
        .collect();
    ValueMeasures {
        coefficients,
        efficiencies,
        amplitudes,
    }
}

/// The measures of the derivatives at (x, m), from the double-double solution `d` there and
/// central differences of double-double derivatives (steps 2⁻³⁰ x and 2⁻³⁰ |m|, one-sided in
/// Im m at Im m = 0; their truncation errors, about 2⁻⁶⁰ relative, are far below what the
/// measures resolve), over the coefficient orders `orders` and the cosines `mu`.
pub fn derivative_measures(
    x: f64,
    m: Complex<f64>,
    mu: &[f64],
    d: &Outputs<Dd>,
    orders: &[usize],
) -> DerivativeMeasures {
    let mu_dd: Vec<Dd> = mu.iter().map(|&v| Dd::from_f64(v)).collect();
    let at = |x: Dd, re: Dd, im: Dd| solve(x, Complex::new(re, im), &mu_dd, None);
    let (xd, rd, id) = (Dd::from_f64(x), Dd::from_f64(m.re), Dd::from_f64(m.im));
    let h_x = xd * Dd::from_f64(2f64.powi(-30));
    let h_m = Dd::from_f64(m.norm() * 2f64.powi(-30));
    let (px, nx) = (at(xd + h_x, rd, id), at(xd - h_x, rd, id));
    let (pr, nr) = (at(xd, rd + h_m, id), at(xd, rd - h_m, id));
    let one_sided = m.im < m.norm() * 2f64.powi(-30);
    let pi = at(xd, rd, id + h_m);
    let ni = if one_sided {
        d.clone()
    } else {
        at(xd, rd, id - h_m)
    };
    let two = Dd::from_f64(2.0);
    // ∂/∂x, ∂/∂Re m (= d/dm for holomorphic quantities) and ∂/∂Im m.
    let gx = |p: Dd, n: Dd| ((p - n) / (two * h_x)).hi();
    let gr = |p: Dd, n: Dd| ((p - n) / (two * h_m)).hi();
    let gi = |p: Dd, n: Dd| ((p - n) / if one_sided { h_m } else { two * h_m }).hi();
    let cx = |p: Complex<Dd>, n: Complex<Dd>| gx(p.re, n.re).hypot(gx(p.im, n.im));
    let cr = |p: Complex<Dd>, n: Complex<Dd>| gr(p.re, n.re).hypot(gr(p.im, n.im));
    let (abs_m, phase) = (m.norm(), 1.0 + m.norm() * x);
    let coefficients = std::array::from_fn(|i| {
        array_measure(
            orders,
            &d.d[i],
            x,
            abs_m,
            &|k| cx(px.d[i][k], nx.d[i][k]),
            &|k| cr(pr.d[i][k], nr.d[i][k]),
        )
    });
    // The natural size of a derivative: Q·(1 + |m|x)/|p|, with Q_ext for Q_abs.
    let mut sizes = efficiency_sizes(d, x);
    sizes[2] = sizes[0];
    let p_sizes = [x, abs_m, abs_m];
    let jacobian = std::array::from_fn(|p| {
        std::array::from_fn(|k| {
            let value = q_of(&d.jac[p], k).hi();
            let scale = value.abs().max(sizes[k] * phase / p_sizes[p]);
            let dx = gx(q_of(&px.jac[p], k), q_of(&nx.jac[p], k));
            let dr = gr(q_of(&pr.jac[p], k), q_of(&nr.jac[p], k));
            let di = gi(q_of(&pi.jac[p], k), q_of(&ni.jac[p], k));
            Measure {
                scale,
                condition: (x * dx.abs() + abs_m * dr.hypot(di)) / scale,
            }
        })
    });
    let s_rms = x * q_of(&d.q, 1).hi().sqrt() / 2.0;
    let amplitudes = std::array::from_fn(|p| {
        std::array::from_fn(|i| {
            let k = 2 + 2 * p + i;
            let natural = s_rms * phase / [x, abs_m][p];
            let scale = d.s[k]
                .iter()
                .map(|&v| cnorm(v).max(natural))
                .fold(0.0, f64::max);
            let num = (0..mu.len())
                .map(|j| x * cx(px.s[k][j], nx.s[k][j]) + abs_m * cr(pr.s[k][j], nr.s[k][j]))
                .fold(0.0, f64::max);
            Measure {
                scale,
                condition: num / scale,
            }
        })
    });
    DerivativeMeasures {
        coefficients,
        jacobian,
        amplitudes,
    }
}
```

Create `scatrs/tests/mie_reference.rs`:

```rust
//! Mie outputs against the Arb reference values in `mie.jsonl` and `mie_derivatives.jsonl`.
//!
//! Every reference point is inside the validated domain, so every error must be within its
//! declared tolerance, `VALIDATED_DOMAIN.tolerance(output, x, m, condition)`, with the scale and
//! condition number of the error measure from `common::mie`. Each test prints the worst
//! C = error/((κ′ + cond)·2⁻⁵²) it measures, against the declared constant.

mod common;

use common::mie::{EFFICIENCY_NAMES, Measure, derivative_measures, reference, value_measures};
use common::{Record, load};
use num_complex::Complex;
use scatrs::mie::{
    AmplitudeBuffers, Mie, MieOptions, MieOutput, VALIDATED_DOMAIN, ValidatedDomain, default_n_max,
};

/// The measures of the efficiencies, in the order of `EFFICIENCY_NAMES`.
const EFFICIENCY_OUTPUTS: [MieOutput; 5] = [
    MieOutput::Extinction,
    MieOutput::Scattering,
    MieOutput::Absorption,
    MieOutput::Backscattering,
    MieOutput::Asymmetry,
];

fn size_parameter(r: &Record) -> f64 {
    r.input_f64("x")
}

fn refractive_index(r: &Record) -> Complex<f64> {
    Complex::new(r.input_f64("m_re"), r.input_f64("m_im"))
}

/// |got − v| for the reference value i of `name`.
fn error(got: f64, r: &Record, name: &str, i: usize) -> f64 {
    r.output(name).dd_error(i, got, 0.0)
}

fn complex_error(got: Complex<f64>, r: &Record, stem: &str, i: usize) -> f64 {
    error(got.re, r, &format!("{stem}_re"), i).hypot(error(got.im, r, &format!("{stem}_im"), i))
}

/// The largest measured C of one output, and where.
struct Worst {
    what: String,
    c: f64,
    case: String,
}

impl Worst {
    fn new(what: &str) -> Self {
        Self {
            what: what.to_owned(),
            c: 0.0,
            case: String::new(),
        }
    }

    /// Checks the error `err` of `output` at the case of `r` against the declared tolerance in
    /// the output's measure, and records its C.
    fn check(&mut self, output: MieOutput, err: f64, measure: Measure, r: &Record, detail: &str) {
        let (x, m) = (size_parameter(r), refractive_index(r));
        assert!(
            VALIDATED_DOMAIN.contains(x, m),
            "{} is outside the domain",
            r.case
        );
        let tol = VALIDATED_DOMAIN.tolerance(output, x, m, measure.condition);
        let rel = err / measure.scale;
        let c = rel / tol * ValidatedDomain::constant(output);
        if c > self.c {
            self.c = c;
            self.case = format!("{} {detail}", r.case);
        }
        assert!(
            rel <= tol,
            "{} {} {detail}: error {rel:.3e} exceeds {tol:.3e} (C = {c:.2}, condition {:.3e})",
            self.what,
            r.case,
            measure.condition
        );
    }
}

impl Drop for Worst {
    fn drop(&mut self) {
        if !std::thread::panicking() {
            eprintln!(
                "{:<26} worst C = {:8.3} at {}",
                self.what, self.c, self.case
            );
        }
    }
}

fn records(file: &str, kind: &str) -> Vec<Record> {
    load(file)
        .into_iter()
        .filter(|r| r.case.ends_with(kind))
        .collect()
}

/// The stored orders of a coefficient record (1-based).
fn orders(r: &Record) -> Vec<usize> {
    r.input_i64s("orders").iter().map(|&n| n as usize).collect()
}

/// Options with enough terms for the largest stored order of coefficient record `r`.
fn options(r: &Record) -> MieOptions {
    let top = *orders(r).last().expect("orders");
    MieOptions::new().with_n_max(default_n_max(size_parameter(r)).max(top))
}

/// max over the stored orders of |error|, for one array.
fn array_error(r: &Record, stem: &str, got: &[Complex<f64>]) -> f64 {
    orders(r)
        .iter()
        .enumerate()
        .map(|(i, &n)| complex_error(got[n - 1], r, stem, i))
        .fold(0.0, f64::max)
}

#[test]
fn coefficients_match_arb() {
    let mut mie = Mie::<f64>::new();
    let mut worst = Worst::new("a_n, b_n");
    for r in records("mie.jsonl", ",coefficients") {
        let (x, m) = (size_parameter(&r), refractive_index(&r));
        let s = mie.compute(x, m, &options(&r)).expect("inside the domain");
        let measures = value_measures(x, m, &reference(x, m, &[]), &orders(&r));
        for (k, (stem, got)) in [("a", s.a()), ("b", s.b())].into_iter().enumerate() {
            let err = array_error(&r, stem, got);
            worst.check(
                MieOutput::Coefficients,
                err,
                measures.coefficients[k],
                &r,
                stem,
            );
        }
    }
}

#[test]
fn efficiencies_and_amplitudes_match_arb() {
    let mut mie = Mie::<f64>::new();
    let mut worst = EFFICIENCY_NAMES.map(Worst::new);
    let mut worst_s = Worst::new("S_1, S_2");
    for r in records("mie.jsonl", ",series") {
        let (x, m) = (size_parameter(&r), refractive_index(&r));
        let s = mie
            .compute(x, m, &MieOptions::new())
            .expect("inside the domain");
        let q = s.efficiencies();
        let mu = r.input_f64s("mu");
        let measures = value_measures(x, m, &reference(x, m, &mu), &[1]);
        let got = [q.q_ext, q.q_sca, q.q_abs, q.q_back, q.g];
        for k in 0..5 {
            let name = EFFICIENCY_NAMES[k];
            if k == 2 && m.im == 0.0 {
                assert_eq!(
                    got[k], 0.0,
                    "{}: Q_abs must be exactly 0 for real m",
                    r.case
                );
                continue;
            }
            let err = error(got[k], &r, name, 0);
            worst[k].check(
                EFFICIENCY_OUTPUTS[k],
                err,
                measures.efficiencies[k],
                &r,
                name,
            );
        }
        let zero = Complex::new(0.0, 0.0);
        let (mut s1, mut s2) = (vec![zero; mu.len()], vec![zero; mu.len()]);
        s.amplitudes(
            &mu,
            AmplitudeBuffers {
                s1: &mut s1,
                s2: &mut s2,
            },
        )
        .expect("valid cosines");
        for j in 0..mu.len() {
            for (i, (stem, v)) in [("s1", s1[j]), ("s2", s2[j])].into_iter().enumerate() {
                let detail = format!("{stem} at mu = {}", mu[j]);
                let err = complex_error(v, &r, stem, j);
                let measure = measures.amplitudes[j][i];
                worst_s.check(MieOutput::Amplitudes, err, measure, &r, &detail);
            }
        }
    }
}

#[test]
fn derivatives_match_arb() {
    let mut mie = Mie::<f64>::new();
    let mut worst_c = Worst::new("coefficient derivatives");
    let mut worst_j = Worst::new("efficiency Jacobian");
    let mut worst_s = Worst::new("amplitude derivatives");
    let coefficient_records = records("mie_derivatives.jsonl", ",coefficients");
    for r in records("mie_derivatives.jsonl", ",series") {
        let (x, m) = (size_parameter(&r), refractive_index(&r));
        let case = r.case.trim_end_matches(",series");
        let c = coefficient_records
            .iter()
            .find(|c| c.case.trim_end_matches(",coefficients") == case)
            .expect("a coefficient record per case");
        let mu = r.input_f64s("mu");
        let d = reference(x, m, &mu);
        let measures = derivative_measures(x, m, &mu, &d, &orders(c));
        let s = mie
            .compute_with_derivatives(x, m, &options(c))
            .expect("inside the domain");
        let dc = s.derivatives().expect("computed");
        let arrays = [
            ("da_dx", dc.da_dx),
            ("da_dm", dc.da_dm),
            ("db_dx", dc.db_dx),
            ("db_dm", dc.db_dm),
        ];
        for (k, (stem, got)) in arrays.into_iter().enumerate() {
            let err = array_error(c, stem, got);
            let measure = measures.coefficients[k];
            worst_c.check(MieOutput::CoefficientDerivatives, err, measure, c, stem);
        }
        let jac = s.efficiencies_jacobian().expect("computed");
        for (p, (key, dq)) in [("dx", jac.x), ("dm_re", jac.m_re), ("dm_im", jac.m_im)]
            .into_iter()
            .enumerate()
        {
            let got = [dq.q_ext, dq.q_sca, dq.q_abs, dq.q_back, dq.g];
            for k in 0..5 {
                let name = format!("{}_{key}", EFFICIENCY_NAMES[k]);
                if k == 2 && m.im == 0.0 && p < 2 {
                    assert_eq!(
                        got[k], 0.0,
                        "{}: {name} must be exactly 0 for real m",
                        r.case
                    );
                    continue;
                }
                let err = error(got[k], &r, &name, 0);
                let measure = measures.jacobian[p][k];
                worst_j.check(MieOutput::EfficiencyJacobian, err, measure, &r, &name);
            }
        }
        let zero = Complex::new(0.0, 0.0);
        let mut v: [Vec<Complex<f64>>; 6] = std::array::from_fn(|_| vec![zero; mu.len()]);
        {
            let [s1, s2, dx1, dx2, dm1, dm2] = &mut v;
            s.amplitudes_with_derivatives(
                &mu,
                AmplitudeBuffers { s1, s2 },
                AmplitudeBuffers { s1: dx1, s2: dx2 },
                AmplitudeBuffers { s1: dm1, s2: dm2 },
            )
            .expect("valid cosines");
        }
        for (p, key) in ["dx", "dm"].into_iter().enumerate() {
            for (i, stem) in ["s1", "s2"].into_iter().enumerate() {
                let name = format!("{stem}_{key}");
                let err = (0..mu.len())
                    .map(|j| complex_error(v[2 + 2 * p + i][j], &r, &name, j))
                    .fold(0.0, f64::max);
                let measure = measures.amplitudes[p][i];
                worst_s.check(MieOutput::AmplitudeDerivatives, err, measure, &r, &name);
            }
        }
    }
}

/// `DoubleDouble` stands in for the exact values in the accuracy map, so it must be accurate
/// far beyond `f64`: the efficiencies within 16·(κ + cond)·2⁻¹⁰⁴ in their measures.
#[test]
fn double_double_matches_arb() {
    let u2 = 2f64.powi(-104);
    let mut worst = 0.0f64;
    let mut at = String::new();
    for r in records("mie.jsonl", ",series") {
        let (x, m) = (size_parameter(&r), refractive_index(&r));
        let d = reference(x, m, &[]);
        let measures = value_measures(x, m, &d, &[1]);
        let kappa = ValidatedDomain::condition_factor(x, m);
        let got = [d.q.q_ext, d.q.q_sca, d.q.q_abs, d.q.q_back, d.q.g];
        for k in [0, 1, 3, 4] {
            let measure = measures.efficiencies[k];
            let name = EFFICIENCY_NAMES[k];
            let err = r.output(name).dd_error(0, got[k].hi(), got[k].lo());
            let c = err / measure.scale / ((kappa + measure.condition) * u2);
            if c > worst {
                worst = c;
                at = format!("{} {name}", r.case);
            }
            assert!(c <= 16.0, "{} {name}: C = {c:.2}", r.case);
        }
    }
    eprintln!("DoubleDouble efficiencies  worst C = {worst:8.3} at {at}");
}

Create `scatrs/tests/mie_accuracy_map.rs`:

````rust
//! The accuracy map of the Mie outputs: `f64` against `DoubleDouble` on a dense grid over the
//! validated domain, every output within its declared tolerance.
//!
//! The grid: x = d·10^e for the mantissas `MANTISSAS` and e = −6, …, 4, up to 2·10⁴, 104 values (decimal
//! values, so the same doubles on every platform: a libm-dependent grid could land next to a
//! sharp resonance on one platform only), against the refractive indices `INDICES`, with the
//! amplitudes at `cosines()`. `DoubleDouble` is within a few (κ + cond)·2⁻¹⁰⁴ of Arb
//! (tests/mie_reference.rs), so it stands in for the exact values.
//!
//! Each error is reported as C = error/((κ′ + cond)·2⁻⁵²), the constant its tolerance would
//! need; the test prints the worst C of each output. With `SCATRS_ACCURACY_MAP=path` it also
//! writes every C as JSON, which the documentation plots (docs/validation/accuracy.md):
//!
//! ```text
//! SCATRS_ACCURACY_MAP=$PWD/docs/validation/data/mie-accuracy-map.json \
//!     cargo test -p scatrs --release --test mie_accuracy_map -- --nocapture
//! ```

mod common;

use std::fmt::Write as _;
use std::sync::atomic::{AtomicUsize, Ordering};

use common::mie::{Measure, Outputs, derivative_measures, q_of, reference, solve, value_measures};
use num_complex::Complex;
use scatrs::mie::{MieOutput, VALIDATED_DOMAIN, ValidatedDomain};
use scatrs::scalar::DoubleDouble as Dd;

/// About ten size parameters per decade.
const MANTISSAS: [&str; 10] = ["1", "1.25", "1.6", "2", "2.5", "3.2", "4", "5", "6.4", "8"];

/// (Re m, Im m): near 1, aerosol and water, high index, strong absorption and metal-like, at
/// the edges of the domain.
const INDICES: [(f64, f64); 24] = [
    (1.0001, 0.0),
    (1.001, 1e-6),
    (1.01, 0.0),
    (1.1, 1e-3),
    (1.33, 0.0),
    (1.33, 1e-8),
    (1.33, 1e-4),
    (1.5, 0.0),
    (1.5, 1e-2),
    (1.5, 0.1),
    (1.5, 1.0),
    (1.5, 10.0),
    (1.75, 0.44),
    (2.0, 0.0),
    (2.5, 1.0),
    (3.0, 1e-3),
    (4.0, 0.01),
    (5.0, 5.0),
    (8.0, 0.1),
    (10.0, 0.0),
    (10.0, 10.0),
    (0.5, 2.0),
    (0.2, 3.0),
    (0.05, 4.0),
];

/// The columns of the map.
const OUTPUTS: [(&str, MieOutput); 10] = [
    ("coefficients", MieOutput::Coefficients),
    ("q_ext", MieOutput::Extinction),
    ("q_sca", MieOutput::Scattering),
    ("q_abs", MieOutput::Absorption),
    ("q_back", MieOutput::Backscattering),
    ("g", MieOutput::Asymmetry),
    ("amplitudes", MieOutput::Amplitudes),
    ("coefficient_derivatives", MieOutput::CoefficientDerivatives),
    ("efficiency_jacobian", MieOutput::EfficiencyJacobian),
    ("amplitude_derivatives", MieOutput::AmplitudeDerivatives),
];

fn size_parameters() -> Vec<f64> {
    (-6..=4)
        .flat_map(|e| MANTISSAS.iter().map(move |d| format!("{d}e{e}")))
        .map(|s| s.parse::<f64>().expect("a decimal literal"))
        .filter(|&x| x <= VALIDATED_DOMAIN.x_max)
        .collect()
}

/// −1 + k/16 for k = 0, …, 32, and 1 − 2^-j for j = 6, 8, …, 20 in the forward peak: all
/// exact doubles.
fn cosines() -> Vec<f64> {
    let uniform = (0..=32).map(|k| -1.0 + f64::from(k) / 16.0);
    let forward = (3..=10).map(|j| 1.0 - 2f64.powi(-2 * j));
    uniform.chain(forward).collect()
}

fn diff(a: f64, b: Dd) -> f64 {
    (Dd::from_f64(a) - b).hi().abs()
}

fn cdiff(a: Complex<f64>, b: Complex<Dd>) -> f64 {
    diff(a.re, b.re).hypot(diff(a.im, b.im))
}

/// max over n < `f.len()` of |f − d|.
fn array_error(f: &[Complex<f64>], d: &[Complex<Dd>]) -> f64 {
    f.iter()
        .zip(d)
        .map(|(&a, &b)| cdiff(a, b))
        .fold(0.0, f64::max)
}

/// C of every output at (x, m), ordered as `OUTPUTS`.
fn point(x: f64, m: Complex<f64>, mu: &[f64]) -> [f64; 10] {
    let f: Outputs<f64> = solve(x, m, mu, None);
    let d = reference(x, m, mu);
    let orders: Vec<usize> = (1..=f.a.len()).collect();
    let v = value_measures(x, m, &d, &orders);
    let dm = derivative_measures(x, m, mu, &d, &orders);
    let c = |output: MieOutput, err: f64, measure: Measure| {
        let tol = VALIDATED_DOMAIN.tolerance(output, x, m, measure.condition);
        err / measure.scale / tol * ValidatedDomain::constant(output)
    };
    let mut out = [0.0f64; 10];
    let mut put = |k: usize, value: f64| out[k] = out[k].max(value);
    put(
        0,
        c(
            MieOutput::Coefficients,
            array_error(&f.a, &d.a),
            v.coefficients[0],
        ),
    );
    put(
        0,
        c(
            MieOutput::Coefficients,
            array_error(&f.b, &d.b),
            v.coefficients[1],
        ),
    );
    for k in 0..5 {
        let output = OUTPUTS[1 + k].1;
        if k == 2 && m.im == 0.0 {
            // Q_abs is exactly 0 for real m.
            put(3, if f.q.q_abs == 0.0 { 0.0 } else { f64::INFINITY });
            continue;
        }
        put(
            1 + k,
            c(
                output,
                diff(q_of(&f.q, k), q_of(&d.q, k)),
                v.efficiencies[k],
            ),
        );
    }
    for j in 0..mu.len() {
        for i in 0..2 {
            let err = cdiff(f.s[i][j], d.s[i][j]);
            put(6, c(MieOutput::Amplitudes, err, v.amplitudes[j][i]));
        }
    }
    for k in 0..4 {
        let err = array_error(&f.d[k], &d.d[k]);
        put(
            7,
            c(MieOutput::CoefficientDerivatives, err, dm.coefficients[k]),
        );
    }
    for p in 0..3 {
        for k in 0..5 {
            if k == 2 && m.im == 0.0 && p < 2 {
                let exact = q_of(&f.jac[p], k) == 0.0;
                put(8, if exact { 0.0 } else { f64::INFINITY });
                continue;
            }
            let err = diff(q_of(&f.jac[p], k), q_of(&d.jac[p], k));
            put(8, c(MieOutput::EfficiencyJacobian, err, dm.jacobian[p][k]));
        }
    }
    for p in 0..2 {
        for i in 0..2 {
            let k = 2 + 2 * p + i;
            let err = (0..mu.len())
                .map(|j| cdiff(f.s[k][j], d.s[k][j]))
                .fold(0.0, f64::max);
            put(
                9,
                c(MieOutput::AmplitudeDerivatives, err, dm.amplitudes[p][i]),
            );
        }
    }
    out
}

fn to_json(xs: &[f64], results: &[[f64; 10]]) -> String {
    let list = |v: Vec<String>| v.join(", ");
    let mut json = String::from("{\n");
    let _ = writeln!(
        json,
        "  \"x\": [{}],",
        list(xs.iter().map(|x| format!("{x:e}")).collect())
    );
    let indices = INDICES
        .iter()
        .map(|(r, i)| format!("[{r:e}, {i:e}]"))
        .collect();
    let _ = writeln!(json, "  \"m\": [{}],", list(indices));
    let declared = OUTPUTS
        .iter()
        .map(|(n, o)| format!("\"{n}\": {}", ValidatedDomain::constant(*o)))
        .collect();
    let _ = writeln!(json, "  \"declared\": {{{}}},", list(declared));
    json += "  \"c\": {\n";
    for (o, (name, _)) in OUTPUTS.iter().enumerate() {
        let rows = (0..xs.len())
            .map(|i| {
                let row = (0..INDICES.len())
                    .map(|j| format!("{:.3e}", results[i * INDICES.len() + j][o]))
                    .collect();
                format!("[{}]", list(row))
            })
            .collect();
        let comma = if o + 1 < OUTPUTS.len() { "," } else { "" };
        let _ = writeln!(json, "    \"{name}\": [{}]{comma}", list(rows));
    }
    json + "  }\n}\n"
}

#[test]
fn accuracy_map_is_within_the_declared_tolerances() {
    let xs = size_parameters();
    let mu = cosines();
    let points: Vec<(f64, Complex<f64>)> = xs
        .iter()
        .flat_map(|&x| INDICES.iter().map(move |&(r, i)| (x, Complex::new(r, i))))
        .collect();
    let next = AtomicUsize::new(0);
    let threads = std::thread::available_parallelism().map_or(1, |n| n.get());
    let mut results = vec![[0.0f64; 10]; points.len()];
    let done: Vec<Vec<(usize, [f64; 10])>> = std::thread::scope(|scope| {
        let workers: Vec<_> = (0..threads)
            .map(|_| {
                scope.spawn(|| {
                    let mut mine = Vec::new();
                    loop {
                        let k = next.fetch_add(1, Ordering::Relaxed);
                        let Some(&(x, m)) = points.get(k) else {
                            break mine;
                        };
                        mine.push((k, point(x, m, &mu)));
                    }
                })
            })
            .collect();
        workers
            .into_iter()
            .map(|w| w.join().expect("worker"))
            .collect()
    });
    for (k, c) in done.into_iter().flatten() {
        results[k] = c;
    }
    if let Ok(path) = std::env::var("SCATRS_ACCURACY_MAP") {
        std::fs::write(&path, to_json(&xs, &results)).expect("write the map");
        eprintln!("wrote {path}");
    }
    let mut failures = Vec::new();
    for (o, (name, output)) in OUTPUTS.iter().enumerate() {
        let (k, worst) = results
            .iter()
            .enumerate()
            .map(|(k, c)| (k, c[o]))
            .fold((0, 0.0), |a, b| if b.1 > a.1 { b } else { a });
        let declared = ValidatedDomain::constant(*output);
        let (x, m) = points[k];
        eprintln!("{name:<24} worst C = {worst:8.3} (declared {declared}) at x = {x:e}, m = {m}");
        if worst.is_nan() || worst > declared {
            failures.push(*name);
        }
    }
    assert!(
        failures.is_empty(),
        "outside the declared tolerance: {failures:?}"
    );
}
````
