```rust
//! The validated domain of the Mie outputs and their declared accuracy.

use core::fmt;

use num_complex::Complex;

/// The outputs whose accuracy [`ValidatedDomain::tolerance`] declares, with the error measure
/// each tolerance bounds.
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Hash)]
#[non_exhaustive]
pub enum MieOutput {
    /// aₙ and bₙ: max over n of |error|, divided by max over n of |aₙ| (and likewise bₙ).
    Coefficients,
    /// Each of ∂aₙ/∂x, daₙ/dm, ∂bₙ/∂x and dbₙ/dm, measured as [`Coefficients`](Self::Coefficients).
    CoefficientDerivatives,
    /// Q_ext, relative error.
    Extinction,
    /// Q_sca, relative error.
    Scattering,
    /// Q_abs, relative error (exactly 0 for real m).
    Absorption,
    /// Q_back: |error| / max(Q_back, Q_sca). Backscattering by large weakly refracting spheres
    /// is many orders of magnitude below Q_sca, and its relative accuracy is limited by the
    /// cancellation in its series.
    Backscattering,
    /// g: |error| / max(|g|, 10⁻³ min(1, x²)).
    Asymmetry,
    /// S₁ and S₂: |error| / max(|S|, S_rms), where S_rms = x√Q_sca/2 is the RMS amplitude, so
    /// the deep minima of the angular pattern are measured against its typical size.
    Amplitudes,
    /// Each ∂Q/∂p of [`EfficiencyJacobian`](super::EfficiencyJacobian): |error| /
    /// max(|∂Q/∂p|, Q′·(1 + |m|x)/|p|), with |p| = x for x and |m| for Re m and Im m, and the
    /// size Q′ of Q in the measure of its value (Q_ext for Q_abs: relative to Q_abs itself,
    /// ∂Q_abs/∂x and ∂Q_abs/∂(Re m) of weakly absorbing spheres can be less accurate, by up to
    /// Q_ext/Q_abs). Q·(1 + |m|x)/|p| is the natural size of a derivative: the outputs vary
    /// with the phase |m|x.
    EfficiencyJacobian,
    /// ∂S/∂x and dS/dm: max over μ of |error| / max over μ of
    /// max(|∂S/∂p|, S_rms·(1 + |m|x)/|p|).
    AmplitudeDerivatives,
}

/// The region of inputs whose accuracy has been validated, and the declared tolerances there.
///
/// Inside it, every output of [`Mie::compute`](super::Mie::compute) in `f64`, with the default
/// truncation or more terms, is within
///
/// C·(κ′(x, m) + cond)·2⁻⁵²
///
/// of the exact value for the exact inputs, in the measure of its [`MieOutput`]
/// ([`tolerance`](Self::tolerance)). Here
///
/// * cond is the output's relative condition number with respect to x and m in that measure:
///   (x·|∂y/∂x| + |m|·|∇ₘy|)/s, where s is the denominator of the measure, |∇ₘy| is
///   √((∂y/∂Re m)² + (∂y/∂Im m)²) (= |dy/dm| for the holomorphic aₙ, bₙ, S₁, S₂), and for
///   the measures over an array the numerator is the largest over the array. It is large near
///   the sharp resonances of weakly absorbing spheres, where no algorithm can do better, since
///   rounding x and m to `f64` already perturbs the outputs by cond·2⁻⁵³.
/// * κ′(x, m) is [`condition_factor`](Self::condition_factor) for the values, and that times
///   max(1, |m|/|m − 1|) for the derivatives. It covers the errors that cond does not: the
///   cancellation in the series (in Q_back, the alternating sum is up to x times smaller than
///   its terms) and the orders' separate losses near m = 1.
/// * C is the output's constant ([`constant`](Self::constant)).
///
/// The constants are measured, at least 4 times the worst case found: at every Arb reference
/// value in the domain, and against double-double on a dense grid (the accuracy map in the
/// documentation). Tests enforce both in CI.
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq)]
#[non_exhaustive]
pub struct ValidatedDomain {
    /// The smallest validated size parameter.
    pub x_min: f64,
    /// The largest validated size parameter.
    pub x_max: f64,
    /// The largest validated Re m (the smallest is any value above 0).
    pub m_re_max: f64,
    /// The largest validated Im m (the smallest is 0).
    pub m_im_max: f64,
}

/// The validated domain: x ∈ [10⁻⁶, 2·10⁴], 0 < Re m ≤ 10, 0 ≤ Im m ≤ 10.
pub const VALIDATED_DOMAIN: ValidatedDomain = ValidatedDomain {
    x_min: 1e-6,
    x_max: 2e4,
    m_re_max: 10.0,
    m_im_max: 10.0,
};

impl ValidatedDomain {
    /// Whether (x, m) is inside the domain.
    pub fn contains(&self, x: f64, m: Complex<f64>) -> bool {
        (self.x_min..=self.x_max).contains(&x)
            && m.re > 0.0
            && m.re <= self.m_re_max
            && (0.0..=self.m_im_max).contains(&m.im)
    }

    /// κ(x, m) = (1 + |m|x)·max(1, |m|/|m − 1|): infinite for m = 1, where the coefficients
    /// vanish.
    pub fn condition_factor(x: f64, m: Complex<f64>) -> f64 {
        (1.0 + m.norm() * x) * near_one(m)
    }

    /// The declared bound on the error of `output` at (x, m): C·(κ′ + `condition`)·2⁻⁵², in
    /// the measure of [`MieOutput`] (see the type documentation for κ′ and the condition
    /// number). With `condition` = 0 this is the a-priori part alone, which the accuracy map
    /// shows to suffice away from sharp resonances.
    pub fn tolerance(&self, output: MieOutput, x: f64, m: Complex<f64>, condition: f64) -> f64 {
        let derivative = matches!(
            output,
            MieOutput::CoefficientDerivatives
                | MieOutput::EfficiencyJacobian
                | MieOutput::AmplitudeDerivatives
        );
        let extra = if derivative { near_one(m) } else { 1.0 };
        let prior = Self::condition_factor(x, m) * extra;
        Self::constant(output) * (prior + condition) * f64::EPSILON
    }

    /// The constant C of [`tolerance`](Self::tolerance).
    ///
    /// | output | C |
    /// |---|---|
    /// | coefficients, efficiencies, g | 16 |
    /// | derivatives of S₁, S₂ | 32 |
    /// | S₁, S₂ | 64 |
    /// | efficiency Jacobian | 128 |
    /// | coefficient derivatives | 256 |
    pub fn constant(output: MieOutput) -> f64 {
        match output {
            MieOutput::Coefficients
            | MieOutput::Extinction
            | MieOutput::Scattering
            | MieOutput::Absorption
            | MieOutput::Backscattering
            | MieOutput::Asymmetry => 16.0,
            MieOutput::AmplitudeDerivatives => 32.0,
            MieOutput::Amplitudes => 64.0,
            MieOutput::EfficiencyJacobian => 128.0,
            MieOutput::CoefficientDerivatives => 256.0,
        }
    }
}

/// max(1, |m|/|m − 1|).
fn near_one(m: Complex<f64>) -> f64 {
    (m.norm() / (m - Complex::new(1.0, 0.0)).norm()).max(1.0)
}

impl fmt::Display for ValidatedDomain {
    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> fmt::Result {
        write!(
            f,
            "x in [{:e}, {:e}], 0 < Re m <= {}, 0 <= Im m <= {}",
            self.x_min, self.x_max, self.m_re_max, self.m_im_max
        )
    }
}
```

Create `docs/theory/mie.md`:

```markdown
# Homogeneous spheres (Lorenz–Mie)

This page derives the methods behind `scatrs::mie` (Python: `scatrs.mie`): the series
and its truncation, the recurrences, the cancellation-free forms of the absorption and the
derivatives, the accuracy model, and the rigorous reference values. The rustdoc of
`scatrs::mie` is the authoritative statement of the contracts; the figures here repeat it.

## Conventions and the series

<!-- synced with scatrs/src/mie/mod.rs rustdoc -->

scatrs follows Bohren & Huffman (1983), with time dependence $e^{-i\omega t}$: the refractive
index relative to the medium is $m = n + ik$ with $k \ge 0$ for absorbing spheres, and the size
parameter is $x = 2\pi r/\lambda$. Codes that use $e^{+i\omega t}$, such as Wiscombe's MIEV0,
report the complex conjugates of $a_n$, $b_n$, $S_1$ and $S_2$.

With the Riccati–Bessel functions $\psi_n(\rho) = \rho j_n(\rho)$,
$\chi_n(\rho) = -\rho y_n(\rho)$, $\xi_n = \psi_n - i\chi_n$, and the logarithmic derivative
$D_n = \psi_n'/\psi_n$, the coefficients are

$$a_n = \frac{A_n\psi_n(x) - \psi_{n-1}(x)}{A_n\xi_n(x) - \xi_{n-1}(x)}, \qquad
b_n = \frac{B_n\psi_n(x) - \psi_{n-1}(x)}{B_n\xi_n(x) - \xi_{n-1}(x)},$$

where $A_n = D_n(mx)/m + n/x$ and $B_n = m\,D_n(mx) + n/x$. Call the denominators
$\Delta_a$ and $\Delta_b$. Two identities, valid for all $n$ and real $x$, carry the rest of
this page:

$$\text{(Wronskian)}\quad \psi_n\xi_n' - \psi_n'\xi_n = i, \qquad
\text{(Casoratian)}\quad \psi_n\chi_{n-1} - \psi_{n-1}\chi_n = -1 .$$

The efficiencies (cross sections divided by $\pi r^2$), the asymmetry parameter and the
amplitudes are

$$Q_\text{sca} = \frac{2}{x^2}\sum_n (2n+1)\bigl(\lvert a_n\rvert^2 + \lvert b_n\rvert^2\bigr),
\qquad Q_\text{ext} = \frac{2}{x^2}\sum_n (2n+1)\,\mathrm{Re}(a_n + b_n),$$

$$Q_\text{back} = \frac{1}{x^2}\Bigl\lvert\sum_n (2n+1)(-1)^n(a_n - b_n)\Bigr\rvert^2, \qquad
g = \frac{4}{x^2 Q_\text{sca}}\sum_n\Bigl[\tfrac{n(n+2)}{n+1}\mathrm{Re}\bigl(a_n a_{n+1}^* +
b_n b_{n+1}^*\bigr) + \tfrac{2n+1}{n(n+1)}\mathrm{Re}(a_n b_n^*)\Bigr],$$

$$S_1(\mu) = \sum_n \frac{2n+1}{n(n+1)}(a_n\pi_n + b_n\tau_n), \qquad
S_2(\mu) = \sum_n \frac{2n+1}{n(n+1)}(a_n\tau_n + b_n\pi_n),$$

with $\pi_n = P_n'(\mu)$ and $\tau_n = n(n+1)P_n(\mu) - \mu\pi_n$, computed by the upward
recurrences of Bohren & Huffman (Eq. 4.47).

## Truncation

<!-- synced with scatrs/src/mie/mod.rs rustdoc -->

`f64` keeps `ceil(x + 6 x**(1/3) + 3)` terms (`default_n_max`), and `DoubleDouble`
`ceil(x + 12 x**(1/3) + 6)`. Wiscombe's $\lceil x + 4.05x^{1/3} + 2\rceil$ (MIEV0, and
Bohren & Huffman's BHMIE) left truncation errors up to about $10^{-10}$ in $Q_\text{ext}$ and
$10^{-6}$ in $Q_\text{back}$ against the Arb references; the constants above were chosen from
the same measurements so that truncation errors are below rounding errors in each type.

Beyond $n \approx x$ the terms decay like $\exp\bigl(-\tfrac23(2t)^{3/2}\bigr)$ with
$n = x + t\,x^{1/3}$ — except at the whispering-gallery resonances of spheres with
$\mathrm{Re}\,m > 1$ and small $\mathrm{Im}\,m$, at orders $x < n < \mathrm{Re}(m)\,x$, where a
single $\lvert a_n\rvert$ can approach 1. Resonances above $N$ are exponentially narrow, so a
size parameter must fall within an exponentially small window to meet one; but no truncation
rule of this form can exclude them. The rule is therefore validated by the accuracy maps
rather than proved, and `n_max` (Python `n_max=`) adds terms where a calculation must
resolve them. The reference values, by contrast, are infinite-series values with a proven
bound on the tail (below).

## Logarithmic derivatives

Let $u_k(\rho) = \psi_k(\rho)/\psi_{k-1}(\rho)$. The recurrence
$\psi_{k-1} + \psi_{k+1} = \tfrac{2k+1}{\rho}\psi_k$ gives

$$u_k = \frac{1}{c_k - u_{k+1}}, \qquad c_k = \frac{2k+1}{\rho}, \qquad
D_n(\rho) = \frac{n+1}{\rho} - u_{n+1}(\rho).$$

**Lemma 1 (enclosure).** If $\lvert c_j\rvert \ge 2$ for all $j > k$, then
$\lvert u_j\rvert \le 1$ for all $j > k$, and $\lvert u_k\rvert \le 1/(\lvert c_k\rvert - 1)$.

*Proof.* The map $T_j(u) = 1/(c_j - u)$ sends the closed unit disk into itself when
$\lvert c_j\rvert \ge 2$, since $\lvert c_j - u\rvert \ge \lvert c_j\rvert - 1 \ge 1$. $\psi$ is
the minimal solution of the recurrence as $k \to \infty$ ($j_k(\rho) \sim
\rho^k/(2k+1)!!$ while $y_k(\rho) \sim (2k-1)!!/\rho^{k+1}$), so by Pincherle's theorem
(Gautschi 1967) $u_j$ is the limit of the approximants $T_j \circ T_{j+1} \circ \cdots \circ
T_{j+K}(0)$, all of which lie in the disk; so does the limit. Then
$\lvert u_k\rvert = 1/\lvert c_k - u_{k+1}\rvert \le 1/(\lvert c_k\rvert - 1)$. ∎

scatrs runs the recurrence downward from $u = 0$ at an index
$k_0 = \max(N + 1, \lceil 1.5\lvert\rho\rvert\rceil)$ plus a margin of half the bits of the
type, plus 4. Above $1.5\lvert\rho\rvert$, $\lvert c_k\rvert \ge 3$, so both the true value
and the iterate stay in the disk of radius $\tfrac12$, where $T_k$ contracts distances by
at least $(\lvert c_k\rvert - \tfrac12)^2 \ge 6.25$: the start value is forgotten to rounding
level before the first stored order. There is no continued fraction to converge, so no
convergence test and no failure mode. An exactly zero denominator (at an exact zero of
$\psi_{k-1}$, a pole of $D_{k-1}$) is replaced by a rounding-level value, as in Lentz's
method. For real $m$ the real recurrence is used: twice as fast, and for $m = 1$ it reproduces
$u_k(x)$ bit for bit, so $a_n = b_n = 0$ exactly.

## Riccati–Bessel functions: a hybrid scheme

The upward recurrence for $\psi_n(x)$ starts with $\psi_1 = \sin x/x - \cos x$, which cancels
catastrophically for small $x$ (relative error about $\varepsilon/x^2$, so $2\cdot10^{-4}$ at
$x = 10^{-6}$), and for $n > x$ the upward recurrence of $\psi_n$, the minimal solution, is
unstable. MIEV0 handles small spheres with a separate small-particle expansion, accurate to
about $10^{-8}$. scatrs uses one scheme for every $x$:

- for $n \le n_s = \lfloor x\rfloor$ (only when $x \ge 1$): $\psi_n$ and $\chi_n$ by upward
  recurrence, where both are of order one, and $a_n$, $b_n$ from the formulas above;
- for $n > n_s$: the ratios $u_n(x)$ (downward, as above; $\psi_n(x)$ has no zeros for
  $n > x$, so $u_n(x) > 0$), $R_n = \xi_{n-1}/\xi_n$ upward by
  $1/R_n = (2n-1)/x - R_{n-1}$ (stable: $\lvert\xi_n\rvert$ increases with $n$),
  $T_n = \psi_n/\xi_n = T_{n-1}\,u_n(x)\,R_n$ and $1/\xi_n = R_n/\xi_{n-1}$, and

$$a_n = T_n\frac{N_a}{A_n - R_n}, \quad b_n = T_n\frac{N_b}{B_n - R_n}, \qquad
N_a = \frac{n+1}{x}\Bigl(\frac{1}{m^2} - 1\Bigr) + u_{n+1}(x) - \frac{u_{n+1}(mx)}{m}, \quad
N_b = u_{n+1}(x) - m\,u_{n+1}(mx).$$

$N_a = D_n(mx)/m - D_n(x)$ and $N_b = m D_n(mx) - D_n(x)$ (substitute
$D_n = (n+1)/\rho - u_{n+1}$): the terms of size $n/x$ that cancel are removed exactly. Without
this, $b_n$ lost all its digits at $x = 10^{-6}$. $T_n$ and $1/\xi_n$ underflow gracefully to 0
as $n$ grows, so any `n_max` is safe.

The small-particle expansion is not needed, and the accuracy maps confirm the scheme across
the domain. The coefficient $1/m^2 - 1 = -(m-1)(m+1)/m^2$ is formed from $m - 1$, which is
exact for $m$ near 1.

## Absorption without cancellation

<!-- synced with scatrs/src/mie/efficiencies.rs rustdoc -->

**Proposition 1.** $\mathrm{Re}\,a_n - \lvert a_n\rvert^2 = -\mathrm{Im}\bigl(D_n(mx)/m\bigr)
/\lvert\Delta_a\rvert^2$, and $\mathrm{Re}\,b_n - \lvert b_n\rvert^2 =
-\mathrm{Im}\bigl(m D_n(mx)\bigr)/\lvert\Delta_b\rvert^2$.

*Proof.* $\mathrm{Re}\,a - \lvert a\rvert^2 = \lvert a\rvert^2(\mathrm{Re}(1/a) - 1)$, and
$1/a_n - 1 = (\Delta_a - \text{numerator})/\text{numerator} = -i(A_n\chi_n -
\chi_{n-1})/(A_n\psi_n - \psi_{n-1})$ since $\xi = \psi - i\chi$. So
$\mathrm{Re}(1/a_n) - 1 = \mathrm{Im} f(A_n)$ with $f(A) = (A\chi_n - \chi_{n-1})/(A\psi_n -
\psi_{n-1})$, a Möbius map with real coefficients, for which
$\mathrm{Im} f(A) = \det\cdot\,\mathrm{Im} A/\lvert A\psi_n - \psi_{n-1}\rvert^2$ with
$\det = \psi_n\chi_{n-1} - \psi_{n-1}\chi_n = -1$ (the Casoratian). Multiplying by
$\lvert a_n\rvert^2 = \lvert\text{numerator}\rvert^2/\lvert\Delta_a\rvert^2$ and using
$\mathrm{Im}A_n = \mathrm{Im}(D_n/m)$ gives the result; $b_n$ likewise. ∎

So scatrs sums $Q_\text{abs} = \frac{2}{x^2}\sum(2n+1)\bigl[-\mathrm{Im}(D_n/m)/
\lvert\Delta_a\rvert^2 - \mathrm{Im}(mD_n)/\lvert\Delta_b\rvert^2\bigr]$ directly: accurate
relative to itself however small, exactly 0 for real $m$, and $Q_\text{ext} = Q_\text{sca} +
Q_\text{abs}$. Computing $Q_\text{abs}$ as $Q_\text{ext} - Q_\text{sca}$ loses about
$\log_{10}(Q_\text{ext}/Q_\text{abs})$ digits — 8 digits of the co-albedo at $k = 10^{-8}$ —
and the usual $Q_\text{ext}$ sum of $\mathrm{Re}(a_n + b_n)$ is itself inaccurate for small
non-absorbing spheres, where $\mathrm{Re}\,a_n = \lvert a_n\rvert^2 \ll \lvert a_n\rvert$.
The optical theorem $Q_\text{ext} = (4/x^2)\,\mathrm{Re}\,S(0)$ remains a test.

## Closed-form derivatives

<!-- synced with scatrs/src/mie/mod.rs rustdoc -->

**Proposition 2.** With $z = mx$, $D = D_n(z)$ and $D' = n(n+1)/z^2 - 1 - D^2$,

$$\frac{\partial a_n}{\partial m} = \frac{i\,(D - zD')}{m^2\Delta_a^2}, \quad
\frac{\partial b_n}{\partial m} = \frac{-i\,(D + zD')}{\Delta_b^2}, \quad
\frac{\partial a_n}{\partial x} = \frac{i\,(m^2-1)\bigl(n(n+1)/x^2 + D^2\bigr)}{m^2\Delta_a^2},
\quad \frac{\partial b_n}{\partial x} = \frac{i\,(m^2-1)}{\Delta_b^2}.$$

*Proof (for $\partial a_n/\partial m$; the others are alike).* Write
$a_n = (m\psi_n' - D\psi_n)/(m\xi_n' - D\xi_n)$ (Bohren & Huffman's form divided by
$\psi_n(mx)$), whose denominator is $-m\Delta_a$. Only $D$ and the explicit $m$ depend on
$m$, with $\partial D/\partial m = xD'$ (the Riccati equation of $D$). The quotient rule gives
the numerator $(D - mxD')(\psi_n\xi_n' - \psi_n'\xi_n) = i(D - zD')$ by the Wronskian; all
other terms cancel. For $\partial/\partial x$, $\psi_n'' = (n(n+1)/x^2 - 1)\psi_n$ and
$\partial D/\partial x = mD'$ reduce the numerator to
$(m^2 - 1)(n(n+1)/x^2 + D^2)$ times the Wronskian. ∎

Each costs a few complex operations per order on top of the values. $a_n$ and $b_n$ are
holomorphic in $m$, so $\partial/\partial(\mathrm{Re}\,m) = d/dm$ and
$\partial/\partial(\mathrm{Im}\,m) = i\,d/dm$. At small $z$ the leading terms of $D + zD'$
cancel (it is $O(z)$ while $D \approx (n+1)/z$); scatrs evaluates, with $u = u_{n+1}(z)$,

$$D' = (n+1)\Bigl(\frac{2u}{z} - \frac{1}{z^2}\Bigr) - (1 + u^2), \qquad
D + zD' = (2n+1)u - z(1 + u^2), \qquad D - zD' = 2D - (D + zD').$$

The Jacobian of $Q_\text{abs}$ differentiates the terms of Proposition 1:
$\partial_p\bigl(-\mathrm{Im} f/\lvert\Delta\rvert^2\bigr) = -\bigl[\mathrm{Im}\,\partial_p f
- 2\,\mathrm{Im} f\,\mathrm{Re}(\partial_p\Delta/\Delta)\bigr]/\lvert\Delta\rvert^2$ with
$f = D/m$ or $mD$, $\partial\Delta_a/\partial x = \xi_n(D' - n/x^2 - nA_n/x + 1) +
\xi_{n-1}D/m$ and $\partial\Delta_a/\partial m = \xi_n\,\partial A_n/\partial m$ (and
likewise for $b_n$). The other Jacobians follow by the chain and product rules. `Dual`
numbers, propagated through the same recurrences, are the test oracle: their values agree
bitwise with `f64`, and their derivatives with the closed forms to within the rounding
errors of the two routes.

(mie-accuracy-model)=
## The accuracy model

<!-- synced with scatrs/src/mie/domain.rs rustdoc -->

Inside the validated domain ($10^{-6} \le x \le 2\cdot10^4$, $0 < \mathrm{Re}\,m \le 10$,
$0 \le \mathrm{Im}\,m \le 10$) every `f64` output is within

$$C\,(\kappa' + \mathrm{cond})\,2^{-52}$$

of the exact value for the exact inputs, in the error measure of its output
(`MieOutput`; for example $\lvert\text{error}\rvert/\max(\lvert S\rvert, S_\text{rms})$ for
the amplitudes, with $S_\text{rms} = x\sqrt{Q_\text{sca}}/2$). The two condition terms are:

- **cond**, the output's relative condition number with respect to $x$ and $m$:
  $(x\lvert\partial y/\partial x\rvert + \lvert m\rvert\,\lvert\nabla_m y\rvert)/s$, with $s$
  the denominator of the measure. Rounding $x$ and $m$ to `f64` already perturbs the output
  by about $\mathrm{cond}\cdot2^{-53}$, so no algorithm can avoid it. It is large near the
  sharp resonances of weakly absorbing spheres.
- **κ′**, an a-priori factor: $\kappa(x, m) = (1 + \lvert m\rvert x)
  \max(1, \lvert m\rvert/\lvert m - 1\rvert)$ for the values, times
  $\max(1, \lvert m\rvert/\lvert m - 1\rvert)$ again for the derivatives. It covers what cond
  does not: the cancellation in the series (the alternating sum of $Q_\text{back}$ can be $x$
  times smaller than its terms) and the separate losses of each order near $m = 1$, where
  $a_n, b_n \propto m - 1$.

The constants C are measured, with a margin of at least 4 over the worst case found at every
Arb reference point and on a dense double-double grid: 16 for the coefficients, efficiencies
and $g$; 64 for $S_1$, $S_2$; 32 for their derivatives; 128 for the efficiency Jacobian; 256
for the coefficient derivatives. See {doc}`/validation/accuracy` for the map, and for why a
model with κ alone (or cond alone) fails.

## Reference values

<!-- synced with reference/scatrs_reference/generators/mie.py docstrings -->

The generator `reference/scatrs_reference/generators/mie.py` computes every value in Arb ball
arithmetic, at the exact double inputs, without Arb's Bessel functions (which lose almost all
their bits at orders in the thousands). It uses the recurrences above, made rigorous:

- $u_k(\rho)$ is seeded by Lemma 1, with the ball $\lvert u\rvert \le 1$, at
  $k_0 \ge 2\lvert\rho\rvert$; every step from there shrinks the uncertainty at least ninefold.
- Complex recurrences use **disk arithmetic** (`scatrs_reference/disk.py`): Arb's complex
  balls are rectangles, which a recurrence that multiplies by numbers of modulus near 1
  rotates and Arb must enclose in larger rectangles — 1.5 times larger per step in the Mie
  recurrences, useless after a few hundred orders. A disk (exact centre, rigorous radius) does
  not grow under rotation.
- $\pi_n$, $\tau_n$ come from Arb's Legendre polynomials, which are accurate at any order.

The precision starts at 320 bits and doubles until every stored value's radius is below
$2^{-200}$ of its scale. Coefficients and their derivatives are finite computations, so
**certified**. The series are summed to $N_\text{ref}$ terms and certified by the following
bound; where that would need more than 60 000 terms ($\lvert m\rvert x$ above about
$6\cdot10^4$) they are summed to $\lceil x + 18x^{1/3} + 32\rceil$ terms and labelled
**high-precision**. Series of derivatives have no tail bound yet and are high-precision.

### Series tail bound

**Proposition 3.** Let $N \ge \lvert m\rvert x$ and $N \ge 1.5x - \tfrac12$, and suppose
$\lvert R_N\rvert \le 1$ (checked in the computation). With

$$U_z = \frac{1}{(2N+3)/\lvert mx\rvert - 1}, \quad U_x = \frac{1}{(2N+3)/x - 1}, \quad
\bar R = \frac{1}{(2N+1)/x - 1}, \quad q = U_x\bar R,$$

every $n > N$ satisfies $\lvert a_n\rvert + \lvert b_n\rvert \le
\lvert T_N\rvert(F_a + F_b)\,q^{\,n-N}$, where

$$F_a = \frac{(N+2)c_1 + e_1}{(N+1)c_2 - e_2} + \frac{c_1}{c_2}, \qquad
F_b = \frac{U_x + \lvert m\rvert U_z}{(2N+3)/x - \lvert m\rvert U_z - \bar R},$$

$c_1 = \lvert 1/m^2 - 1\rvert/x$, $e_1 = U_z/\lvert m\rvert + U_x$,
$c_2 = \lvert 1 + 1/m^2\rvert/x$, $e_2 = 1/(\lvert m\rvert^2x) + U_z/\lvert m\rvert + \bar R$,
whenever both denominators are positive.

*Proof.* By Lemma 1 (with $\lvert c_j\rvert = (2j+1)/\lvert\rho\rvert \ge 2$ for $j \ge N + 1$),
$\lvert u_k(mx)\rvert \le U_z$ and $0 < u_k(x) \le U_x$ for $k > N$. From
$1/R_n = (2n-1)/x - R_{n-1}$ and $\lvert R_N\rvert \le 1$, induction gives
$\lvert R_n\rvert \le \bar R \le \tfrac12$ for $n > N$. So $\lvert T_{n+1}\rvert =
\lvert T_n\rvert\,u_{n+1}(x)\,\lvert R_{n+1}\rvert \le q\lvert T_n\rvert$. In
$a_n = T_n N_a/(A_n - R_n)$, the forms of $N_a$ and of
$A_n - R_n = (n + (n+1)/m^2)/x - u_{n+1}(mx)/m - R_n$ give $\lvert N_a\rvert \le (n+1)c_1 + e_1$
and $\lvert A_n - R_n\rvert \ge nc_2 - e_2$; the ratio is a Möbius function of $n$, monotone on
$n \ge N + 1$, so it is at most the sum of its value at $N + 1$ and its limit. Likewise
$\lvert N_b\rvert \le U_x + \lvert m\rvert U_z$ and $\lvert B_n - R_n\rvert \ge
(2n+1)/x - \lvert m\rvert U_z - \bar R$. ∎

Each series tail is then a sum of a polynomial weight times a geometric sequence, bounded by
its first term over $1 - q\rho$, where $\rho$ is the weight's ratio at $N + 1$ (the weights
are products of factors $an + b$, whose ratios decrease). For $S_1$, $S_2$ the weights use
$\lvert\pi_n\rvert \le n(n+1)/2$ and $\lvert\tau_n\rvert \le n^2(n+1)^2/4$: from
$P_n' = \sum_{k} (2k+1)P_k$ over $k = n-1, n-3, \ldots$ and $\lvert P_k\rvert \le 1$,
$\lvert P_n'\rvert \le n(n+1)/2$ and $\lvert P_n''\rvert \le (n-1)n^2(n+1)/4$, and
$\tau_n = \mu\pi_n - (1 - \mu^2)P_n''$. The bound is added to each value's error; the
generator's tests check that it dominates the next terms and that a longer sum lies inside
the certified ball.
```

Create `docs/validation/accuracy.md`:

````markdown
---
file_format: mystnb
kernelspec:
  name: python3
  display_name: Python 3
  language: python
---

# Accuracy of the Mie outputs

The accuracy of every Mie output is declared in the code, as a tolerance relative to the
conditioning of the problem (see {ref}`mie-accuracy-model`), and enforced by two tests in
continuous integration on Linux, macOS and Windows:

- `scatrs/tests/mie_reference.rs` compares every output with the Arb reference values
  ({doc}`reference-suite`): 306 spheres for the values, 80 for the derivatives.
- `scatrs/tests/mie_accuracy_map.rs` compares `f64` with `DoubleDouble` on a dense grid: 104
  size parameters from $10^{-6}$ to $2\cdot10^4$ against 24 refractive indices, with the
  amplitudes at 41 angles. `DoubleDouble` is itself checked against Arb.

This page plots the map. Each value is

$$C = \frac{\text{error}}{(\kappa' + \mathrm{cond})\,2^{-52}},$$

the constant the declared tolerance $C\,(\kappa' + \mathrm{cond})\,2^{-52}$ would need at that
point; the declared constants are at least 4 times the largest value found.

```{code-cell} python
import json
from pathlib import Path

import matplotlib.pyplot as plt
import numpy as np

data = json.loads(Path("data/mie-accuracy-map.json").read_text())
x = np.array(data["x"])
m = [complex(*p) for p in data["m"]]
names = list(data["c"])
print(f"{len(x)} size parameters × {len(m)} refractive indices")
print(f"{'output':<26}{'worst C':>10}{'declared':>10}")
for name in names:
    worst = np.max(data["c"][name])
    print(f"{name:<26}{worst:10.2f}{data['declared'][name]:10.0f}")
    assert worst <= data["declared"][name]
```

## The map

Each panel shows $\log_{10} C$ for one output: size parameter across, refractive index down.
White cells are exact (for example $Q_\text{abs}$ of real $m$, which is exactly 0).

```{code-cell} python
fig, axes = plt.subplots(5, 2, figsize=(11, 16), sharex=True, sharey=True, layout="constrained")
labels = [f"{z.real:g}{z.imag:+g}i" for z in m]
for ax, name in zip(axes.flat, names, strict=True):
    c = np.array(data["c"][name]).T
    with np.errstate(divide="ignore"):
        image = ax.pcolormesh(
            np.arange(len(x) + 1), np.arange(len(m) + 1), np.log10(c),
            vmin=-3, vmax=np.log10(data["declared"][name]), cmap="viridis",
        )
    ax.set_title(f"{name} (declared C = {data['declared'][name]:g})")
    fig.colorbar(image, ax=ax, label="log10 C")
ticks = np.arange(0, len(x), 10)
for ax in axes[-1]:
    ax.set_xticks(ticks + 0.5, [f"{x[i]:.0e}" for i in ticks], rotation=60)
    ax.set_xlabel("size parameter x")
for ax in axes[:, 0]:
    ax.set_yticks(np.arange(len(m)) + 0.5, labels, fontsize=7)
plt.show()
```

## Why the tolerance has two condition terms

A tolerance proportional to the a-priori factor $\kappa(x, m) = (1 + \lvert m\rvert x)
\max(1, \lvert m\rvert/\lvert m - 1\rvert)$ alone holds for absorbing spheres, but not for
weakly absorbing or lossless ones: their resonances are arbitrarily sharp, so the outputs
near a resonance are far more sensitive to $x$ and $m$ than $\lvert m\rvert x$ suggests. On a
first version of this grid the constants needed under $\kappa$ alone reached 360 for the
coefficients and 7600 for the efficiency Jacobian, all at real $m$ — and grew as the grid was
refined, since a denser grid lands closer to sharper resonances. The relative condition
number, computed from double-double derivatives (and, for the derivatives, from central
differences of them), measures that sensitivity at each point.

The condition number alone is not enough either: the summed series lose accuracy to
cancellation that the inputs' conditioning does not show. Backscattering by a metallic sphere
($m = 0.05 + 4i$, $x = 2500$) needed $C \approx 6600$ under cond alone: its alternating
series is about $x$ times smaller than its terms. With both terms every output stays below
$C = 10$ for the values and below 60 for the derivatives.

## By refractive index

The worst $C$ of each output over all size parameters, for each refractive index of the grid.
Absorbing spheres stay close to 1; the larger values belong to weakly absorbing and lossless
spheres near sharp resonances, and to the derivatives.

```{code-cell} python
print(f"{'m':<14}" + "".join(f"{n[:10]:>11}" for n in names))
for j, z in enumerate(m):
    row = [max(data["c"][name][i][j] for i in range(len(x))) for name in names]
    print(f"{z.real:g}{z.imag:+g}i".ljust(14) + "".join(f"{v:11.2f}" for v in row))
```

### Task 6: Python API (`scatrs.mie`)

`scatrs.mie.efficiencies`, `coefficients`, `amplitudes` and `default_n_max`: x and m broadcast to a shape S (numpy rules) and pass to Rust as views (zero strides where broadcast, never materialised); one loop in Rust per call with the GIL released; results are frozen dataclasses of numpy arrays handed over without copying, with `.d(param)` for `"x"`, `"m_real"`, `"m_imag"` (and `"m"`, the complex derivative, for coefficients and amplitudes). Coefficients are zero-padded to the largest number of terms, with `n_terms` per sphere; amplitudes have shape S + mu.shape. Outputs are capped at `MAX_MIE_VALUES` = 2²⁷ complex values per call.

**Files:**
- Create: `scatrs-py/src/mie.rs`, `python/scatrs/mie.py`, `docs/examples/mie.md`
- Modify: `scatrs-py/src/lib.rs`, `python/scatrs/{__init__.py,_args.py}`, `python/scatrs/_scatrs/__init__.pyi`, `docs/api.md`, `docs/conf.py`, `docs/index.md`
- Test: `tests/test_mie.py`, `tests/test_package.py`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: Task 4's API; `scatrs-py`'s `errors::to_pyerr`; `scatrs._args.{real_array, size}`; `scatrs._scatrs._testing.with_write_borrow`.
- Produces:
  - Extension: `MAX_MIE_ORDER`, `MAX_MIE_VALUES`; `mie_default_n_max(x) -> uint64[len]`; `mie_efficiencies(x, m, n_max, derivatives, allow_unvalidated) -> (float64[5, len], float64[3, 5, len] | None)`; `mie_coefficients(…) -> (uint64[len], complex128[len, N], complex128[len, N], complex128[4, len, N] | None)`; `mie_amplitudes(x, m, mu, …) -> (complex128[2, len, n_mu], complex128[4, len, n_mu] | None)`.
  - Python: `scatrs.mie.{efficiencies, coefficients, amplitudes, default_n_max, MieEfficiencies, MieCoefficients, MieAmplitudes, PARAMETERS, MAX_MIE_ORDER, MAX_MIE_VALUES}`; `scatrs._args.complex_array(x, name)`.

import threading
import time
from concurrent.futures import ThreadPoolExecutor

import numpy as np
import pytest

from scatrs import InvalidInputError, OutsideValidatedDomainError, _scatrs, mie
from scatrs._scatrs import _testing


def test_wiscombe_published_values():
    # Wiscombe (1979), x = 1000, m = 0.75: Q_sca and S(0) (conjugated: MIEV0 uses e^(+iwt)).
    q = mie.efficiencies(1000.0, 0.75)
    assert abs(q.q_sca - 1.997908) < 1e-6
    assert q.q_abs == 0.0 and q.q_ext == q.q_sca
    s = mie.amplitudes(1000.0, 0.75, 1.0)
    assert abs(s.s1 - (4.994770e5 + 1.336502e4j)) < 1e-6 * abs(s.s1)


def test_shapes_broadcast():
    x = np.array([[0.5], [5.0], [50.0]])
    m = np.array([1.33, 1.5 + 0.01j])
    q = mie.efficiencies(x, m)
    for f in (q.q_ext, q.q_sca, q.q_abs, q.q_back, q.g):
        assert f.shape == (3, 2) and f.dtype == np.float64
    c = mie.coefficients(x, m)
    assert c.n_terms.shape == (3, 2)
    assert c.a.shape == (3, 2, c.n_terms.max()) and c.a.dtype == np.complex128
    mu = np.linspace(-1, 1, 12).reshape(3, 4)
    s = mie.amplitudes(x, m, mu)
    assert s.s1.shape == (3, 2, 3, 4)
    # Scalars give scalars (0-d arrays).
    assert mie.efficiencies(1.0, 1.5).q_ext.shape == ()


def test_elements_equal_scalar_calls():
    x = np.array([0.1, 3.0, 300.0])
    m = np.array([1.33 + 1e-8j, 2.5 + 1j, 0.2 + 3j])
    q = mie.efficiencies(x, m)
    for i in range(3):
        qi = mie.efficiencies(x[i], m[i])
        assert q.q_ext[i] == qi.q_ext and q.g[i] == qi.g


def test_coefficients_are_zero_padded():
    c = mie.coefficients([0.1, 20.0], 1.5)
    n0, n1 = c.n_terms
    assert n0 < n1 == c.a.shape[-1]
    assert np.all(c.a[0, n0:] == 0) and np.all(c.b[0, n0:] == 0)
    assert np.all(c.a[0, :n0] != 0)
    np.testing.assert_array_equal(c.n_terms, mie.default_n_max([0.1, 20.0]))


def test_default_n_max():
    np.testing.assert_array_equal(mie.default_n_max([1e-6, 0.1, 1.0, 1000.0]), [4, 6, 10, 1063])


def test_n_max_and_its_domain():
    c = mie.coefficients(10.0, 1.5, n_max=60)
    assert c.a.shape == (60,)
    with pytest.raises(OutsideValidatedDomainError):
        mie.efficiencies(10.0, 1.5, n_max=5)
    mie.efficiencies(10.0, 1.5, n_max=5, allow_unvalidated=True)


def test_optical_theorem_and_symmetries():
    for x, m in [(0.3, 1.33), (25.0, 1.5 + 0.1j), (700.0, 0.2 + 3j)]:
        q = mie.efficiencies(x, m)
        s = mie.amplitudes(x, m, [1.0, -1.0])
        assert abs(4 / x**2 * s.s1[0].real / q.q_ext - 1) < 1e-12
        assert s.s1[0] == s.s2[0] and s.s1[1] == -s.s2[1]
        assert abs(4 * abs(s.s1[1]) ** 2 / x**2 / q.q_back - 1) < 1e-11


def _fd(f, h):
    return (f(+h) - f(-h)) / (2 * h)


@pytest.mark.parametrize(("x", "m"), [(0.7, 1.5 + 0.01j), (8.0, 1.33 + 1e-4j), (40.0, 2.5 + 1j)])
def test_derivatives_match_finite_differences(x, m):
    q = mie.efficiencies(x, m, derivatives=True)
    # One term more than the default, so that x ± h keeps the same number of terms.
    n = int(mie.default_n_max(x)) + 1
    c = mie.coefficients(x, m, n_max=n, derivatives=True)
    s = mie.amplitudes(x, m, [0.9, 0.0, -0.5], derivatives=True)
    hx, hm = 1e-6 * x, 1e-7
    steps = {
        "x": (lambda h: (x + h, m), hx),
        "m_real": (lambda h: (x, m + h), hm),
        "m_imag": (lambda h: (x, m + 1j * h), hm),
    }
    for p, (at, h) in steps.items():
        for name in ("q_ext", "q_sca", "q_abs", "q_back", "g"):
            fd = _fd(lambda e, at=at, name=name: getattr(mie.efficiencies(*at(e)), name), h)
            got = getattr(q.d(p), name)
            assert abs(got - fd) <= 1e-6 * max(abs(fd), abs(getattr(q, name))), (p, name)
        fd_a = _fd(lambda e, at=at: mie.coefficients(*at(e), n_max=n).a, h)
        np.testing.assert_allclose(c.d(p).a, fd_a, rtol=0, atol=1e-6 * np.abs(fd_a).max())
        fd_s = _fd(lambda e, at=at: mie.amplitudes(*at(e), [0.9, 0.0, -0.5]).s1, h)
        np.testing.assert_allclose(s.d(p).s1, fd_s, rtol=0, atol=1e-6 * np.abs(fd_s).max())
    # The complex derivative: d/dm = ∂/∂(Re m), and ∂/∂(Im m) = i d/dm.
    np.testing.assert_array_equal(c.d("m").a, c.d("m_real").a)
    np.testing.assert_array_equal(c.d("m_imag").a, 1j * c.d("m").a)
    np.testing.assert_array_equal(s.d("m_imag").s2, 1j * s.d("m").s2)


def test_derivative_subsets():
    q = mie.efficiencies(5.0, 1.5 + 0.1j, derivatives=["m_imag"])
    assert q.params == ("m_imag",)
    q.d("m_imag")
    with pytest.raises(ValueError, match="no derivative"):
        q.d("x")
    with pytest.raises(ValueError, match="unknown parameter"):
        mie.efficiencies(5.0, 1.5, derivatives=["radius"])
    with pytest.raises(TypeError):
        mie.efficiencies(5.0, 1.5, derivatives="x")
    with pytest.raises(ValueError, match="no derivative"):
        mie.efficiencies(5.0, 1.5).d("x")
    c = mie.coefficients(5.0, 1.5 + 0.1j, derivatives=["m_real"])
    c.d("m")  # the complex derivative is available with either m parameter


@pytest.mark.parametrize(
    ("x", "m"),
    [(-1.0, 1.5), (0.0, 1.5), (np.nan, 1.5), (1e-31, 1.5), (1.0, 1.5 - 1e-9j), (1.0, -1.5),
     (1.0, np.nan), (1.0, 0.0), (1.0, 2e3)],
)  # fmt: skip
def test_invalid_inputs(x, m):

def test_error_names_the_element():

@pytest.mark.parametrize(("x", "m"), [(3e4, 1.5), (1.0, 11.0), (1.0, 1.5 + 11j), (1.0, 2j)])
def test_outside_the_domain(x, m):
    with pytest.raises(OutsideValidatedDomainError):
        mie.efficiencies(x, m)
    assert np.isfinite(mie.efficiencies(x, m, allow_unvalidated=True).q_ext)


def test_type_errors():
    with pytest.raises(TypeError):
        mie.efficiencies(1.0 + 0j, 1.5)
    with pytest.raises(TypeError):
        mie.efficiencies(1.0, "1.5")
    with pytest.raises(ValueError):
        mie.efficiencies([1.0, 2.0], [1.5, 1.6, 1.7])  # shapes do not broadcast
    with pytest.raises(InvalidInputError):
        mie.amplitudes(1.0, 1.5, [1.5])


def test_empty_inputs():
    q = mie.efficiencies(np.zeros((0, 3)), 1.5)
    assert q.q_ext.shape == (0, 3)
    c = mie.coefficients(np.zeros(0), 1.5)
    assert c.a.shape == (0, 0)
    s = mie.amplitudes([1.0, 2.0], 1.5, np.zeros(0))
    assert s.s1.shape == (2, 0)


def test_output_size_limit():
    with pytest.raises(InvalidInputError, match="MAX_MIE_VALUES"):
        mie.amplitudes(np.full(2**14, 1.0), 1.5, np.zeros(2**13), allow_unvalidated=True)


def test_outputs_are_rust_vectors_handed_over_without_copying():
    x, m = np.array([1.0, 2.0]), np.array([1.5 + 0j, 1.5 + 0j])
    values, _ = _scatrs.mie_efficiencies(x, m, None, False, False)
    assert not isinstance(values.base, np.ndarray)
    q = mie.efficiencies(np.array([1.0, 2.0]), 1.5)
    assert q.q_ext.flags.c_contiguous and q.q_ext.base is not None


class _Spy:
    def __init__(self):
        self.seen = []

    def __getattr__(self, name):
        return getattr(_scatrs, name)

    def mie_efficiencies(self, x, m, *args):
        self.seen.append((x, m))
        return _scatrs.mie_efficiencies(x, m, *args)


def test_inputs_are_broadcast_without_copying(monkeypatch):
    spy = _Spy()
    monkeypatch.setattr(mie, "_scatrs", spy)
    x = np.linspace(1, 2, 4).reshape(4, 1)
    m = np.array([1.5 + 0j, 1.6 + 0j, 1.7 + 0j])
    mie.efficiencies(x, m)
    xb, mb = spy.seen[-1]
    assert xb.shape == mb.shape == (4, 3)
    assert np.shares_memory(xb, x) and np.shares_memory(mb, m)
    assert xb is not x and mb is not m
    assert 0 in xb.strides and 0 in mb.strides  # broadcast, not materialised


def test_buffer_error_when_an_input_is_borrowed_for_writing():
    x = np.array([1.0, 2.0])
    with pytest.raises(BufferError):
        _testing.with_write_borrow(x, lambda: mie.efficiencies(x, 1.5))


def test_threaded_results_equal_serial_results():
    x = np.geomspace(0.1, 500, 40)
    serial = mie.efficiencies(x, 1.5 + 0.01j).q_ext
    with ThreadPoolExecutor(4) as pool:
        results = list(pool.map(lambda _: mie.efficiencies(x, 1.5 + 0.01j).q_ext, range(8)))
    for r in results:
        np.testing.assert_array_equal(r, serial)


def test_gil_is_released_while_computing():
    span = {}

    def worker():
        span["start"] = time.perf_counter()
        mie.efficiencies(np.full(200, 1.5e4), 1.5 + 0.01j, derivatives=True)
        span["end"] = time.perf_counter()

    stamps = []
    thread = threading.Thread(target=worker)
    thread.start()
    while thread.is_alive():
        time.sleep(0.0005)
        stamps.append(time.perf_counter())
    thread.join()
    quarter = (span["end"] - span["start"]) / 4
    inside = sum(span["start"] + quarter < t < span["end"] - quarter for t in stamps)
    assert span["end"] - span["start"] > 0.02
    assert inside >= 5
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Write the extension functions**

Create `scatrs-py/src/mie.rs`:

```rust
//! Bindings for `scatrs::mie`.
//!
//! Every function takes `x` (float64) and `m` (complex128) arrays of one shape, usually views
//! that `np.broadcast_arrays` made (zero strides where broadcast), and reads them in place, in
//! C order, with the GIL released; so they must not be modified from another thread during the
//! call. The outputs have one row per element in that order; `scatrs.mie` reshapes them.
//! Arrays are borrowed with `try_readonly` (see `special.rs` for why).

use numpy::ndarray::{Array2, Array3, ArrayViewD};
use numpy::{
    Complex64, IntoPyArray, PyArray1, PyArray2, PyArray3, PyArrayDyn, PyArrayMethods,
    PyReadonlyArrayDyn, PyUntypedArrayMethods,
};
use pyo3::exceptions::{PyBufferError, PyMemoryError, PyRuntimeError, PyTypeError};
use pyo3::prelude::*;
use scatrs::mie::{AmplitudeBuffers, Mie, MieOptions, MieSolution};

use crate::errors::to_pyerr;

/// The most complex values one call returns, 2²⁷ (2 GiB). Larger requests raise
/// `InvalidInputError`, as in `special.rs`.
pub const MAX_MIE_VALUES: usize = 1 << 27;

fn borrow<'py, T: numpy::Element>(
    a: &'py Bound<'py, PyArrayDyn<T>>,
    name: &str,
) -> PyResult<PyReadonlyArrayDyn<'py, T>> {
    if !a.is_aligned() {
        return Err(PyTypeError::new_err(format!(
            "{name} must be an aligned array"
        )));
    }
    a.try_readonly()
        .map_err(|e| PyBufferError::new_err(format!("{name} cannot be read: {e}")))
}

/// The options, and a check that x and m have the same shape.
fn prepare(
    x: &ArrayViewD<'_, f64>,
    m: &ArrayViewD<'_, Complex64>,
    n_max: Option<usize>,
    allow_unvalidated: bool,
) -> PyResult<MieOptions> {
    if x.shape() != m.shape() {
        return Err(PyTypeError::new_err(format!(
            "x and m must have one shape (broadcast them first), got {:?} and {:?}",
            x.shape(),
            m.shape()
        )));
    }
    let mut opts = MieOptions::new().with_allow_unvalidated(allow_unvalidated);
    if let Some(n) = n_max {
        opts = opts.with_n_max(n);
    }
    Ok(opts)
}

/// Solves element `i`, prefixing any error with its index.
fn solve<'m>(
    mie: &'m mut Mie<f64>,
    i: usize,
    x: f64,
    m: Complex64,
    opts: &MieOptions,
    derivatives: bool,
) -> scatrs::Result<MieSolution<'m, f64>> {
    let r = if derivatives {
        mie.compute_with_derivatives(x, m, opts)
    } else {
        mie.compute(x, m, opts)
    };
    r.map_err(|e| match e {
        scatrs::Error::InvalidInput(s) => scatrs::Error::InvalidInput(format!("element {i}: {s}")),
        scatrs::Error::OutsideValidatedDomain(s) => {
            scatrs::Error::OutsideValidatedDomain(format!("element {i}: {s}"))
        }
        other => other,
    })
}

fn too_many(py: Python<'_>, what: &str, count: Option<usize>) -> PyErr {
    let count = count.map_or_else(|| "more than usize::MAX".to_owned(), |c| c.to_string());
    to_pyerr(
        py,
        scatrs::Error::InvalidInput(format!(
            "{what} would hold {count} values, more than MAX_MIE_VALUES = {MAX_MIE_VALUES}"
        )),
    )
}

/// A zeroed vector of `len` values, or `MemoryError`.
fn zeros<T: Clone + Default>(len: usize, what: &str) -> PyResult<Vec<T>> {
    let mut v = Vec::new();
    v.try_reserve_exact(len).map_err(|e| {
        PyMemoryError::new_err(format!("cannot allocate {len} values for {what}: {e}"))
    })?;
    v.resize(len, T::default());
    Ok(v)
}

fn shape_error(e: impl std::fmt::Display) -> PyErr {
    PyRuntimeError::new_err(format!("output shape: {e}"))
}

/// The default number of terms for each x (C order); see `scatrs.mie.default_n_max`.
#[pyfunction]
pub fn mie_default_n_max<'py>(
    py: Python<'py>,
    x: Bound<'py, PyArrayDyn<f64>>,
) -> PyResult<Bound<'py, PyArray1<u64>>> {
    let x = borrow(&x, "x")?;
    let n: Vec<u64> = x
        .as_array()
        .iter()
        .map(|&v| scatrs::mie::default_n_max(v) as u64)
        .collect();
    Ok(PyArray1::from_vec(py, n))
}

type EfficiencyArrays<'py> = (Bound<'py, PyArray2<f64>>, Option<Bound<'py, PyArray3<f64>>>);

/// Efficiencies (5, len) in the order Q_ext, Q_sca, Q_abs, Q_back, g, and with
/// `derivatives` their Jacobian (3, 5, len) with respect to x, Re m and Im m.
#[pyfunction]
pub fn mie_efficiencies<'py>(
    py: Python<'py>,
    x: Bound<'py, PyArrayDyn<f64>>,
    m: Bound<'py, PyArrayDyn<Complex64>>,
    n_max: Option<usize>,
    derivatives: bool,
    allow_unvalidated: bool,
) -> PyResult<EfficiencyArrays<'py>> {
    let (x, m) = (borrow(&x, "x")?, borrow(&m, "m")?);
    let (xv, mv) = (x.as_array(), m.as_array());
    let opts = prepare(&xv, &mv, n_max, allow_unvalidated)?;
    let len = xv.len();
    let mut values = zeros::<f64>(5 * len, "the efficiencies")?;
    let mut jacobian = zeros::<f64>(if derivatives { 15 * len } else { 0 }, "the Jacobian")?;
    py.detach(|| -> scatrs::Result<()> {
        let mut mie = Mie::<f64>::new();
        for (i, (&xi, &mi)) in xv.iter().zip(mv.iter()).enumerate() {
            let s = solve(&mut mie, i, xi, mi, &opts, derivatives)?;
            let q = s.efficiencies();
            for (k, v) in [q.q_ext, q.q_sca, q.q_abs, q.q_back, q.g]
                .into_iter()
                .enumerate()
            {
                values[k * len + i] = v;
            }
            if let Some(j) = s.efficiencies_jacobian() {
                for (p, d) in [j.x, j.m_re, j.m_im].into_iter().enumerate() {
                    let row = [d.q_ext, d.q_sca, d.q_abs, d.q_back, d.g];
                    for (k, v) in row.into_iter().enumerate() {
                        jacobian[(p * 5 + k) * len + i] = v;
                    }
                }
            }
        }
        Ok(())
    })
    .map_err(|e| to_pyerr(py, e))?;
    let values = Array2::from_shape_vec((5, len), values).map_err(shape_error)?;
    let jacobian = if derivatives {
        let a = Array3::from_shape_vec((3, 5, len), jacobian).map_err(shape_error)?;
        Some(a.into_pyarray(py))
    } else {
        None
    };
    Ok((values.into_pyarray(py), jacobian))
}

type CoefficientArrays<'py> = (
    Bound<'py, PyArray1<u64>>,
    Bound<'py, PyArray2<Complex64>>,
    Bound<'py, PyArray2<Complex64>>,
    Option<Bound<'py, PyArray3<Complex64>>>,
);

/// The number of terms of each element (len), aₙ and bₙ (len, N) zero-padded to the largest
/// N, and with `derivatives` ∂a/∂x, da/dm, ∂b/∂x, db/dm (4, len, N).
#[pyfunction]
pub fn mie_coefficients<'py>(
    py: Python<'py>,
    x: Bound<'py, PyArrayDyn<f64>>,
    m: Bound<'py, PyArrayDyn<Complex64>>,
    n_max: Option<usize>,
    derivatives: bool,
    allow_unvalidated: bool,
) -> PyResult<CoefficientArrays<'py>> {
    let (x, m) = (borrow(&x, "x")?, borrow(&m, "m")?);
    let (xv, mv) = (x.as_array(), m.as_array());
    let opts = prepare(&xv, &mv, n_max, allow_unvalidated)?;
    let len = xv.len();
    // The core checks each n_max; this only sizes the output (wrong sizes fail there first).
    let terms: Vec<u64> = xv
        .iter()
        .map(|&v| n_max.unwrap_or_else(|| scatrs::mie::default_n_max(v)) as u64)
        .collect();
    let width = terms.iter().copied().max().unwrap_or(0) as usize;
    let arrays = if derivatives { 6 } else { 2 };
    let total = len
        .checked_mul(width)
        .and_then(|v| v.checked_mul(arrays))
        .filter(|&v| v <= MAX_MIE_VALUES);
    let Some(per_array) = total.map(|t| t / arrays) else {
        let count = len.checked_mul(width).and_then(|v| v.checked_mul(arrays));
        return Err(too_many(py, "the coefficients", count));
    };
    let mut a = zeros::<Complex64>(per_array, "the coefficients")?;
    let mut b = zeros::<Complex64>(per_array, "the coefficients")?;
    let mut d = zeros::<Complex64>(if derivatives { 4 * per_array } else { 0 }, "derivatives")?;
    py.detach(|| -> scatrs::Result<()> {
        let mut mie = Mie::<f64>::new();
        for (i, (&xi, &mi)) in xv.iter().zip(mv.iter()).enumerate() {
            let s = solve(&mut mie, i, xi, mi, &opts, derivatives)?;
            let row = i * width..i * width + s.n_terms();
            a[row.clone()].copy_from_slice(s.a());
            b[row.clone()].copy_from_slice(s.b());
            if let Some(dc) = s.derivatives() {
                for (k, src) in [dc.da_dx, dc.da_dm, dc.db_dx, dc.db_dm]
                    .into_iter()
                    .enumerate()
                {
                    let off = k * per_array;
                    d[off + row.start..off + row.end].copy_from_slice(src);
                }
            }
        }
        Ok(())
    })
    .map_err(|e| to_pyerr(py, e))?;
    let a = Array2::from_shape_vec((len, width), a).map_err(shape_error)?;
    let b = Array2::from_shape_vec((len, width), b).map_err(shape_error)?;
    let d = if derivatives {
        let d = Array3::from_shape_vec((4, len, width), d).map_err(shape_error)?;
        Some(d.into_pyarray(py))
    } else {
        None
    };
    Ok((
        PyArray1::from_vec(py, terms),
        a.into_pyarray(py),
        b.into_pyarray(py),
        d,
    ))
}

type AmplitudeArrays<'py> = (
    Bound<'py, PyArray3<Complex64>>,
    Option<Bound<'py, PyArray3<Complex64>>>,
);

/// S₁ and S₂ (2, len, n_mu) at the cosines `mu`, and with `derivatives` ∂S₁/∂x, ∂S₂/∂x,
/// dS₁/dm, dS₂/dm (4, len, n_mu).
#[pyfunction]
#[allow(clippy::too_many_arguments)]
pub fn mie_amplitudes<'py>(
    py: Python<'py>,
    x: Bound<'py, PyArrayDyn<f64>>,
    m: Bound<'py, PyArrayDyn<Complex64>>,
    mu: Bound<'py, PyArrayDyn<f64>>,
    n_max: Option<usize>,
    derivatives: bool,
    allow_unvalidated: bool,
) -> PyResult<AmplitudeArrays<'py>> {
    let (x, m, mu) = (borrow(&x, "x")?, borrow(&m, "m")?, borrow(&mu, "mu")?);
    let (xv, mv) = (x.as_array(), m.as_array());
    let opts = prepare(&xv, &mv, n_max, allow_unvalidated)?;
    let mu: Vec<f64> = mu.as_array().iter().copied().collect();
    let (len, n_mu) = (xv.len(), mu.len());
    let arrays = if derivatives { 6 } else { 2 };
    let per_array = len
        .checked_mul(n_mu)
        .filter(|v| v.checked_mul(arrays).is_some_and(|t| t <= MAX_MIE_VALUES));
    let Some(per_array) = per_array else {
        let count = len.checked_mul(n_mu).and_then(|v| v.checked_mul(arrays));
        return Err(too_many(py, "the amplitudes", count));
    };
    let mut s = zeros::<Complex64>(2 * per_array, "the amplitudes")?;
    let mut d = zeros::<Complex64>(if derivatives { 4 * per_array } else { 0 }, "derivatives")?;
    py.detach(|| -> scatrs::Result<()> {
        let mut mie = Mie::<f64>::new();
        let (s1, s2) = s.split_at_mut(per_array);
        // Empty unless `derivatives`.
        let half = d.len() / 2;
        let (dx, dm) = d.split_at_mut(half);
        let (dx1, dx2) = dx.split_at_mut(half / 2);
        let (dm1, dm2) = dm.split_at_mut(half / 2);
        for (i, (&xi, &mi)) in xv.iter().zip(mv.iter()).enumerate() {
            let sol = solve(&mut mie, i, xi, mi, &opts, derivatives)?;
            let row = i * n_mu..(i + 1) * n_mu;
            let values = AmplitudeBuffers {
                s1: &mut s1[row.clone()],
                s2: &mut s2[row.clone()],
            };
            if derivatives {
                sol.amplitudes_with_derivatives(
                    &mu,
                    values,
                    AmplitudeBuffers {
                        s1: &mut dx1[row.clone()],
                        s2: &mut dx2[row.clone()],
                    },
                    AmplitudeBuffers {
                        s1: &mut dm1[row.clone()],
                        s2: &mut dm2[row.clone()],
                    },
                )?;
            } else {
                sol.amplitudes(&mu, values)?;
            }
        }
        Ok(())
    })
    .map_err(|e| to_pyerr(py, e))?;
    let s = Array3::from_shape_vec((2, len, n_mu), s).map_err(shape_error)?;
    let d = if derivatives {
        let d = Array3::from_shape_vec((4, len, n_mu), d).map_err(shape_error)?;
        Some(d.into_pyarray(py))
    } else {
        None
    };
    Ok((s.into_pyarray(py), d))
}
```

- [ ] **Step 4: Write the Python module**

Create `python/scatrs/mie.py`:

```python
"""Lorenz–Mie scattering by homogeneous spheres (vectorized wrappers over the Rust core).

The size parameters ``x`` and refractive indices ``m`` broadcast against each other to a shape
``S``; every result has that shape, extended by the orders or the angles. Conventions are those
of Bohren & Huffman (1983): time dependence e^(−iωt), so absorbing media have ``Im m >= 0``, and
``x = 2 pi r / wavelength``. See :doc:`/theory/mie` for the method and
:doc:`/validation/accuracy` for the validated domain and the accuracy.

Each function runs entirely in Rust, with the GIL released, over all elements in turn.
``derivatives`` is ``True`` (all of ``"x"``, ``"m_real"``, ``"m_imag"``) or a sequence of those
names; the result's ``d`` method (for example :meth:`MieEfficiencies.d`) then returns the
derivative with respect to one of them, in the same structure. Derivatives cost little: all
three together take 1.3 to 1.8 times as long as the values alone.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from collections.abc import Sequence
from dataclasses import dataclass, field
from typing import Final, SupportsIndex

import numpy as np
from numpy.typing import ArrayLike, NDArray

from scatrs import _args, _scatrs

__all__ = [
    "MAX_MIE_ORDER",
    "MAX_MIE_VALUES",
    "PARAMETERS",
    "MieAmplitudes",
    "MieCoefficients",
    "MieEfficiencies",
    "amplitudes",
    "coefficients",
    "default_n_max",
    "efficiencies",
]

MAX_MIE_ORDER: Final[int] = _scatrs.MAX_MIE_ORDER
"""The largest number of terms accepted: ``2**20``."""

MAX_MIE_VALUES: Final[int] = _scatrs.MAX_MIE_VALUES
"""The most complex values one call returns, counting derivatives: ``2**27`` (2 GiB)."""

PARAMETERS: Final = ("x", "m_real", "m_imag")
"""The parameters ``derivatives`` may name."""


def _parameters(derivatives: bool | Sequence[str]) -> tuple[str, ...]:
    if derivatives is True:
        return PARAMETERS
    if derivatives is False:
        return ()
    if isinstance(derivatives, str):
        raise TypeError("derivatives must be a bool or a sequence of parameter names")
    names = tuple(derivatives)
    for name in names:
        if name not in PARAMETERS:
            raise ValueError(f"unknown parameter {name!r}; expected one of {PARAMETERS}")
    return names


def _inputs(
    x: ArrayLike, m: ArrayLike
) -> tuple[NDArray[np.float64], NDArray[np.complex128], tuple[int, ...]]:
    """``x`` and ``m`` broadcast to one shape, as fresh views for Rust (see ``scatrs._args``)."""
    xs = _args.real_array(x, "x")
    ms = _args.complex_array(m, "m")
    xb, mb = np.broadcast_arrays(xs, ms)
    return xb, mb, xb.shape


def _n_max(n_max: SupportsIndex | None) -> int | None:
    return None if n_max is None else _args.size(n_max, "n_max")


def _check(params: tuple[str, ...], param: str) -> None:
    if param not in params and not (param == "m" and {"m_real", "m_imag"} & set(params)):
        raise ValueError(f"no derivative with respect to {param!r}; computed: {params}")


def default_n_max(x: ArrayLike) -> NDArray[np.int64]:
    """The default number of terms, ``ceil(x + 6 x**(1/3) + 3)``, for each size parameter.

    Parameters
    ----------
    x : array_like
        Size parameters (real).

    Returns
    -------
    numpy.ndarray
        int64, the shape of ``x``.
    """
    xs = _args.real_array(x, "x")
    return _scatrs.mie_default_n_max(xs.reshape(xs.shape)).astype(np.int64).reshape(xs.shape)


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class MieEfficiencies:
    """Efficiencies and asymmetry parameter of spheres, each an array of shape ``S``.

    Attributes
    ----------
    q_ext, q_sca, q_abs, q_back : numpy.ndarray
        Extinction, scattering, absorption and backscattering efficiencies (cross sections
        divided by pi r**2). ``q_abs`` is computed without the cancellation of
        ``q_ext - q_sca``, and is exactly 0 for real ``m``; ``q_ext = q_sca + q_abs``.
    g : numpy.ndarray
        Asymmetry parameter.
    params : tuple of str
        The parameters :meth:`d` accepts.
    """

    q_ext: NDArray[np.float64]
    q_sca: NDArray[np.float64]
    q_abs: NDArray[np.float64]
    q_back: NDArray[np.float64]
    g: NDArray[np.float64]
    params: tuple[str, ...] = ()
    _jacobian: NDArray[np.float64] | None = field(default=None, repr=False)

    def d(self, param: str) -> MieEfficiencies:
        """The derivatives with respect to ``param`` (``"x"``, ``"m_real"`` or ``"m_imag"``).

        Raises
        ------
        ValueError
            If the derivative was not requested.
        """
        _check(self.params, param)
        if param == "m":
            raise ValueError("efficiencies are real: use 'm_real' or 'm_imag'")
        assert self._jacobian is not None
        rows = self._jacobian[PARAMETERS.index(param)]
        return MieEfficiencies(*rows)


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class MieCoefficients:
    """Mie coefficients aₙ, bₙ of spheres, arrays of shape ``S + (N,)``, with ``N`` the largest
    number of terms of any sphere; ``a[..., n - 1]`` is aₙ, zero beyond each sphere's
    ``n_terms``.

    Attributes
    ----------
    a, b : numpy.ndarray
        complex128.
    n_terms : numpy.ndarray
        int64, shape ``S``: the number of terms of each sphere.
    params : tuple of str
        The parameters :meth:`d` accepts.
    """

    a: NDArray[np.complex128]
    b: NDArray[np.complex128]
    n_terms: NDArray[np.int64]
    params: tuple[str, ...] = ()
    _derivatives: NDArray[np.complex128] | None = field(default=None, repr=False)

    def d(self, param: str) -> MieCoefficients:
        """The derivatives with respect to ``param``: ``"x"``, ``"m_real"``, ``"m_imag"``, or
        ``"m"`` for the complex derivative d/dm (which equals ∂/∂(Re m); ∂/∂(Im m) is i d/dm).

        Raises
        ------
        ValueError
            If the derivative was not requested.
        """
        _check(self.params, param)
        assert self._derivatives is not None
        da_dx, da_dm, db_dx, db_dm = self._derivatives
        if param == "x":
            return MieCoefficients(da_dx, db_dx, self.n_terms)
        if param == "m_imag":
            return MieCoefficients(1j * da_dm, 1j * db_dm, self.n_terms)
        return MieCoefficients(da_dm, db_dm, self.n_terms)


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class MieAmplitudes:
    """Amplitude scattering functions S₁(μ), S₂(μ), arrays of shape ``S + mu.shape``.

    Attributes
    ----------
    s1, s2 : numpy.ndarray
        complex128 (Bohren & Huffman 1983, Eq. 4.74).
    params : tuple of str
        The parameters :meth:`d` accepts.
    """

    s1: NDArray[np.complex128]
    s2: NDArray[np.complex128]
    params: tuple[str, ...] = ()
    _derivatives: NDArray[np.complex128] | None = field(default=None, repr=False)

    def d(self, param: str) -> MieAmplitudes:
        """The derivatives with respect to ``param``: ``"x"``, ``"m_real"``, ``"m_imag"``, or
        ``"m"`` for the complex derivative d/dm.

        Raises
        ------
        ValueError
            If the derivative was not requested.
        """
        _check(self.params, param)
        assert self._derivatives is not None
        ds1_dx, ds2_dx, ds1_dm, ds2_dm = self._derivatives
        if param == "x":
            return MieAmplitudes(ds1_dx, ds2_dx)
        if param == "m_imag":
            return MieAmplitudes(1j * ds1_dm, 1j * ds2_dm)
        return MieAmplitudes(ds1_dm, ds2_dm)


_SHARED_DOC = """
    Parameters
    ----------
    x : array_like
        Size parameters, 2 pi r / wavelength (real).
    m : array_like
        Refractive indices relative to the medium (real or complex), broadcast against ``x``.
    {extra}n_max : int, optional
        The number of terms; by default ``ceil(x + 6 x**(1/3) + 3)``. Fewer terms than the
        default are outside the validated domain.
    derivatives : bool or sequence of str, optional
        Also compute derivatives with respect to these of ``"x"``, ``"m_real"`` and
        ``"m_imag"`` (``True`` for all three); read them with the result's ``d`` method.
    allow_unvalidated : bool, optional
        Compute outside the validated domain (``1e-6 <= x <= 2e4``, ``0 < Re m <= 10``,
        ``0 <= Im m <= 10``) instead of raising
        :class:`~scatrs.errors.OutsideValidatedDomainError`.

    Raises
    ------
    InvalidInputError
        For ``x`` outside ``[1e-30, 1e6]``, non-finite ``m``, ``Re m < 0`` or ``Im m < 0``
        (gain media), ``|m|`` outside ``[1e-6, 1e3]``, ``n_max`` outside
        ``[1, MAX_MIE_ORDER]``{extra_raise}, or an output above ``MAX_MIE_VALUES``. The
        message names the first failing element (in C order).
    OutsideValidatedDomainError
        For valid inputs outside the validated domain, unless ``allow_unvalidated``.
    TypeError
        For non-numeric ``x`` or ``m`` (complex ``x``), or a ``derivatives`` string.
    ValueError
        For an unknown parameter name, or (from numpy) shapes that do not broadcast.
    BufferError
        If another rust-numpy extension holds a writeable borrow of an input.
    MemoryError
        If the outputs cannot be allocated.
"""


def efficiencies(
    x: ArrayLike,
    m: ArrayLike,
    *,
    n_max: SupportsIndex | None = None,
    derivatives: bool | Sequence[str] = False,
    allow_unvalidated: bool = False,
) -> MieEfficiencies:
    params = _parameters(derivatives)
    xb, mb, shape = _inputs(x, m)
    values, jacobian = _scatrs.mie_efficiencies(
        xb, mb, _n_max(n_max), bool(params), bool(allow_unvalidated)
    )
    fields = [v.reshape(shape) for v in values]
    jac = None if jacobian is None else jacobian.reshape((3, 5, *shape))
    return MieEfficiencies(*fields, params=params, _jacobian=jac)


efficiencies.__doc__ = """Efficiencies and asymmetry parameter of spheres.

    Returns
    -------
    MieEfficiencies
        Each field has the broadcast shape of ``x`` and ``m``.
""" + _SHARED_DOC.format(extra="", extra_raise="")


def coefficients(
    x: ArrayLike,
    m: ArrayLike,
    *,
    n_max: SupportsIndex | None = None,
    derivatives: bool | Sequence[str] = False,
    allow_unvalidated: bool = False,
) -> MieCoefficients:
    params = _parameters(derivatives)
    xb, mb, shape = _inputs(x, m)
    terms, a, b, d = _scatrs.mie_coefficients(
        xb, mb, _n_max(n_max), bool(params), bool(allow_unvalidated)
    )
    width = a.shape[1]
    n_terms = terms.astype(np.int64).reshape(shape)
    dd = None if d is None else d.reshape((4, *shape, width))
    return MieCoefficients(
        a.reshape((*shape, width)), b.reshape((*shape, width)), n_terms, params, dd
    )


coefficients.__doc__ = """Mie coefficients aₙ and bₙ of spheres.

    Returns
    -------
    MieCoefficients
        Shape ``S + (N,)``, zero-padded to the largest number of terms ``N``.
""" + _SHARED_DOC.format(extra="", extra_raise="")


def amplitudes(
    x: ArrayLike,
    m: ArrayLike,
    mu: ArrayLike,
    *,
    n_max: SupportsIndex | None = None,
    derivatives: bool | Sequence[str] = False,
    allow_unvalidated: bool = False,
) -> MieAmplitudes:
    params = _parameters(derivatives)
    xb, mb, shape = _inputs(x, m)
    mus = _args.real_array(mu, "mu")
    s, d = _scatrs.mie_amplitudes(
        xb, mb, mus.reshape(mus.shape), _n_max(n_max), bool(params), bool(allow_unvalidated)
    )
    out = (*shape, *mus.shape)
    dd = None if d is None else d.reshape((4, *out))
    return MieAmplitudes(s[0].reshape(out), s[1].reshape(out), params, dd)


amplitudes.__doc__ = """Amplitude scattering functions S₁ and S₂ of spheres.

    Returns
    -------
    MieAmplitudes
        Shape ``S + mu.shape``.
""" + _SHARED_DOC.format(
    extra="mu : array_like\n        Cosines of the scattering angle, in [-1, 1].\n    ",
    extra_raise=", ``mu`` outside [-1, 1] or NaN",
)
```

diff --git a/python/scatrs/_args.py b/python/scatrs/_args.py
index c1f5014..f84107b 100644
--- a/python/scatrs/_args.py
+++ b/python/scatrs/_args.py
@@ -98,3 +98,24 @@ def real_array(x: ArrayLike, name: str) -> NDArray[np.float64]:
     if xs.dtype == np.float64 and xs.flags.c_contiguous and xs.flags.aligned:
         return xs
     return np.require(xs, dtype=np.float64, requirements=["C", "A"])
+
+
+def complex_array(x: ArrayLike, name: str) -> NDArray[np.complex128]:
+    """``x`` as an aligned, C-contiguous, native-endian complex128 array of the same shape.
+
+    As :func:`real_array`, for complex numbers: such an array is returned as it is, and other
+    real or complex arrays, lists and scalars are converted, copying once.
+
+    Raises
+    ------
+    TypeError
+        If ``x`` is not numeric: boolean, string, object, datetime or timedelta.
+    ValueError
+        Raised by numpy for a ragged nested sequence.
+    """
+    xs = np.asarray(x)
+    if xs.dtype.kind not in "iufc":
+        raise TypeError(f"{name} must be numbers, not an array of dtype {xs.dtype}")
+    if xs.dtype == np.complex128 and xs.flags.c_contiguous and xs.flags.aligned:
+        return xs
+    return np.require(xs, dtype=np.complex128, requirements=["C", "A"])

 def wigner_d(m: int, n: int, l_max: int, x: NDArray[np.float64]) -> NDArray[np.float64]: ...
+def mie_default_n_max(x: NDArray[np.float64]) -> NDArray[np.uint64]: ...
+def mie_efficiencies(
+    x: NDArray[np.float64],
+    m: NDArray[np.complex128],
+    n_max: int | None,
+    derivatives: bool,
+    allow_unvalidated: bool,
+) -> tuple[NDArray[np.float64], NDArray[np.float64] | None]: ...
+def mie_coefficients(
+    x: NDArray[np.float64],
+    m: NDArray[np.complex128],
+    n_max: int | None,
+    derivatives: bool,
+    allow_unvalidated: bool,
+) -> tuple[
+    NDArray[np.uint64],
+    NDArray[np.complex128],

+def mie_amplitudes(
+    x: NDArray[np.float64],
+    m: NDArray[np.complex128],
+    mu: NDArray[np.float64],
+    n_max: int | None,
+    derivatives: bool,
+    allow_unvalidated: bool,

- [ ] **Step 6: Write the example page**

Create `docs/examples/mie.md`:

````markdown
---
file_format: mystnb
kernelspec:
  name: python3
  display_name: Python 3
  language: python
---

# Example: Mie scattering by spheres

{mod}`scatrs.mie` computes the Mie coefficients, efficiencies and amplitudes of homogeneous
spheres, vectorized over size parameter and refractive index, with derivatives. This page runs
whenever the documentation is built; its checks are `assert`s, so a failing check fails the
build.

```{code-cell} python
import numpy as np

from scatrs import mie

# Water droplets in visible light, from Rayleigh to geometric optics.
x = np.geomspace(0.01, 1000, 9)
q = mie.efficiencies(x, 1.33 + 1e-8j)
for xi, qe, w, g in zip(x, q.q_ext, q.q_sca / q.q_ext, q.g, strict=True):
    print(f"x = {xi:8.2f}   Q_ext = {qe:.6f}   single-scattering albedo = {w:.10f}   g = {g:+.4f}")
```

## Extinction paradox and the optical theorem

Large spheres remove twice their geometric cross section ($Q_\text{ext} \to 2$), and
$Q_\text{ext}$ equals $(4/x^2)\,\mathrm{Re}\,S(0)$, the optical theorem, which scatrs does not
use to compute it.

```{code-cell} python
q = mie.efficiencies(1e4, 1.5 + 0.01j)
s = mie.amplitudes(1e4, 1.5 + 0.01j, 1.0)
print(q.q_ext, 4 / 1e4**2 * s.s1.real)
assert abs(q.q_ext - 2) < 0.01
assert abs(4 / 1e4**2 * s.s1.real / q.q_ext - 1) < 1e-11
```

## Weak absorption without cancellation

For weakly absorbing particles, $Q_\text{abs} = Q_\text{ext} - Q_\text{sca}$ cancels:
here about 8 digits. scatrs computes $Q_\text{abs}$ from an exact per-term identity instead
({doc}`/theory/mie`), so the co-albedo $1 - \omega = Q_\text{abs}/Q_\text{ext}$ keeps full
precision. Absorption is linear in $k$ for small $k$, which the direct value shows to all its
digits, and the difference does not.

```{code-cell} python
for k in (1e-6, 1e-8, 1e-10):
    q = mie.efficiencies(20.0, 1.33 + 1j * k)
    print(f"k = {k:.0e}: Q_abs/k direct {q.q_abs / k:.15f}, "
          f"by difference {(q.q_ext - q.q_sca) / k:.15f}")
q6 = mie.efficiencies(20.0, 1.33 + 1e-6j)
q10 = mie.efficiencies(20.0, 1.33 + 1e-10j)
assert abs((q10.q_abs / 1e-10) / (q6.q_abs / 1e-6) - 1) < 1e-4  # linear in k
```

## The phase function

$F_{11} = (\lvert S_1\rvert^2 + \lvert S_2\rvert^2)/2$, normalized so that
$\tfrac12\int_{-1}^1 F_{11}\,d\mu = 1$ once divided by $x^2 Q_\text{sca}/4$. $F_{11}$ is a
polynomial of degree $2N$ in $\mu$, so a Gauss–Legendre rule with $N + 1$ points integrates it
exactly.

```{code-cell} python
from scatrs.special import gauss_legendre

x0, m0 = 30.0, 1.5 + 0.001j
n = int(mie.default_n_max(x0))
mu, w = gauss_legendre(n + 1)
s = mie.amplitudes(x0, m0, mu)
q = mie.efficiencies(x0, m0)
f11 = (abs(s.s1) ** 2 + abs(s.s2) ** 2) / 2 / (x0**2 * q.q_sca / 4)
print("normalization:", 0.5 * np.sum(w * f11))
print("asymmetry parameter:", 0.5 * np.sum(w * f11 * mu), q.g)
assert abs(0.5 * np.sum(w * f11) - 1) < 1e-13
assert abs(0.5 * np.sum(w * f11 * mu) - q.g) < 1e-13
```

## Derivatives

`derivatives=True` returns the derivatives with respect to the size parameter and the real
and imaginary parts of the refractive index, in closed form, for about 1.3 to 1.8 times the
cost of the values. Here they are checked against central differences.

```{code-cell} python
x0, m0 = 12.0, 1.45 + 0.02j
q = mie.efficiencies(x0, m0, derivatives=True)
h = 1e-6
for name, dq, at in [
    ("x", q.d("x"), lambda e: mie.efficiencies(x0 + e, m0)),
    ("m_real", q.d("m_real"), lambda e: mie.efficiencies(x0, m0 + e)),
    ("m_imag", q.d("m_imag"), lambda e: mie.efficiencies(x0, m0 + 1j * e)),
]:
    fd = (at(h).q_ext - at(-h).q_ext) / (2 * h)
    print(f"dQ_ext/d{name}: closed form {dq.q_ext:+.10f}, finite difference {fd:+.10f}")
    assert abs(dq.q_ext - fd) < 1e-7 * max(1, abs(fd))
```
````

### Task 7: Benchmarks

Criterion benchmarks of the kernel across x (values, the full Jacobian, amplitudes at 100 angles), and the measured cost in the theory chapter. The spec's target for the Jacobian (≤ 2× the values) is checked by reading the output; phase 1b adds the instruction-count gate and the cross-code comparison.

**Files:**
- Create: `scatrs/benches/mie.rs`
- Modify: `scatrs/Cargo.toml`, `Cargo.lock` (by cargo), `docs/theory/mie.md`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: Task 4's API.
- Produces: `cargo bench -p scatrs --bench mie` (groups `mie/efficiencies/{x}`, `mie/efficiencies+jacobian/{x}`, `mie/amplitudes-100/{x}`).

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the benchmark**

Create `scatrs/benches/mie.rs`:

````rust
//! Wall-clock benchmarks of the Mie kernel (criterion): values, values with the full
//! (x, Re m, Im m) Jacobian, and amplitudes at 100 angles, across size parameters.
//!
//! ```text
//! cargo bench -p scatrs --bench mie
//! ```

use std::hint::black_box;

use criterion::{BenchmarkId, Criterion, criterion_group, criterion_main};
use num_complex::Complex;
use scatrs::mie::{AmplitudeBuffers, Mie, MieOptions};

const SIZES: [f64; 6] = [0.1, 1.0, 10.0, 100.0, 1000.0, 10000.0];

fn mie(c: &mut Criterion) {
    let m = Complex::new(1.5, 0.01);
    let opts = MieOptions::new();
    let mut group = c.benchmark_group("mie");
    for x in SIZES {
        let mut ws = Mie::<f64>::new();
        group.bench_with_input(BenchmarkId::new("efficiencies", x), &x, |b, &x| {
            b.iter(|| ws.compute(black_box(x), m, &opts).unwrap().efficiencies())
        });
        group.bench_with_input(BenchmarkId::new("efficiencies+jacobian", x), &x, |b, &x| {
            b.iter(|| {
                ws.compute_with_derivatives(black_box(x), m, &opts)
                    .unwrap()
                    .efficiencies_jacobian()
            })
        });
        let mu: Vec<f64> = (0..100)
            .map(|k| (std::f64::consts::PI * f64::from(k) / 99.0).cos())
            .collect();
        let (mut s1, mut s2) = (
            vec![Complex::new(0.0, 0.0); 100],
            vec![Complex::new(0.0, 0.0); 100],
        );
        group.bench_with_input(BenchmarkId::new("amplitudes-100", x), &x, |b, &x| {
            b.iter(|| {
                let sol = ws.compute(black_box(x), m, &opts).unwrap();
                sol.amplitudes(
                    &mu,
                    AmplitudeBuffers {
                        s1: &mut s1,
                        s2: &mut s2,
                    },
                )
                .unwrap();
            })
        });
    }
    group.finish();
}

criterion_group!(benches, mie);
criterion_main!(benches);
````

Declare it, and document the cost:

```bash
git apply <<'PATCH'
diff --git a/docs/theory/mie.md b/docs/theory/mie.md
index 74b18c4..ab44130 100644
--- a/docs/theory/mie.md
+++ b/docs/theory/mie.md
@@ -282,3 +282,14 @@ $\lvert P_n'\rvert \le n(n+1)/2$ and $\lvert P_n''\rvert \le (n-1)n^2(n+1)/4$, a
 $\tau_n = \mu\pi_n - (1 - \mu^2)P_n''$. The bound is added to each value's error; the
 generator's tests check that it dominates the next terms and that a longer sum lies inside
 the certified ball.
+
+## Cost
+
+<!-- synced with scatrs/benches/mie.rs -->
+
+$O(N + \lvert m\rvert x)$ per sphere: the $D_n(mx)$ recurrence starts at about
+$1.5\lvert m\rvert x$. Measured with `cargo bench -p scatrs --bench mie` on an Apple M4 Pro
+($m = 1.5 + 0.01i$): 0.54 µs at $x = 0.1$, 1.1 µs at $x = 10$, 36 µs at $x = 1000$ and 345 µs
+at $x = 10^4$ for the efficiencies (about 34 ns per term); with the full
+$(x, \mathrm{Re}\,m, \mathrm{Im}\,m)$ Jacobian 1.3 to 1.8 times that; amplitudes at 100 angles
+add about 4.5 ns per angle and term.

 proptest = "1"
 serde = { version = "1", features = ["derive"] }
 # float_roundtrip makes f64 parsing correctly rounded; without it, reference values can be 1 ulp off.
 serde_json = { version = "1", features = ["float_roundtrip"] }
+
+[[bench]]
+name = "mie"
+harness = false

-The design specification fixes these conventions for the scattering kernels. No phase 0 code
-uses them yet.
+These conventions apply to the Mie functions now, and to the layered-sphere and T-matrix
+functions to come.
 
 - **Refractive index.** $m = n + ik$, where $\operatorname{Im} m \ge 0$ means absorbing (the
-  Bohren–Huffman and Mishchenko convention). Negative $\operatorname{Im} m$ is rejected with
-  `InvalidInput`; it is never silently flipped.
+  Bohren–Huffman and Mishchenko convention, time dependence $e^{-i\omega t}$). Negative
+  $\operatorname{Im} m$ is rejected with `InvalidInput`; it is never silently flipped. Codes
+  using $e^{+i\omega t}$, such as Wiscombe's MIEV0, report the complex conjugates of the
+  coefficients and amplitudes.
+- **Size parameter.** $x = 2\pi r/\lambda$, with $\lambda$ the wavelength in the medium.
+  Derivatives are with respect to $x$; multiply by $2\pi/\lambda$ for the radius.
 - **Lengths.** Radii and wavelength may be in any unit, provided both use the same one. Cross
-  sections are returned in that unit squared.
-- **Phase-matrix normalization.** $\tfrac12 \int_{-1}^{1} F_{11}(\mu)\,d\mu = 1$.
-- **Greek coefficients.** $F_{11}(\Theta) = \sum_l \alpha_{1,l} P_l(\cos\Theta)$, with
-  $\alpha_{1,0} = 1$ and $\alpha_{1,1} = 3g$. The other elements follow Hovenier, van der Mee
-  & Domke (2004):
-  - $\alpha_2 \pm \alpha_3$ expand against $d^l_{2,\pm2}$;
-  - $\alpha_4$ expands against $P_l$;
-  - $\beta_1$ and $\beta_2$ expand against $d^l_{0,2}$.
-- **Random orientation.** The phase matrix has six independent elements: $F_{11}$, $F_{12}$,
-  $F_{22}$, $F_{33}$, $F_{34}$ and $F_{44}$. This assumes the ensemble is macroscopically
-  isotropic and mirror-symmetric.
-- **Fixed orientation.** Euler angles $(\alpha, \beta, \gamma)$ use the z-y-z convention.
-  Incidence and scattering directions $(\theta, \varphi)$ are in the laboratory frame. Both
-  follow Mishchenko.
-- **Shapes.**
-  - The spheroid aspect ratio $\varepsilon$ is the equatorial semi-axis divided by the polar
-    one: $\varepsilon > 1$ is oblate.
-  - The cylinder aspect ratio is the diameter divided by the length.
-  - A Chebyshev particle is $r(\theta) = r_0(1 + \varepsilon\cos n\theta)$, with integer
-    $n \ge 2$ and $\lvert\varepsilon\rvert < 1$.
-- **Size of non-spherical particles.** The radius of the sphere with the same volume, or the
-  same surface area, as the user selects.
+  sections are returned in that unit squared (the efficiencies are cross sections divided by
+  $\pi r^2$).
+- **Coefficients.** $a_n$, $b_n$ for $n = 1, \ldots, N$ (Bohren & Huffman 1983, Eq. 4.53),
+  stored at index $n - 1$.
+- **Amplitudes.** $S_1(\mu)$ and $S_2(\mu)$ at $\mu = \cos\Theta$ (Bohren & Huffman 1983,
+  Eq. 4.74).
+- **Phase-matrix normalization.** $\tfrac12 \int_{-1}^{1} F_{11}(\mu)\,d\mu = 1$, with
+  $F_{11} = (\lvert S_1\rvert^2 + \lvert S_2\rvert^2)/(x^2 Q_\text{sca}/2)$.
+- **Derivatives with respect to m.** The coefficients and amplitudes are holomorphic in $m$:
+  their derivative $d/dm$ equals $\partial/\partial(\operatorname{Re} m)$, and
+  $\partial/\partial(\operatorname{Im} m) = i\,d/dm$. Real outputs give
+  $\partial/\partial(\operatorname{Re} m)$ and $\partial/\partial(\operatorname{Im} m)$.

 - **Vectorisation.** A loop over many `Dual`s is not vectorised across elements.
 
+## Mie scattering
+
+- **Truncation and narrow resonances.** The default truncation, `ceil(x + 6 x**(1/3) + 3)`
+  terms, can miss an exponentially narrow whispering-gallery resonance of a high-index,
+  weakly absorbing sphere above that order. The size parameter must fall in an exponentially
+  small window to meet one. Pass a larger `n_max` to include higher orders. See
+  {doc}`theory/mie`.
+- **Validated domain.** $10^{-6} \le x \le 2\cdot10^4$, $0 < \operatorname{Re} m \le 10$,
+  $0 \le \operatorname{Im} m \le 10$. Outside it, calls raise `OutsideValidatedDomain`
+  unless `allow_unvalidated` is set. Accepted inputs extend to $x \in [10^{-30}, 10^6]$ and
+  $\lvert m\rvert \in [10^{-6}, 10^3]$, where the outputs are finite but their accuracy is not
+  validated. The number of terms is at most `MAX_MIE_ORDER` = $2^{20}$.
+- **Accuracy near resonances.** The declared accuracy is relative to the conditioning of each
+  output. Near the sharp resonances of weakly absorbing and lossless spheres the outputs are
+  very sensitive to $x$ and $m$, and their absolute errors grow accordingly. See
+  {doc}`validation/accuracy`.
+- **Absorption derivatives.** $\partial Q_\text{abs}/\partial x$ and
+  $\partial Q_\text{abs}/\partial(\operatorname{Re} m)$ of weakly absorbing spheres are
+  accurate relative to $Q_\text{ext}$, not to $Q_\text{abs}$ itself: near a resonance with
+  $k = 10^{-8}$ their relative error reached $4\cdot10^{-9}$.
+- **Cost.** $O(x + \lvert m\rvert x)$ per sphere; the Python functions loop over the spheres
+  on one thread. Parallel batches arrive in phase 1b.
+- **Series derivatives in the reference suite** have no tail bound yet, so they are labelled
+  high-precision.
+
 ## Python bindings
 
 - **Shared inputs.** Calls release the GIL and read input arrays in place. Do not modify or
   resize an input array from another thread during a call.
+- **Output size.** One Mie call returns at most `MAX_MIE_VALUES` = $2^{27}$ complex values,
+  counting derivatives (2 GiB).
 - **Per-call overhead.** Measured during code review on an Apple M4 Pro (not committed), the
   extension call costs about 180 ns and the Python wrapper about 0.84 µs. Prefer one call on a
   large array to many small calls.

+- W. J. Wiscombe, *Mie Scattering Calculations: Advances in Technique and Fast,

+- R. L. Smith, "Algorithm 116: Complex division", *Communications of the ACM* 5 (1962), 435.
+  The complex reciprocal.
+- I. Gargantini and P. Henrici, "Circular arithmetic and the determination of polynomial

+  generator.
